| 2026-11-12 | **Apple TV Back never closes the app except from the tab bar** (owner: "pressing back in places closes the app too"). Playing anything moved to Now Playing with focus still on the tab bar, so the next Back went to the Home Screen; Now Playing now takes focus when opened by playback and Back returns to the tab you came from, covers hand focus back to their opening control, a Browse-tile search clears back to Browse, and the sign-in screen no longer swallows Back with a no-op. Rule and helper (`tvEntersFocus`) in §2 Apple TV; `TVBackTests` checks every surface with the remote. |
# Sounding design bible

The single source of truth for how Sounding looks, moves, reads and behaves on iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple TV, Apple Watch and the website. **Every agent and every future change reads this first.** Where an older spec in `Design/specs/` disagrees, this file wins. Where this file and an explicit user instruction disagree, the user wins: update this file in the same change.

Last updated 2026-11-12. Owner: Alex Forbes-Reed.

---

## 1. Principles
1. **Apple-native first.** Sounding should feel like an app Apple shipped: stock controls, system materials (Liquid Glass on iOS 26/27, macOS 26), system type, platform conventions. The bar the user set: *"not apple normal at all"* is a failure, and so is *"cheap"*.
2. **One signature, used with discipline.** The brand is a **copper 3.5 mm aux jack**. In the UI it appears as the procedural **copper line art** (§4) and as the copper accent (§3). Nothing else decorates.
3. **Content is the hero.** Artwork, waveforms, comments and tracklists. Chrome recedes.
4. **Calm over clever.** One idea per screen, few elements, generous space. If it looks busy, remove things.
5. **Smooth or it doesn't ship.** Lag, flicker and jank are bugs (§6). The user's words: *"slick, smooth, not glitchy"*.
6. **Real over fake.** Real data, real screenshots and real music. Sample fixtures never reach a release build or marketing.
7. **Low cost.** Low CPU, low memory, low network. Never get the user rate-limited (§9).

---

## 2. Platform idioms

### All platforms
- Use system controls: `Toggle`, `Picker`, `Slider`, `Menu`, `Button` with system styles, `LabeledContent`, `Gauge`, `ProgressView`.
- Settings and chrome are grouped `Form`/`List` sections, with at most **one short footer** per section and no implementation prose.
- Empty and error states use `ContentUnavailableView`, **centred** in the available content area: horizontally and vertically, never jammed under a header. Loading on list and grid pages (Recently Added, Tracks, Likes, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Sets, Followers, collection and artist pages) is the shared `LoadingPlaceholder` (`Shared/UI/Components/LoadingPlaceholder.swift`): placeholder tiles or rows shaped like the real layout (same tile size and spacing, a soft rounded artwork shape and two text bars; track rows with `SCTrackListRow` metrics), `.redacted(reason: .placeholder)` with a gentle shimmer that stops under Reduce Motion and Low Power Mode, and the library-sync line above it where the page shows one. On Apple TV it stays focusable. Elsewhere loading is a centred `ProgressView`, never a lone spinner in a corner.
- No custom rounded "card" panels with icon tiles. Group with whitespace, sections and type.
- **The app tint is brand orange everywhere, never system blue** (owner, 2026-11-09). Every scene root and the sheets presented from it carry `.tint(Color.scAccent)` (iPhone and iPad `WindowGroup`, Mac scenes, Apple TV, Watch, both Share extensions), and every target's `AccentColor` asset is `scAccent` with `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_GLOBAL_ACCENT_COLOR_NAME = AccentColor` (iOS, iPadOS, macOS, tvOS, watchOS, the iOS, Mac and Watch widgets, both Share extensions; the extensions share `Branding/ExtensionAccent.xcassets`). Code never writes `Color.accentColor`, `.blue` or a hand-made system blue for chrome: use `Color.scAccent` or the `.tint` shape style. On the Mac `Color.accentColor` follows the user's System Settings accent, so it is banned there outright. Kept as they are: semantic data colours (waveforms, cue, key and deck colours) and the Settings icon tile palette (§3). A collection page's artwork accent (§3) still overrides the tint on its own hero buttons and playing mark.
- **Sidebars:** the **selected sidebar item** on iPad and Mac is `Color.scAccent`. On iPad it is one `.tint(Color.scAccent)` on the `TabView`. On the Mac the unselected sidebar icons are orange too, like Music's accent-coloured sidebar icons; on iPad nothing else in a sidebar is orange.
- **Brand orange is the iPhone and iPad page tint** (owner, 2026-11-09: "the blue pills like Overview, Tracks, etc. should be orange"; supersedes the system-blue page tint). Every tab's content carries `.tint(PadChrome.pageTint)` (`Color.scAccent`) on iPad, and the iPhone `TabView` carries the same tint, so selected glass pills, links, table headers and the selected tab are orange. Collection pages still put their artwork accent on their own hero buttons and playing mark (§3 Collection accent). White on `scAccent` is only 2.9:1, so a selected orange pill we draw (search scopes, any section pill) labels itself in `Color.scAccentOn` (7.4:1), as the Mac sidebar does. The Mac content and sheets presented from the root (Settings, sign-in) are orange too (superseded 2026-11-09: no system blue anywhere); Mac content selection is the same `scAccent` fill with a `scAccentOn` label as the sidebar.
- No orange-tinted standard controls. Copper is for a few deliberate accents only (§3). The one exception is a collection page's artwork accent (§3), which may be any hue the artwork has.
- A second exception: the **selected sidebar item** on iPad and Mac is `Color.scAccent`. On iPad it is one `.tint(Color.scAccent)` on the `TabView`. Sidebar **glyphs** are brand orange too, regular weight, outline, like Music's tinted sidebar (owner, 2026-11-09: "the sidebar is quite noisy"); labels stay primary. That includes the Search item: its `.search`-role tab takes the same `SidebarTabIcon` label, because the system's default search glyph ignores the tint (owner, 2026-11-09: "search icon white"). Nothing else in a sidebar is orange.
- **Brand orange is the iPhone and iPad page tint** (owner, 2026-11-09: "the blue pills like Overview, Tracks, etc. should be orange"; supersedes the system-blue page tint). Every tab's content carries `.tint(PadChrome.pageTint)` (`Color.scAccent`) on iPad, and the iPhone `TabView` carries the same tint, so selected glass pills, links, table headers and the selected tab are orange. Collection pages still put their artwork accent on their own hero buttons and playing mark (§3 Collection accent). White on `scAccent` is only 2.9:1, so a selected orange pill we draw (search scopes, any section pill) labels itself in `Color.scAccentOn` (7.4:1), as the Mac sidebar does. The Mac keeps the system accent in content. Sheets presented from the root (Settings, sign-in) stay system blue.
- Hit targets are at least 44 pt (iOS) and focusable everything (tvOS).
- **Play On holds still and remembers** (owner, 2026-11-09: "play on super jumpy, apple tv keeps popping in and out"; "remember plays on … and restore after reboot/restart/close"). Every Output list (iPhone and iPad Connect sheet and mini player Play On, Mac Play On popover and toolbar menu, Play on *TV* track menu items, the Watch's Output) comes from one `OutputRouter.entries`, built by `OutputList` and `OutputPresence` (`Core/Playback/OutputPresence.swift`). One identity per Output: the link's `OutputDevice.id`; Bonjour, the Companion connection and the active link merge on it, and only a same-named, same-class pair that isn't both live collapses to one row. A device seen this session stays listed normally for 10 s after it drops, then dimmed and disabled as **Not Responding** until 45 s, then leaves; the current Output never leaves (it says **Connecting…** until first seen). Updates are coalesced (0.4 s) and only published when the list changes; rows keep their ids and don't animate in or out. Order: the current Output, then This *Device*, then by name. The chosen Output is a device-local preference (`red.forbes.sounding.output.v1`, This *Device* included when picked). On launch a remembered Apple TV is selected at once (so Remote and the restored queue follow it) and confirmed when it appears; if it hasn't within 25 s, Output quietly returns to This *Device* without an error, keeping the preference for next launch. Restoring never starts playback. AirPlay and Bluetooth routes stay with the system (no API selects them for us), and Sonos is not restored, since resuming it means streaming.
- **Link a nearby Apple TV from Play On** (owner, 2026-11-09: "let me connect to a tv via the play on dialog/modal/popover"). The iPhone and iPad Connect sheet and the Mac Play On popover have a **Nearby** section under the linked Outputs: one system row per Apple TV that's advertising but not linked to this device (merged by kind and name like every Nearby list, sorted by name, hidden while signed out), with its `DeviceClass` symbol, its name, its nearby status and a trailing **Link** in the tint. Tapping runs the same numeric-comparison or one-tap link as Add Apple TV, inline: the row says **Connecting…** with a spinner; a code to compare shows as a sheet on iPhone and iPad (**It Matches** / **It Doesn't Match**) and inline under the row in the Mac popover; then the credentials go across (`CompanionLinker`) and the TV becomes the Output at once, so the list shows it as current. Failures stay in the row as **Couldn't Connect** with **Try Again** (the reason in the accessibility hint and, on the Mac, the tooltip), never an alert. One link at a time; other rows are disabled meanwhile. With no unlinked TVs nearby the section isn't shown; if nothing is linked either, one quiet **Add Apple TV…** row opens the existing flow. It reuses the app's running Link offer browser (`startIfNeeded`), never a fresh browse per open. Logic is shared: `PlayOnNearbyLinking` (`Shared/UI/Components/PlayOnNearbyLinking.swift`). A TV on another SoundCloud account lists as **Signed in to another account**, and **Link** first asks "<TV> is signed in to another SoundCloud account" / "Link it to control it from this iPhone. You can switch it to your account afterwards." with **Link** and **Cancel**.
- **TV accounts** (owner, 2026-11-12; `Design/specs/tv-signin-flows.md`). One pure function, `TVAccountRelation.resolve` in shared `TVAccountMatch.swift`, decides what iPhone, iPad and Mac show for a TV: same account, signed out, needs sign-in, phone signed out, or another account. A linked TV on another account is allowed and always labelled: its Play On and Devices rows say **Signed in as Sam**, and the first time you play on or control it (once per Link and pair of accounts) a `confirmationDialog` asks "Living Room is signed in as Sam" with **Play on Living Room** (or **Control Living Room**), **Switch Living Room to Alex…** and **Cancel**. Switching is confirmed on the phone ("Switch Living Room to Alex?") and on the TV, and the pair is sent only after the TV accepts; progress and outcomes stay inline, never an alert. It's hidden for TVs on older builds. Failures are never silent: a move the TV's account can't play says "Couldn't Play on Living Room" / "It's signed in as Sam, who can't play this."; a refused transport command puts "Living Room couldn't do that." in the mini player subtitle or Mac capsule for 4 s; library, likes and cue sync refusals stay quiet. A TV on another account stays out of the other-devices bar unless it's the Output, and never gets the nearby card or the outside notification. The Now Playing bar never names the TV's account.
- **A playing Apple TV is controlled, never replaced** (owner, 2026-11-11: "the Apple TV plays music but can't be controlled from the iPhone"). Choosing a TV in Play On while this device isn't playing and the TV is makes the TV the Output without sending this device's paused queue, so it keeps playing and this device becomes its remote; a queue that is playing here, or paused here while the TV is idle, still moves to the TV as before. When the active TV starts playing while this device is idle (it hasn't played, and This *Device* hasn't been chosen, for 10 minutes), the Output follows the TV on its own, without changing the remembered preference, so the mini player shows the TV's track and every control drives it. Nothing starts or stops playing on either side. Logic: `OutputHandover` and `OutputFollow` (`Core/Playback/OutputPresence.swift`).
- **The remote says when it isn't connected** (owner, 2026-11-11: device connection "not clear or stable"). While the Output is a TV whose link hasn't delivered live state, iPhone and iPad say **Connecting to Living Room…** (mini player subtitle; the Now Playing route badge with a small spinner) instead of "Playing on Living Room", and the Mac capsule says the same, or **Living Room isn't reachable** once reconnecting has failed. Transport and Up Next taps made meanwhile are held for 10 s and sent once it connects (play or pause, seek, shuffle, repeat and sleep keep only the latest); older taps are dropped, never replayed later. Controllers send a heartbeat every 5 s and drop a TV that has sent nothing for 15 s; the TV drops a controller that is silent for 30 s, and both drop a dead network peer in about 15 s. A link that drops after it had settled reconnects at once. The TV restarts its listener when it stops and whenever the app returns to the foreground. Logic: `CompanionHeartbeat`, `CompanionCommandOutbox`, `CompanionConnectionPhase` (`Shared/Networking/CompanionFailure.swift`).
- **Your other devices show in the bar, like iOS** (owner, 2026-11-11: "it should be a little bit like how iOS works where you kind of see what other devices are doing, even if you're not using them actively"). One presence model (`DevicePresence` in `Core/Playback/DevicePresence.swift`, `DevicePresenceModel` in `Shared/ViewModels/`, shared menu and labels in `Shared/UI/Components/DevicePresenceViews.swift`) says what each of your devices on this account is playing: name and `DeviceClass` symbol, track, artwork, playing or paused, whether it is this device's Output, and how fresh that is.
  - **Sources, best first:** the live Companion state of the linked TV this device is connected to; then the Continue record every device already publishes (§9), over Link while linked, else through iCloud. No new requests, records or polling: one wake at the next freshness edge.
  - **Honest freshness:** a playing record is live for 90 s after it was written (a playing device writes every 30 s); after that it reads "Living Room was playing *Title* · a few minutes ago", never "is playing". Paused reads "Living Room paused *Title*", with the age once it is 2 minutes old. Both leave after 30 minutes. A connected TV that is idle removes any older claim at once, and the paused copy of the track that moved to this device isn't shown. Same-named devices of one class are one entry.
  - **Which one:** the Output first, then live devices by most recently started, then paused, then stale. More than one shows "+1" (or more) and lists them all in the menu.
  - **Playing here, others playing:** iPhone puts the other device's symbol between the title and the transport; iPad's wide bar says "Living Room is playing *Title*" before •••; the Mac capsule shows the symbol at its trailing end. Each is a native `Menu`: the device's sentence as the section header with its actions, or **Other Devices** with a submenu per device. The bar's context menu ends with the same section, and VoiceOver reads it after the track.
  - **Nothing playing here:** Continue From still wins; otherwise the bar (the iPhone accessory stays visible for it) shows the other device's artwork, title and "Playing on Living Room" (or the paused or stale caption), and tapping it opens the same menu. Continue From's caption follows the same rule: "From Living Room · 2 hr ago" once its record isn't live.
  - **Actions:** **Control Living Room** makes a linked, reachable TV the Output without sending anything and pauses this device, so the TV keeps playing and this device becomes its remote. **Play on Living Room** sends this device's queue there (only while it holds a SoundCloud track and plays here). **Play Here** continues that device's track here, pausing a connected TV first; other devices keep playing. A failure is one alert, "Couldn't Switch Device".
  - **Output elsewhere:** the bar keeps "Playing on Living Room" and "Connecting to Living Room…" from the connection rules, and the Output is left out of the other-devices line.
  - **Apple TV:** Home gets one hero page for the most relevant other device ("Playing on Alex's iPhone", the title, the artist, **Play Here**) unless Continue From already shows it; the empty Now Playing names it with **Play Here**; while the TV plays, a quiet, unfocusable caption at the top left says "Alex's iPhone is playing *Title*".
  - **Watch:** the iPhone sends up to four entries (never its Output) in the watch context; Now Playing has one caption line under the title and the empty state names the device. Read only.
  - **Privacy:** only your own devices on this SoundCloud account (records are account-tagged); nothing leaves iCloud and Link.
- **Output volume is one model** (owner, 2026-11-09: "iphone now playing volume ignored? but play on not ignored"; "let me use phone volume button?"). Every volume control (Now Playing, Play On, the Mac toolbar and Play On popover, iPad ⌘↑ and ⌘↓, the Watch, the volume buttons) goes through one route (`OutputVolumeRoute` in `Core/Playback/OutputVolume.swift`): **Sonos** when a Sonos room plays, else the **linked Apple TV or Mac** when it is the Output (Sounding's own app volume there), else **this device's system volume** (`MPVolumeView` on iPhone and iPad, the output device on the Mac). The TV and Sonos share `RemoteVolumeChannel`: the slider moves at once, sends are throttled to about 8 a second with the last value always sent, and reports from the device are ignored while dragging and for 1.5 s after, so the slider never jumps back. Sonos volume is read back every 2 s, so changes from the Sonos app or the speaker show up. While a TV or Sonos is the Output, iPhone and iPad Now Playing and Play On show its slider, named for it ("Living Room Volume").
  - **Volume buttons** (iPhone and iPad) step a TV or Sonos Output by 1/16 per press, like Spotify Connect and the Sonos app, while Sounding is in the foreground. A small glass capsule under the status bar names the Output and shows its level for 1.6 s instead of the system HUD. The phone's own volume is put back after every press, so it never changes while the Output is elsewhere (at 0 or 100 % it is nudged one step in so both buttons register, and restored when the Output comes back or the app leaves the foreground). If another app is playing audio on the phone, the buttons stay with the phone. In the background, on the lock screen and in Control Center the buttons set the phone's own volume as usual: iOS has no public remote-volume API, and the press is only visible to the app while it is active.
- **New cross-device or proactive offers must surface where the user is already looking (mini player, hero), never as a plain list row** (owner, 2026-11-09, on Continue from: "far too non-descript, buried on the page, stop doing this"). Use the mini player first, a Home hero second, with the artwork, a clear caption and real buttons; arrive with a subtle fade and rise that honours Reduce Motion; never play or interrupt on their own.
- **First launch is SoundCloud-first; SoundCloud stays optional.** The signed-out welcome keeps its line-art hero and leads with **Sign In with SoundCloud** (prominent), with a smaller **Use Without SoundCloud** link under it. After either, one follow-up page shows once per install, built like Apple's setup and What's New pages from the shared `OnboardingFeaturePage` (`FeatureRow`), with no line art: a small "✓ Signed in as <name>" with the SoundCloud avatar (left out after Use Without SoundCloud), a large brand-orange hierarchical `folder.fill.badge.plus`, **Add Your Own Music**, three feature rows (One Library, Auto Mix and Decks, On Your Apple TV), then **Choose Folder…** (the normal Music Folders importer, prominent) and **Not Now** at the bottom. It must read as a new page: it pushes in and fully replaces the welcome (opaque, no welcome controls left visible or focusable), never crossfades in place or sits over it as a sheet. Once answered it never returns (`MusicStep.answeredKey`); choosing any folder in the importer, even one already in the Library, answers it; after a sign-out only the welcome comes back. Choosing a folder, or going on without SoundCloud, lands in the Library; Not Now after signing in lands on Home. Everything stays in Settings › Music Folders. iPhone pushes the page in full screen; iPad pushes it in too, with the content in a centred form-sized column (about 540 by 760); the Mac shows both in one sheet over the main window, the page pushing in after the art-banner welcome; Apple TV in §9. Debug: `SOUNDING_TV_WELCOME=1` forces the welcome, `SOUNDING_TV_MUSIC_STEP=1` the step, `SOUNDING_TV_FORCE_SESSION_HEALTH=signedOut` the signed-out state; fixture and UI-test launches skip both unless forced.
- **SoundCloud sign-in is a dialog, never full screen.** iPad and Mac show it as a centred form sheet (about 520 by 720) over a dimmed window, iPhone as a normal sheet, from every entry point including the welcome. Swipe or click-outside doesn’t dismiss it; Cancel does. It’s dark to match SoundCloud’s page and shows a centred `ProgressView` until the form has drawn. Every device loads SoundCloud’s mobile sign-in page with an iPhone Safari user agent, links that leave sign-in open in the browser, and the email and password fields are marked for Password AutoFill (the Passwords key on the keyboard reaches 1Password). The web flow is one shared core, `SoundCloudWebSignIn`, used by iOS, the Mac and silent renewal. No native email or password fields: we never type the user’s email ourselves.
- **Signed-out SoundCloud sections share one prompt.** `SoundCloudSectionGate` and `SoundCloudSignInPrompt` (`Shared/UI/Components/SoundCloudSignInGate.swift`) draw "Sign In to See Your Likes" and the rest on iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV; only the sign-in action differs per platform (`soundCloudSignIn(isPresented:)`: the sign-in sheet on iOS and the Mac, the QR sign-in screen on the TV).
- **Track collections sort and filter the same way everywhere** (`TrackCollectionQuery` / `TrackCollection` in `TrackSort.swift`). Sort: Title, Artist, Duration, Date Added (Date Liked on Likes), Plays, BPM, Key, Energy, Genre, Source; keys with no data in the list are hidden, unanalysed values sort last in both directions, ties keep the list's own order, and the choice is remembered per screen. Filters: Show (Downloaded, Files, SoundCloud, Previews; these combine with "or"), then Sets, Analysed, In Key with Now Playing, Not Played and a Tempo range (these combine with "and"). Sort and filter act on what's loaded; a sort other than the list's natural order loads the rest with a progress line and Stop, and a filtered list offers Load All rather than fetching on its own. No matches is a centred `ContentUnavailableView` with Clear Filters. iPhone and iPad: a "Sort & Filter" toolbar menu (Sort By on artist pages). There is one track row style (owner decision 2026-11-09; Roomy and Dense are gone): 52 pt rows with 44 pt artwork, the Apple Music song-list feel, BPM and Key on the second line. In regular width (iPad) the menu starts with a **Show Columns** toggle, remembered per list (Tracks, Likes, Sets, playlists and albums, artist pages; `trackList.showsColumns.<list>`, migrated once from the old density: Dense becomes on, Roomy off). On, each row shows aligned columns under a row of sortable column headers: on iPad the columns are the set and order chosen in Settings › Library › Columns (`TrackColumnLayout`, the same choice as the Mac table; owner, 2026-11-09), elsewhere Title, Artist, Album, Time, BPM and Key that drive the list's `TrackCollectionQuery`; the active header is the brand orange with its direction chevron (owner, 2026-11-09: he asked for orange table headers; an exception to the no-orange-controls rule, §3 Copper accent). On iPhone, in compact width and at accessibility sizes the toggle is hidden and rows stay as they are. The toggle changes only the rows: an album or playlist page keeps the same hero, artwork backdrop, release footer and shelves either way, and the switch animates. Rows keep the same actions, swipe actions and context menus in both. Mac: the library `Table` with the same filters in its filter menu. Apple TV: one focusable Sort & Filter menu in the list header beside Play and Shuffle (the shared `TrackCollectionMenuItems`, label showing the current sort), no table; View As Roomy is the artwork grid and Dense is one-line rows (44 pt artwork, "Title · Artist", the first two `TrackColumnLayout` extras, `SCTrackListRow`), on Likes, Tracks, Sets, Setlists and Downloads; Roomy is the TV default. Column titles, widths, sort mapping, value formatting (BPM, key, time, plays, source) and the Show Columns preference are shared code (`Shared/ViewModels/TrackTableColumns.swift`).
- **The sidebar always shows where you are.** A page that has its own sidebar item (Likes, Profile, Recently Added, Following, a Library page) is never pushed inside another item's stack, and a playlist from your library opens inside All Playlists. Opening it from Home, Search, a context menu, a link or a deep link selects its item instead, through one routing point per platform: iPad `SidebarNavigator` (`SidebarLink`, `PlaylistLink`, the `ArtistRoutingStack` own-profile redirect), Mac `MacRootSelection` and `MacSidebarRedirect`, Apple TV `NavigationState.selectedTab` (`showProfile()`, `TVUserLink`). Pages with no item of their own (someone else's playlist or artist, an album) push inside the current item and leave its highlight alone. On Mac, jumps between items join Back and Forward, which restore the item and its highlight. Never `List(selection:)`.
- **Album names show where Music shows them** (owner: "does SoundCloud not store album name?"). The album is the track's `publisher_metadata.album_title`, then its release title, then an album page we've already loaded that holds the track (`Track.albumName`, `Track.knownAlbum` from `PlaybackQueueSourceDirectory`); never a new request. Now Playing's second line is "Artist — Album" on iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV (Apple TV leaves the album out when the "From" line already names it). Behind the Track has an **Album** row that opens the album when we know its page, plain text otherwise; it replaces the Release row when they match. Track menus on iPhone and iPad have **Go to Album** (album title as the subtitle) when the page is known, otherwise the album name greyed; the Mac's Go to Album uses a known page before asking SoundCloud. iPad rows at regular width read "Artist · Album" outside album pages; tables keep the Album column.
- **Identify Song** (owner, 2026-11-09; like Shazam). One shared flow: `SongIdentifier` listens on the microphone through ShazamKit's `SHManagedSession` (only while the sheet is open; the microphone is asked for then), `IdentifySongSheet` shows it on iPhone, iPad and Mac.
  - **Listening:** centred `SoundingSoundRings` swelling around a copper `waveform`, "Listening…" and "Keep the music playing nearby."; still under Reduce Motion or when the app is inactive. No match and failures are centred `ContentUnavailableView`s with **Try Again** (and **Open Settings** when the microphone is off).
  - **Result** (a grouped `Form`): the artwork (220 pt), title, artist and when ("Yesterday", or "In <mix> at 23:10"), then **Find on SoundCloud** (`.glassProminent`) and **Add to Library** (`.glass`, likes the best SoundCloud match; "In Library" once liked; needs a SoundCloud sign-in), then **Open in Apple Music** (only when Shazam gives a link) and **Share**. Find on SoundCloud makes one `search/tracks` request on tap (never on its own), ranks by title and artist, and shows the top five with play, a heart and Add to Playlist…, plus **See All Results** (the normal Search). A rate limit says "SoundCloud is limiting requests".
  - **Recently Identified:** the last 50, newest first, swipe or Remove to delete, Clear; stored as an Apple ID preference (`red.forbes.sounding.synced.identifiedSongs.v1`), so it follows the Apple ID and survives SoundCloud sign-out. Mix identifications land there too.
  - **Surfaces, never buried:** a `shazam.logo` toolbar button on Search (iPhone, iPad, Mac); Library › **Identify Song** ⇧⌘I and **Recently Identified** on the Mac; the **Identify Song** App Shortcut and Control Center control (they open the app on the sheet, closing Now Playing first); on the Watch, **Identify Song** is the first row under Now Playing on the root list, listening on the Watch's own microphone, with Recently Identified under it once there is one.
  - **In a mix:** the Tracklist pane's button is **Identify This Track** (it fingerprints the audio at the playhead, not the microphone). A match shows in the pane as before, with **Details** (iPhone, iPad) or **Details…** (Mac) opening the same result.
- **Pages push as values on the tab's own stack.** On iPhone and iPad every `ArtistRoutingStack` owns one `NavigationPath`: artists (`ArtistRouter.open`), albums and playlists (`PlaylistLink`, `LibraryCollectionRoute`, `PlaylistPageRoute`), Setlists (`LibraryPageRoute`) and the account menu's social pages (`ArtistRouter.open(social:)`) are appended to it and their destinations, artists' `User` included, are registered once at the stack root (`appNavigationDestinations()`). A library playlist opened from another item selects All Playlists and is pushed onto its path. Never put a view-destination `NavigationLink` or a `navigationDestination` inside a lazy shelf, grid or list (a page opened that way pops back when the shelf unloads; this was the "tapping an album takes me back to the album" bug). Apple TV pages without a path open shelf items with a page-level `navigationDestination(item:)`. Each collection page's More by shelf uses its own zoom namespace so transition sources never repeat in one stack.
- **Stations, like Radio in Apple Music** (owner, 2026-11-09: "Add Stations to the sidebar next to Decks"). One shared model (`StationsModel`, `StationLibrary`, `StationPlayer` in `Shared/ViewModels/Stations.swift`) and shared parts (`StationViews.swift`) drive `StationsView` (iPhone and iPad), `MacStationsView` and `TVStationsView`. The page is a **Your Station** hero (a wide card: artwork, "Station" eyebrow, title, "Based on your likes and plays", a glass **Play**, the artwork mesh behind it; it plays your latest likes' and plays' track stations interleaved), then **Recently Played** (stations you started, kept on the device per account, 20 at most), **Liked Stations** (SoundCloud's liked stations from `GET api.soundcloud.com/me/likes/system-playlists`, checked at most every 6 hours, plus any the library sync already holds), **Stations for You** (track stations from recent plays and artist stations from your most liked artists) and **Artist Stations** (followed artists, newest follow first, then up to a day-old list of related artists). Stations for You and Artist Stations are built on the device from the library database and Recently Played, so they cost nothing until one is played; playing a station fetches its SoundCloud station (api-v2 first, then `api.soundcloud.com/system-playlists/{urn}`, then related tracks) through the gateway cache and stops at a 429. Tiles are square artwork with a small "Station" badge (`dot.radiowaves.left.and.right`), the title ("<Track> Station", "<Artist> Station") and "Based on <artist>". Long-press: Play, Start Station (from the top with shuffle off), Go to Artist, Go to Track (Behind the Track), Share, and Remove from Recently Played on that shelf; there is no Like Station yet. Loading is the shaped placeholder (hero bar and two shelves), empty is a centred `ContentUnavailableView`, signed out is the shared sign-in prompt. **Start Station** sits in track menus (iPhone and iPad rows, the mini player menu, the Mac track menu, Apple TV track rows) and artist menus (artist ••• on iPhone, iPad and Mac, people menus), and every station start, Home's station tiles included, lands in Recently Played. While a station plays, Now Playing's caption line says "Station · Based on <seed>" (iPhone, iPad, Mac under the artist; Apple TV in place of "From …"). Where it lives: the iPad sidebar right after Decks (`PhoneTab.stations`), the Mac sidebar after Decks, and an Apple TV tab after Decks; the iPhone keeps its four tabs and reaches Stations from Home's station shelves and the Library.

### iPhone (iOS 26.5+)
- Tabs: **Home, Library, Playlists, Search** (Search uses the system search role). Playlists is the same page as iPad's All Playlists (Likes pinned first, View As Grid or List, New Playlist +), so it is not in the Library list. There is no Social tab (owner, 2026-11-09). The **account avatar** at the top right of Home, Library and Playlists (and the Library pages) is a menu, like Music's: Profile, Activity (with the unread count), Following, Followers, then Settings, which opens as a sheet; the social pages push in the current tab's stack. A small red dot on the avatar marks unread activity. Signed out, the avatar is a plain `person.crop.circle` button that opens Settings.
- **One Library**, not a SoundCloud mirror. The Library order everywhere is **Recently Added, Tracks, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Sets, Downloads, Mixes**; each platform lists the items it has in that order. **Setlists live inside Sets** (owner, 2026-11-09: "Setlists goes under Sets now"): the Sets page starts with a **Setlists** row (with the count, only when there are saved setlists) that opens the saved setlists; there is no Setlists item of its own anywhere. The iPhone Library tab lists **Tracks** first (your own files and SoundCloud likes in one list, each row with a small source mark: `folder` for a file, `cloud` for SoundCloud), then Albums, Artists, Sets, Downloads, Mixes, Decks, Stations and History (Playlists has its own tab), with the Recently Added grid below. **Likes is not a Library item:** it is a pinned playlist, the first item of Playlists on every platform (like Music's Favourite Songs), titled "Likes" with "N tracks" from the library database and a heart (`heart.fill`) over the line art and accent gradient (`LikesPlaylist.swift`); it opens the existing Likes page (sort, Show Columns, downloads), pushed inside Playlists so Playlists stays highlighted, and anything that used to open Likes now opens Playlists › Likes. Without a SoundCloud account every row still shows: Sets and Playlists (and the Likes page) each open a centred `ContentUnavailableView` ("Sign In to See Your Likes", one line on what appears there, and **Sign In**, which starts the normal SoundCloud sign-in). Recently Added no longer hides: it is useful without an account (your own music) and shows SoundCloud items when there is one.
- **Playlists: Grid/List toggle and New Playlist** (iPhone, iPad, Mac; Apple TV stays a grid). The toolbar has **+** (New Playlist) and its own **Grid/List toggle button** (owner, 2026-11-09: never a "View As" item buried in a menu): one icon (`CollectionViewModeToggle` in `LibraryCollectionGrid.swift`) showing the mode it switches to, `list.bullet` in Grid and `square.grid.2x2` in List, with a symbol replace transition. The mode is saved per page in the synced setting `collectionViewModes` (iPhone and iPad default to List, Mac to Grid). List rows are 56 pt artwork, the title and "N tracks · owner"; Likes is first in both views. Albums (iPhone, iPad and Mac, Grid by default) and playlist folders have the same toggle.
- **Library page chrome, like Apple Music** (owner, 2026-11-09). On library and collection pages (Recently Added, Tracks, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Likes, Sets, Stations, History, folders): the title is large on iPhone (compact width) and **inline with the toolbar icons on iPad** (`libraryPageTitleDisplayMode()`); the page search sits **directly under the title**, always shown (`libraryPageSearchable`, `.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .always)`) on iPhone and iPad, and is the toolbar search field on the Mac (⌘F focuses it). Album and playlist pages keep the drawer under the title but hidden until pulled (`.automatic`) so the full-bleed artwork stays at the top. **Albums** starts with the shared Play and Shuffle glass pair (`LibraryCollectionPlayButtons` over `CollectionHeroPlayButtons`): Play plays every listed album in order, Shuffle shuffles them, both from the library database without new requests; a search narrows what they play. Every album page keeps the same pair in its hero. **New Playlist…** is a form sheet: Title (required), Description (optional), Visibility (Private by default), Cancel and Create. It is also the first item of Add to Playlist, the iPad sidebar's New Playlist row and the Mac's File › New Playlist… (⌘N). Creating writes the playlist to the library database at once and takes it out again if SoundCloud refuses; a 429 shows the usual rate notice and an alert saying SoundCloud is limiting requests. The new playlist opens; an empty playlist you own shows a centred "No Tracks Yet" with **Add Tracks**, which opens a searchable picker of your Likes.
- **One Library**, not a SoundCloud mirror. The Library order everywhere is **Recently Added, Tracks, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Sets, Downloads, Mixes**; each platform lists the items it has in that order. **Setlists live inside Sets** (owner, 2026-11-09: "Setlists goes under Sets now"): the Sets page starts with a **Setlists** row (with the count, only when there are saved setlists) that opens the saved setlists; there is no Setlists item of its own anywhere. The iPhone Library tab lists **Tracks** first (your own files and SoundCloud likes in one list, each row with a small source mark: `folder` for a file, `cloud` for SoundCloud), then Albums, Artists, Stations, Decks, Downloads and History by default (Playlists has its own tab), with the Recently Added grid below. **Edit** (top right, like Music) opens a checklist with drag handles over Tracks, Albums, Artists, Stations, Decks, Sets, Downloads, Mixes and History: tap to show or hide, drag to reorder (`LibraryRowsEditor`, `LibraryRowChoices` in `Shared/ViewModels/LibraryRows.swift`). Sets and Mixes start hidden; Decks and Stations start shown, never buried. The choice is one synced preference (`red.forbes.sounding.synced.libraryRows`) shared with the iPad sidebar's Library section, so hiding Downloads on the iPhone hides it on the iPad too. Hidden pages stay reachable: Mixes through Decks and Auto Mix, Sets through Search's filters. Rows stay native list rows with the system inset separator. **Likes is not a Library item:** it is a pinned playlist, the first item of Playlists on every platform (like Music's Favourite Songs), titled "Likes" with "N tracks" from the library database and a heart (`heart.fill`) over the line art and accent gradient (`LikesPlaylist.swift`); it opens the existing Likes page (sort, Show Columns, downloads), pushed inside Playlists so Playlists stays highlighted, and anything that used to open Likes now opens Playlists › Likes. Without a SoundCloud account every row still shows: Sets and Playlists (and the Likes page) each open a centred `ContentUnavailableView` ("Sign In to See Your Likes", one line on what appears there, and **Sign In**, which starts the normal SoundCloud sign-in). Recently Added no longer hides: it is useful without an account (your own music) and shows SoundCloud items when there is one.
- **Playlists: View As and New Playlist** (iPhone, iPad, Mac; Apple TV stays a grid). The toolbar has **+** (New Playlist) and a menu with a **View As** picker: Grid or List, saved per page in the synced setting `collectionViewModes` (iPhone and iPad default to List, Mac to Grid). List rows are 56 pt artwork, the title and "N tracks · owner"; Likes is first in both views. Albums has the same View As menu on iPhone and iPad (Grid by default, saved per page in `collectionViewModes`). **New Playlist…** is a form sheet: Title (required), Description (optional), Visibility (Private by default), Cancel and Create. It is also the first item of Add to Playlist, the iPad sidebar's New Playlist row and the Mac's File › New Playlist… (⌘N). Creating writes the playlist to the library database at once and takes it out again if SoundCloud refuses; a 429 shows the usual rate notice and an alert saying SoundCloud is limiting requests. The new playlist opens; an empty playlist you own shows a centred "No Tracks Yet" with **Add Tracks**, which opens a searchable picker of your Likes.
- **Playlist folders are Sounding's own** (owner, 2026-11-09: SoundCloud has no folders, so add them Apple Music style). Folders live only in Sounding and sync across your devices through the Account settings record (`PlaylistFolderStore` in `Core/Library/PlaylistFolders.swift`, one `SyncedRecord` per folder in the `playlistFolders` setting); nothing is ever written to SoundCloud, where your playlists stay a flat list. Folders nest, hold playlists and folders in your order, and are keyed to the SoundCloud account, so signing out wipes them from the device and another account never sees them. Rules:
  - **Playlists page** (iPhone, iPad, Mac): folders come first, after Likes, as rows or tiles with a `folder` glyph tile; a playlist inside a folder is shown only in that folder (search finds it anywhere). The **+** is a menu: **New Playlist** and **New Playlist Folder** (an alert with a name field). The Mac adds File › **New Playlist Folder…** (⌥⌘N) and a "Folders" header above My Playlists.
  - **Folder page:** its folders and playlists in your order, the same View As (Grid or List, one saved mode for all folders), **+** with New Playlist and New Folder, and **•••** with Rename and Delete Folder. Empty is a centred "No Playlists" `ContentUnavailableView`. Deleting a folder asks first and never deletes playlists: its contents move up to the parent level.
  - **Every library playlist's context menu** has **Add to Folder** (each folder by path, then **New Folder…**) and **Remove from Folder** when it's in one. Folder context menus have Rename, Remove from Folder (nested ones) and Delete Folder.
  - **Drag and drop** (iPad and Mac): drag a playlist or folder onto a folder to move it in; drop onto a playlist inside a folder to place it before that one. A folder can't move into itself or its own subfolders.
  - **Apple TV is read-only:** folder tiles follow Likes on the Playlists grid and open a grid of their contents.
  - **Sync:** each folder merges on its own, newest change wins; deletes leave a tombstone so they reach every device. **Sidebar pins** sync the same way (`SidebarPins` in `Shared/ViewModels/SidebarPins.swift`, one `SyncedRecord` per pin in the account setting `sidebarPins`, keyed by `playlist:<id>` or `folder:<id>`, with a position for your order); unpinning leaves a tombstone, deleting a playlist (once SoundCloud confirms) or a folder unpins it, and SoundCloud never sees pins. Playlists that are gone from SoundCloud disappear from folders silently (pruned once the library has synced and the folder hasn't changed for a day).
  - Inserts, moves and renames animate with `.snappy`.
- **Managing your own playlists** (owner, 2026-11-09; iPhone, iPad and Mac; Apple TV is read only). Only playlists you own (never someone else's, never an album) get, in the page's ••• menu and in every playlist context menu: **Edit Playlist…**, **Make Public** or **Make Private**, and **Delete Playlist…** (destructive). **Edit Playlist** is a sheet (a form sheet on iPad, a sheet on the Mac) built from the shared `PlaylistEditSheet` (`Shared/UI/Components/PlaylistEditing.swift`): a grouped `Form` with the artwork, Title, Description (multi-line), a **Public** toggle and the track list in edit mode (drag to reorder, swipe or minus to remove), Cancel and Done. Done sends one `PUT` with the new state through `PlaylistEditCenter` (`Core/SoundCloud/PlaylistEditCenter.swift`); the page and library update at once and roll back, with the error inline in the sheet, if SoundCloud refuses. **Delete Playlist…** asks first, "Delete Playlist?" / "This deletes it from SoundCloud for everyone." (an action sheet on iPhone, an alert on iPad and Mac), then removes it from the library, from any playlist folder and from the iPad sidebar (a playlist made this session too) and pops the page. Track rows inside an owned playlist get **Remove from Playlist**; on the Mac, Delete removes the selected row and rows drag to reorder while the table is in playlist order (no column sort). There are no Move Up/Down items. Private playlists show a small `lock.fill` before the secondary line of tiles and rows and the hero's metadata line, on every platform including Apple TV.
- **Mac sidebar Playlists section**, after Library: **All Playlists, Likes and Mixes only** (owner, 2026-11-09: "don't put all playlists in the sidebar, just All Playlists, Likes, Mixes"), plus your playlist folders after Likes (folders only, never a row per playlist; each pushes its folder page and has Rename, Pin to Sidebar and Delete Folder in its context menu). The section collapses. Playlists you pin go in the **Pinned** section (§2 Mac). Individual playlists open inside All Playlists (albums inside Albums); New Playlist lives in All Playlists' toolbar and File › New Playlist… (⌘N).
- **Recently Added is one mixed, date-sorted feed, newest first** (`Shared/ViewModels/RecentlyAddedFeed.swift`, used by iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV), like Music's. It holds: playlists you made (creation date) or liked (like date), liked albums (shown as Album), liked tracks (like date), people you follow (follow date, circular avatar, opens their profile) and your own music (albums grouped by their tags; loose files grouped by artist and day). **Liked tracks:** a day with three or more likes is one **N Liked Tracks** tile (2x2 artwork mosaic, subtitle "Liked · Today") that opens Likes; one or two likes show as individual tiles that play. Every tile carries a small subtitle saying what it is: "Liked Track · Artist", "Playlist · Owner", "Album · Owner", "Following", "Your Music · Artist". The feed covers the last six months and at most 200 items, is built off the main thread from the library database (no extra SoundCloud requests), and only republishes when the list actually changes, so scrolling never re-sorts. A grid of tiles on every platform (lazy, stable ids); loading is the shared `LoadingPlaceholder`, empty is a centred `ContentUnavailableView`. Dates: likes and liked playlists use SoundCloud's `created_at` on the like; SoundCloud does not date follows, so the library sync stamps a follow with the time it first saw it (follows found by the first full backfill have no date and stay out of the feed); own music uses the time the scan first saw the file in a folder that was already scanned, else the file's modified date.
- **Albums** are liked SoundCloud albums plus albums built from the tags of your own files; an own-file album linked to a SoundCloud album (§9 Library identity) is **one tile** with both source marks and opens the own-file album. **Artists** are the artists of your own files plus the artists of SoundCloud albums you like, one entry per artist: an own-file artist linked to a SoundCloud user is a single row carrying both marks (`folder` and `cloud`). People you follow are under the account menu's Following. Albums is an adaptive grid from the shared `LibraryCollectionsModel` (`Shared/ViewModels/LibraryCollections.swift`) and `LibraryCollectionGrid`; a SoundCloud album opens its playlist page, a local album opens a track list with Play and Shuffle. Album pages show **Also on SoundCloud** (own-file album) or **In Your Files** (SoundCloud album) as a small glass link to the other copy (`LibraryAlbumCounterpartLink`), and Play on a SoundCloud album plays the linked local files where they exist. **Artists** looks like Music on iPad (`LibraryArtistsBrowser.swift`): at regular width (iPad, Mac) a compact list about a third of the width (260–380 pt), each row about 56 pt with a 40 pt circular avatar (SoundCloud avatar, else artwork, else line art), the name and its source marks, sorted by Name or Recently Added from a sort button, searchable; the selected row is a rounded **`Color.scAccent`** fill like the sidebar (label in `scAccentOn`, as every orange fill we draw), never the system blue and never a capsule (state-driven buttons, never `List(selection:)`), and the first artist is selected when nothing is. Rows have context menus: Play, Shuffle, **Link to SoundCloud Artist…** (a searchable sheet of the people you follow, like or have albums from) and **Unlink**. Beside it the selected artist fills the pane like Music's library artist page, over the artwork's `CollectionPageMesh` with the collection accent: a compact header (168 pt circular image left of the name, falling under it when narrow; the name in large bold with a chevron to the full artist page; source marks with "N tracks · N albums"; glass **Play** and **Shuffle**; **View on SoundCloud** when linked, opening the SoundCloud profile), then **Albums** (own-file and SoundCloud albums in one grid), **Tracks** (compact rows in columns, eight then Show All, each with its source mark), a **Sets & Mixes** shelf (tracks at least the Sets length) and **Appears On** (albums by others that credit the artist). No pastel blue anywhere on the page. On iPhone and compact width it is the list only, and a row pushes that same page. Apple TV keeps its Artists grid.
- **Social has no tab or sidebar section** (owner, 2026-11-09): Profile, Activity, Following and Followers open from the account avatar menu (top right on iPhone and iPad, the toolbar account menu on the Mac), each a page pushed in the current stack. **Following and Followers are one shared people page** (`PeopleDirectoryModel` in `Shared/ViewModels/PeopleDirectory.swift`, `PeopleDirectoryView` in `Shared/UI/Components/PeopleDirectoryViews.swift`, used by iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV), read whole from the library database with no extra requests: search (`.searchable`; the tvOS search field on Apple TV), a Sort menu (Recently Followed, Name, Most Followers, remembered per page), and per person the avatar, name, follower count and a bordered **Follow** or **Following** button (unfollowing asks first; the button never turns orange). Rows on iPhone and iPad, a circle grid on Mac and Apple TV. SoundCloud gives no follow date, so the library sync stamps the first time it sees a follow or follower after its first full sync; those show "Followed in March" (with the year when it is not this year) and sort first, older ones show no date and keep SoundCloud's order after them. The date lives in the library database record and travels with its existing peer and iCloud sync. Recently Added reads the same stamps for follows. There is no Files row: music folders are chosen in Settings › Music Folders. No separator above the first row of the Library or Songs lists.
- The Liquid Glass tab bar, with the mini player as the tab bar's bottom accessory.
- **Widgets** (`SoundingTV-Widget`): **Now Playing** (small, medium, Lock Screen rectangular, circular and inline) and **Recently Played** (small, medium, Lock Screen rectangular and circular), plus the Play or Pause and Resume controls. Native widget idioms only: system tertiary fill background, system tint, `.widgetAccentable()` on the title and controls, artwork in accented and clear modes through `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.desaturated)`. Empty states are centred (symbol, title, one line) and the gallery and placeholder show a redacted sample. Artwork is never fetched in a widget: the app writes a 240 px JPEG per snapshot item (now playing, last mix and the recents) into the app group's `WidgetArtwork` folder (`WidgetArtworkWriter`, from the shared artwork cache) and the widget reads that file, falling back to a music-note tile. Taps use `soundingtv://widget/...` links (`SoundingWidgetLink`): Recently Played and Continue play the item (a track resumes at its saved position; an already loaded track just plays), Now Playing opens the app. The publisher reloads both widget kinds and the controls, debounced behind its 750 ms write and only when the track, play state, seek, mix or the first recents change or new artwork lands. A playing entry flips to finished at the track's end on its own timeline, so a widget never keeps claiming playback after an app that died. **Play** (owner, 2026-11-12) is the configurable widget: it is edited to pick one playlist, setlist or station (`CollectionWidgetIntent`, `WidgetCollectionEntity`; the picker lists Likes, playlists, setlists and stations the app has published) and shows it with a **Play** button, plus **Shuffle** for playlists; while it plays the button is Pause. Small and medium show the one pick. **Large** and **extra large** (iPad and Mac) add a list of the other published collections beneath or beside it, each row one tap to play. Now Playing and Recently Played also take large and extra large. Playback always goes through the `PlayWidgetCollectionIntent` `AudioPlaybackIntent` in the app, never in the extension; the widget reads `sounding-widget-collections.json` and its artwork from the app group (`WidgetCollectionPublisher` writes them, debounced, from the cached library, setlists and the Stations model, at most 24 entries, with no new requests). Next Track is a control beside Play or Pause and Resume. The Now Playing Live Activity also supplies a compact layout (`.small`, `.medium` activity families) for the Watch Smart Stack and CarPlay: artwork, title, artist or next track and a progress bar, read-only.
- **Live Activities** only show what the system media controls on the Lock Screen cannot: Auto Mix (next move, countdown to the mix, Mix Now), the track inside a mix with a tracklist, or a Sleep Timer countdown. Never for plain playback, never led by title, artwork, play/pause or next.
- Large titles on root screens and library pages; inline titles on other pushed screens. On iPad, library pages put the title inline (§2 Library page chrome).
- Swipe actions and context menus on every row and card.
- **Long-press lifts a tight rounded card, like Apple Music** (owner, 2026-11-09: "nicer selection boundary"). Every iOS/iPadOS `.contextMenu` target sets its preview and pointer-hover shape right before the menu with a shared modifier (`DownloadsUI.swift`): `.libraryTilePreviewShape()` for grid and shelf tiles (continuous 22 pt corners, 8 pt even padding around the tile's content, never the cell's gutters), `.libraryCardPreviewShape()` for hero cards, `.libraryRowPreviewShape()` for rows (continuous 14 pt corners, just the row, never edge to edge), `.libraryCirclePreviewShape()` for a bare circular image, and the mini player's own capsule for its preview. The hover effect uses the same shape. New menu targets must use one of these. The Mac has no lift; its hover and selection shapes match the same rounded rectangle.
- **iPhone matches Music's compact screens** (owner, 2026-11-09). Spacing tokens in `PhoneLayout` (`HomeShelfComponents.swift`): 20 pt page margin, 16 pt between tiles, 32 pt between shelves, 12 pt under a header, 30 pt library icon column; use them instead of new numbers. **Home**: large title "Home"; Continue From hero first; the first non-people shelf that isn't Recent is a row of tall hero cards (`HomeHeroCardLabel`: square artwork over a ground taken from the artwork's `CollectionAccent` wash, white text, the next card peeking 24 pt); every shelf header is "Title ›" and opens the whole shelf (`HomeShelfDetailView`: a list for tracks, a grid otherwise), no new requests. **Search**: follows the Apple Music search layout (rules in the 2026-11-09 "Search like Apple Music" decision); scopes are the system scope bar (`.searchScopes`, shown while searching): SoundCloud, Library. **Library**: index rows are `LibraryIndexLabel` (accent icon from the shared `SidebarSymbol` set in a fixed column, title3 text, chevron, separator inset past the icon), Recently Added grid below with a "Recently Added ›" header. Section titles are `PhoneSectionTitle` (title3 bold). Track and user rows are 48 pt artwork.
- **Home has no inline banners** (owner, 2026-11-12: "awful hidden hard to see banners"). Anything that needs the user's attention rises as the shared bottom card (`bottomCard` in `Shared/UI/Components/BottomCard.swift`, a sheet on the Mac): a nearby Apple TV waiting to sign in (the nearby card, §9), or **Reconnect SoundCloud** when the account needs reconnecting (§9). The old "Apple TV is waiting to sign in" and "Sign in again to refresh your picks." rows are gone from iPhone Home. Empty shelves keep the centred `ContentUnavailableView` states (reconnect, guest, failed).
- **Decks on iPhone and iPad** (`PhoneDecksView`, owner 2026-11-09: "for iOS decks improve their UX"). Always dark, over the on-air deck's artwork mesh (`CollectionPageMesh`, toned dark under a scrim; plain chassis under Reduce Transparency). Opened full screen it has a glass **✕** at the top left; as the iPad Decks tab it has none. Three layouts from one set of parts:
  - **iPhone landscape:** the glass top bar (✕, the Auto Mix strip, the TV button, •••), the stacked waveforms filling the middle, then Deck A, the mixer and Deck B in a 180 pt row. Deck B is mirrored so both transports sit at the outer edges under the thumbs. The mixer shows one EQ band plus the filter, chosen with a High/Mid/Low segmented control above the knobs.
  - **iPhone portrait (one-handed):** ✕, "Decks" and ••• at the top, then waveforms, Deck A, the full mixer (High, Mid, Low, Filter, channel faders) and Deck B in a scroll; the **crossfader and the Auto Mix strip are pinned at the bottom** in a `safeAreaBar`, where the thumb is.
  - **iPad (regular width and height):** bigger parts (56 pt artwork and transport, 60 pt pads, 50 pt knobs); waveforms about a quarter of the height; Deck A, the full mixer and Deck B side by side from 860 pt of width, else the two decks side by side over a centred mixer; under them an inline **Library** (Suggested, Up Next, Likes, Library; search by title, artist, BPM or key; A and B buttons on every row; rows drag onto either deck), shown or hidden with the `music.note.list` glass button and in Decks View. Compact width uses the iPhone portrait layout.
  - **Each deck:** artwork (line art when there is none), badge, title, artist with the On Air / Mixing In chip, remaining time, then "128.0 BPM · 8A" or the state ("Loading 40%", "Finding BPM…", "No BPM"). Transport: CUE (hold to preview from here; playing, back to the cue point), Play, **Sync** (a toggle: on locks tempo and phase to the other deck, tapping again or touching tempo releases it; disabled without a BPM), and a **Tempo** knob (±16%, "+1.2%" beside it when there is room). Pads: a **Loop** button (tap toggles a beat-quantised loop; hold for ¼ beat to 16 bars) and four hot cues A–D (tap an empty pad to set it; hold a set pad to delete it), or 1/2/4/8 bars while looping. Holding the header gives Load Another Track…, Tempo & Key…, Analyse Now and Eject. Empty is a centred "Deck A Is Empty" with **Load Track…**; a failed load is a centred error with **Choose Another Track**. Any deck accepts a dropped track.
  - **Gestures:** drag a waveform lane sideways to nudge a playing deck (a temporary ±8% bend) or scrub a paused one, snapping to beats with a selection tick per beat; pinch to zoom through 4, 8, 16, 32 and 64 bars (remembered). Every knob and fader: drag to move, **double-tap to reset** (EQ, filter and tempo to centre, channel fader to full, crossfader to centre), **touch and hold for fine control** (one fifth of the travel, with a "Fine · −3.0 dB" glass bubble). EQ, filter, tempo and crossfader have a **centre detent** with a rigid haptic; the ends give a soft one; pads, play, cue and hot cues tap lightly; Sync succeeds with a success haptic and warns when it can't.
  - **Controllers (iPad, owner 2026-11-12):** a supported controller drives Decks as on the Mac once Decks has opened, with the same own-music rule (`dj-controllers.md`). ••• › **Controllers…** opens a sheet with the shared Controllers and Network MIDI sections: a **Network MIDI** toggle (off by default, remembered on the device) that makes the iPad a network MIDI session, then the network MIDI hosts nearby, each with **Connect** or **Disconnect**.
  - **Auto Mix strip:** off, "Auto Mix is off · You have both decks" with **Turn On**; before a move "Mix into *Track*" over the move and countdown ("Echo out, then fade · in 12 bars") with **Mix Now**; during it "Bringing in *Track*" over "Bass swap · Bar 3 of 16" and step dots; any control you touch turns it yellow, "*Control* is yours" with Take Over All and **Hand Back** (on the next beat). Its menu: Auto Mix, Mix Now, Extend 8 Bars, Mix In Next…, Skip Next Track, Take Over All.
  - **Loading:** a searchable sheet ("Load Deck A", "Mix In Next") with a segmented Suggested / Up Next / Likes / Library control. Suggested ranks Up Next and the Library against the other deck (or what's playing) by key (same, or one step on the Camelot wheel) and tempo (within 6%, half and double time count), up to 50, headed "Matches *Title* · 126.0 BPM · 8A"; every row shows its source mark, BPM and key (the key green when it fits). Tap loads the named deck, swipe loads the other, the context menu offers both and Mix In Next.
  - **•••:** Load Deck A…, Load Deck B…, Mix In Next…; Save This Set (the current Auto Mix session, into Mixes) and Play On…; Mix on *TV*; Decks View…, Lock to Landscape (iPhone); Close Decks. Lock to Landscape holds the decks in landscape only while they're on screen.
  - Dynamic Type is capped at XL in iPhone landscape, XXL on iPad and XXXL in portrait; at accessibility sizes the library rows wrap and move BPM and key into the second line.
- **Decks on the Apple TV** (`PhoneRemoteDecksView`, inside `PhoneDecksView`, iPhone and iPad). When Output is a linked Apple TV that has Decks on screen, opening Decks goes straight to the TV's decks; the phone's own decks stay one tap away. The header is a glass capsule, "Mixing on Living Room" over one status line ("Decks aren't open on Living Room", the Auto Mix headline, "Auto Mix is off" with **Turn On**, or "*Control* is yours" with **Hand Back**), then an **iPhone** glass button that returns to the phone's decks, and •••: Auto Mix, Extend 8 Bars, Hand Back, Load Next…, Use iPhone Decks, Close Decks. Each TV deck shows artwork, badge, title, artist, remaining time, BPM at the current tempo, a position bar with elapsed time and the cue point, and On Air, Mixing In or Up Next; under it CUE (playing: back to the cue point and pause; paused: set the cue point here), Play/Pause, Sync and a tempo knob (±16%). The mixer is High, Mid, Low and Filter per deck, then the crossfader; the knobs and crossfader are the phone decks' own controls (double-tap to centre, touch and hold for fine control, centre detents with haptics), over the TV's On Air artwork mesh, with the same ✕ when opened full screen; on iPad the decks and mixer sit side by side in both orientations. **Mix Now**, **Load Next…** and **Extend** sit at the bottom. Load Next lists the TV's Up Next first, then SoundCloud Likes, and puts the pick next on the TV. During Auto Mix any touch takes that control over on the TV, exactly like the phone's own decks. Deck A is always the TV's On Air deck, as on the TV, so the crossfader is drawn A on the left whichever TV deck that is. Not reachable is a centred `ContentUnavailableView` with **Try Again** and **Use iPhone Decks**. In the phone's own decks, an **appletv** glass button named after the TV (and "Mix on Living Room" in •••) goes back. Never call it a "remote mode".

### iPad
- `TabView(.sidebarAdaptable)` is the one main sidebar, drawn by the system like Music on iPad: Home, Search, Decks, Stations; then **Pinned** (only when something is pinned, see below); then a collapsible **Library** section: Recently Added, Tracks, Albums, Artists and History by default, with Sets (with Setlists inside) and Downloads one tap away in Edit (owner, 2026-11-09: "the sidebar is quite noisy"; the Library rows follow the synced `LibraryRowChoices` shared with the iPhone Library list, in its order; any choice the user made before stays); then a collapsible **Playlists** section with **All Playlists** (the Playlists page, with View As and New Playlist +), **Likes**, one item per top-level playlist folder (folder glyph and name, opening its folder page; `PhoneTab.playlistFolder`, customization id `pad.folder.<id>`, which the retired-item cleanup never touches) and **Mixes**, never a row per playlist (owner, 2026-11-09). If the selected folder is deleted the sidebar lands on All Playlists. A playlist from your library (own or liked, known from the library database, debounced) opened from any other item selects All Playlists and opens there (`SidebarNavigator.open(_:)`, `SidebarPendingPlaylistDestination`); inside All Playlists it pushes in place. Someone else's playlist and albums still push inside the current item. A saved selection that names a retired item (a playlist row, Setlists, New Playlist) lands on All Playlists or Sets, and a retired Social item lands on Home. There is no Social section (owner, 2026-11-09). The sidebar is edited exactly like Music's, entirely by the system (`.tabViewCustomization`, stored in UserDefaults under `PadSidebarCustomization.storageKey`): **Edit** shows a checkmark circle per item to show or hide it, drag handles to reorder, **Reset**, and the system's "Drag to customise items in the sidebar or tab bar."; any sidebar item can be dragged into the tab bar, and the tab bar's items can be reordered there. Every `Tab` and `TabSection` carries a stable `customizationID`; items that start sidebar-only use `.defaultVisibility(.hidden, for: .tabBar)`. Home can be reordered but not hidden, Search can't be moved, Decks, Stations and every Library and Playlists item can be hidden, reordered and dragged into the tab bar. Every section (Pinned, Library, Playlists) collapses from its header chevron like Music's, and the system remembers it on the device. The collapsed state lives in UIKit's tab customisation, which SwiftUI's `TabViewCustomization` can't carry, so the customisation is held in a plain store (`PadSidebarCustomizationStore`) that the tab view reads but SwiftUI doesn't observe: a system customisation write never re-renders the root and never pushes a stale customisation back, which used to reopen a section the moment it closed. Never hold it in `@AppStorage` or `@State` again. Nothing re-sets the sidebar's selection when it hasn't changed (a redundant write re-expands the section that holds the selected item), and the root tab view takes no inputs it doesn't draw, so unrelated playback updates never rebuild the sidebar. Sidebar glyphs come from the shared `SidebarSymbol` set (`Shared/Design/SidebarSymbols.swift`, also used by the Mac sidebar, the iPhone Library list and the Apple TV Library sidebar), drawn in brand orange at regular weight, one distinct glyph per item: Recently Added `clock`, Tracks `music.note`, Albums `square.stack`, Artists `music.microphone`, Sets `headphones`, Downloads `arrow.down.circle`, History `clock.arrow.circlepath`, Decks `dial.medium`, Stations `dot.radiowaves.left.and.right`, All Playlists `music.note.list`, Likes `heart`, Mixes `waveform.path`, a folder `folder`, a pinned playlist `play.square.stack`. Row height and section spacing are the system's.
- **Pins, like Music's** (owner, 2026-11-09: "allow me to put playlists and folders of playlists in the sidebar"). **Pin to Sidebar** and **Unpin from Sidebar** are in every library playlist's context menu, every folder's context menu, a playlist page's and a folder page's ••• menu, on iPad and Mac (`SidebarPinButton`; never on iPhone or Apple TV, which have no sidebar). Albums and playlists outside your library can't be pinned. Pins sit in a **Pinned** section after Stations, in your order; with nothing pinned there is no section. A pinned playlist is its own sidebar item (`PhoneTab.playlist`), so opening it from anywhere selects it (`SidebarNavigator.open(_:)`); a pinned folder is a disclosure group with its playlists nested and an **All Playlists** row first that opens the folder page (`PhoneTab.pinnedFolder`). Reorder with the sidebar's Edit; Unpin is in each pin's context menu. Deleting a playlist or a folder unpins it. Pins sync like playlist folders (below). A saved customization that still names a retired item (`pad.library.likes`, `pad.settings`, `pad.library.playlists`, `pad.library.setlists`, `pad.library.mixes`, `pad.playlists.new`, `pad.social`) is dropped at launch (`PadSidebarCustomization`). The sidebar toggle sits at the top right of the sidebar. The signed-in user ends the sidebar, as the last row of its own list (`tabViewSidebarFooter`, not a floating bottom bar, no extra top padding): avatar circle, display name in regular weight and a secondary trailing gear, opening the Settings sheet; signed out it is a **Sign In** row with a gear button beside it that opens Settings. It scrolls with the list, so it can never cover the last item. There is no Settings sidebar item: Settings is that sheet (`SettingsPresenter`), and ⌘, opens it too. Every item shows without a SoundCloud account; the SoundCloud ones open the same centred sign-in state as iPhone. Never a `List(selection:)`. No Files item.
- Each sidebar item owns its own `NavigationStack`, so a pushed page keeps its item highlighted and switching items returns to where you were in each.
- **The account avatar sits at the top right of iPad root pages too** (Home, Library pages, All Playlists, Likes, Sets), the same menu as iPhone without its Settings item (owner, 2026-11-09, reversing the earlier no-avatar rule): Profile, Activity, Following, Followers, pushed in the current item's stack. Settings stays on the sidebar footer. The selected sidebar row is orange (§1).
- **The account never flickers.** The sidebar footer, the avatar menu, the Mac footer and the Apple TV read `SoundCloudCredentialSource.accountUser`: the current user, else the last user confirmed in this session. A session synced from iCloud or a linked device, or a token refresh, keeps the name and avatar on screen until `/me` answers; only a real sign-out or signing in as someone else clears it. It never drops to "SoundCloud" or the stock picture in between.
- Home's For You / Following menu sits in the trailing toolbar group on iPad, beside the account avatar, never alone at the leading edge beside the floating tab bar; the title menu offers the same choice.
- **Home has no inline banners** (owner, 2026-11-12: "awful hidden hard to see banners"). Anything that needs the user's attention rises as the shared bottom card (`bottomCard` in `Shared/UI/Components/BottomCard.swift`, a sheet on the Mac): a nearby Apple TV waiting to sign in (the nearby card, §9), or **Reconnect SoundCloud** when the account needs reconnecting (§9). The old "Apple TV is waiting to sign in" and "Sign in again to refresh your picks." rows are gone from iPad Home. Empty shelves keep the centred `ContentUnavailableView` states (reconnect, guest, failed).
- Settings is a sheet, opened from the account row at the bottom of the sidebar (or the gear beside Sign In, or ⌘,), the same Settings sheet as iPhone. Its category list is a `List` with `.listStyle(.sidebar)` of buttons, never `List(selection:)` (that re-sets the tabs every frame and pins the CPU at 100%). The account is a header row at the top (avatar, name, @username) that opens Account; every other row is a one-line `Label` with its `SettingsIconTile`, fixed row insets, no separators and no glass. No nested split views: Playlists and Following push list, then detail, inside their tab's stack.
- Nothing behind the tabs or split columns paints an opaque background, so the system glass shows the content beneath. Sidebars look however the system draws them. On the iPadOS 27.2 simulator that is a full-height glass column with square edges, for Apple's Settings and Files and for a bare `TabView` or `NavigationSplitView` alike. No app code there makes it an inset rounded panel; check a real iPad before chasing one.
- **Artist and profile pages are Apple Music artist pages** (owner, 2026-11-09; iPhone and iPad, `ArtistDetailView`). No section tabs and no page search. From the top: the full-bleed `PersonHero` (banner or blurred avatar, the circular avatar beside the name, a Liquid Glass capsule for Verified, Following or the follower count, the round accent Play); the system Back and, top right, **Follow** (`person.crop.circle.badge.plus`, checkmark once following; unfollowing asks first) and •••, both system glass toolbar buttons. Then **Latest Release** beside **Top Tracks** (iPad: release 220 pt wide, top tracks in a two-column grid of 8; iPhone: the release row, then 5 track rows), then `CollectionShelf` rows: **Albums**, **Singles & EPs** (set type EP or Single, `ArtistReleaseKind`), **Playlists**, **Reposts** and **Likes**, each hidden when empty and each with a "›" that pushes the full list (sort, Show Columns, search and paging live there). Then **About <name>**: a full-width `.fill.quaternary` band (`PersonAboutSection`, shared with the Mac) with the bio (four lines, More), the location, Followers, Following and Tracks (the first two open the people pages) and the website and SoundCloud links. Last, **Similar Artists**: a circular shelf (`PersonShelfTile`) with a "›" page, from SoundCloud's related artists, falling back to the most followed artists they follow who have tracks (`ArtistSimilarModel`, cached 15 minutes per artist). Reposts, Likes and Similar Artists load only when the About band scrolls into view, one request each, in order, and stop at a rate limit. The Mac keeps its section tabs and gains the Singles & EPs shelf, the About band (the bio moves out of the header into it) and Similar Artists on Overview.
- Adaptive grids sized to the container.
- Lists and grids fill the whole detail column with the system margins, like Music. No centred readable-width cap.
- Add Folder is a row in Settings › Music Folders and the action of the empty Songs state. One file importer per screen; a scanning folder shows "Scanning N of M…", and a folder that can't be added says so in an alert.
- Pointer hover (lift) and keyboard shortcuts: Space to play/pause, ⌘←/⌘→ to skip, ⌘F to search. Space never plays or pauses while a text field is being edited.
- The mini player is the Music-style bar along the bottom of the detail area (§5 Mini player), always shown, with "Not Playing" when nothing plays.
- Popovers for anything anchored to a control: Play On and Go to Artist from the mini player, Play On from Now Playing. They adapt to sheets at compact width. Add to Playlist comes from menus, so it's a centred form sheet. On the Mac the track context menu and the Now Playing ••• menu both have **Add to Playlist…**, which opens the same searchable sheet as the iPhone (`AddToPlaylistModel` is the shared model; the Mac sheet is `MacAddToPlaylistSheet`, presented by the root window).
- Drag and drop: track rows drag (as a Sounding track, or the SoundCloud link to other apps). Drop on a playlist row to add to it, or on the mini player to add to Up Next.
- Changing the width never leaves the selection on a tab that's about to hide. At compact width the tab bar is the iPhone's (Home, Library, Playlists, Search): a Library item, Decks or Stations shows as Library, a Playlists item (All Playlists, Likes, Mixes) as Playlists.
- The iPhone layout must stay unchanged.

### Mac (macOS 27)
- **The transport lives in the window toolbar**, like Music on macOS 26. There's no bottom bar.
- **Toolbar items, and nothing else:** back/forward (pushed pages only) and the transport as one leading glass group; the now-playing capsule as the principal item; Play On, Up Next and Activity as one trailing group; then the page's own items and Search. No volume, Like or ••• in the toolbar: Like and the track menu live on the capsule's context menu and in Now Playing. Nothing ever goes into the `>>` overflow, from the minimum width (1040 pt) up.
- **Capsule:** 38 pt tall, 30 pt artwork inset equally top, bottom and leading, one line of title + artist (artist drops first, then the title truncates), and a thin progress line; times appear on hover. Its width is computed from the window, sidebar and inspector widths (`MacToolbarMetrics`) and shrinks from 440 pt to 180 pt before anything else gives.
- The toolbar is Liquid Glass over content with the system `.soft` top scroll-edge effect. Don't set `titlebarAppearsTransparent`; it disables the edge effect. No titlebar separator, no band. `MacViewportScrollView` also masks scrolled content out under the toolbar with a 10 pt fade at its bottom edge, so cards and rows never sit behind toolbar controls. Artist heroes are the exception: their art runs under the toolbar from the first frame (a backdrop behind the scroll view that follows the scroll offset, since scroll content can't draw into the toolbar inset), with the edge effect and mask off, until the hero has scrolled out of the window. Album and playlist pages are the other exception: their artwork backdrop is part of the scroll content and reaches up under the toolbar, with the edge effect and mask off until the backdrop has scrolled away (§3 Collection backdrop).
- **Headers never put text under the toolbar:** header text, buttons and pickers fade to 0 as they reach the toolbar (`macFadesUnderToolbar`). Only artwork and hero backgrounds may pass under it. Pushed pages remove the system title and show their name as a plain toolbar title (`MacScrolledPageTitle`) only once the header title has scrolled away. Page-level pickers live in the content (Home's For You/Following, Search's SoundCloud/Library as a `safeAreaBar`), never as a second principal item.
- **Section tabs** (artist and profile) are Liquid Glass capsule buttons in a `GlassEffectContainer`, the selected one `.glassProminent`; when they don't fit, the first tabs stay and the rest move into a "More" menu.
- **Artist and profile pages** use the shared `PersonHero` parts (banner or blurred avatar under the toolbar, white name and badge over the scrim) and, under the header, the iPad's Overview: **Latest Release** (the newest album, else the newest track as a single), **Top Tracks** (SoundCloud's most played, "›" opens Tracks), then **Albums** and **Playlists** as `CollectionShelf` rows of `ArtworkShelfCard`s. Loading on Overview, Tracks, Likes, Reposts, Activity and the people tabs is the shaped `LoadingPlaceholder`, not a spinner. Your own-file albums open on the shared `CollectionHeroHeader` with the artwork accent, like SoundCloud albums.
- **Track lists:** where the list is the page or tab (playlist, album, artist Tracks), one sortable list on the page background with a transparent header that pins under the toolbar, right-aligned numeric columns, rounded inset hover and selection, column show/hide from the header's context menu, and a quiet "N tracks, X minutes" footer.
