Shared grid in `MacTrackTableMetrics`. Overview previews are compact rows with a "›" section header that opens the full list. Likes stays a native `Table` with a transparent background, no zebra rows and the same footer.
- Up Next, Comments, Tracklist and Behind the Track live in a native `.inspector`. Up Next says "Playing from <playlist, album, station or Likes>" under its title.
- The `Settings` scene is laid out like System Settings: a sidebar of tiled categories (§3 Settings icons) in four groups, and a `.formStyle(.grouped)` page beside it titled with the category. The **Auto Mix** page starts with the same controls as iPhone's Playback › DJ (`AutoMixBasicsSections`: Auto Mix, then only while it's on Style, Blend, Keep Queue Order and the live Status, then an Energy section with Follow the Night, Energy and Riser), then Your Moves. Playback no longer carries Auto Mix. Match My Workout stays iPhone-only. Decks › Start Auto Mix… (⌥⌘S) opens the shared start sheet where you are, like the capsule's Auto Mix toggle, instead of switching to Decks.
- Library tables are native `Table`s, with BPM/Key/Genre columns and inline correction.
- **Home has no inline banners** (owner, 2026-11-12: "awful hidden hard to see banners"). Anything that needs the user's attention rises as the shared bottom card (`bottomCard` in `Shared/UI/Components/BottomCard.swift`, a sheet on the Mac): a nearby Apple TV waiting to sign in (the nearby card, §9), or **Reconnect SoundCloud** when the account needs reconnecting (§9). The old "Apple TV is waiting to sign in" and "Sign in again to refresh your picks." rows are gone from Mac Home (it never had them). Empty shelves keep the centred `ContentUnavailableView` states (reconnect, guest, failed).
- **Sidebar, like Apple Music** (owner, 2026-11-09: "update the mac build so that it has a similar split on its sidebar"; 2026-11-12: "can we improve the sidebar"). A system `.sidebar` List in the standard sidebar material. Top group: Home, Search, Decks, Stations, and **Hardware only while a controller or a DJ player is connected** (it is always reachable from Decks › Hardware ⌥⌘H). Then a collapsible **Library** section: Recently Added (the playlists you make or like, newest first, as a card grid), Tracks, Albums, Artists, Sets (with Setlists inside), **Prep**, Downloads, History and Private Shares (Mac only, last); then **Playlists** (All Playlists, Likes, Mixes, then your playlist folders, whose playlists open in place and highlight their own row). **The sidebar is the stock macOS source list, nothing custom** (owner, 2026-11-12: "sidebar is not true macos sidebar is it? the macos app doesn't fit this"): a `List(selection:)` in `.listStyle(.sidebar)` whose rows are plain `Label`s with `.tag`, so the system draws the row height, the neutral selection pill, the bold selected label, the small grey section headers, the hover and the arrow-key selection; nothing paints over the sidebar material (no `MacVisualEffectBackground`, no divider, no custom pill or `macSelectableRow`). The glyphs are the shared `SidebarSymbol` set, tinted brand orange by the app accent, as Apple Music keeps its red in its icons. Symbols: Decks `dial.medium` (as on Apple TV), Hardware `slider.vertical.3`, Sets `headphones`, Prep `waveform.path.badge.plus`, All Playlists `music.note.list`, Private Shares `lock`. The account row is a quiet 24 pt avatar and name at the foot of the sidebar (hover highlight only) and opens Settings. There is no Social section (owner, 2026-11-09): the toolbar's **account avatar**, where the bell was, with a red dot for unread activity, is a menu of Profile, Activity (the inbox popover), Messages, Following, Followers, Your Tracks and Settings…; signed out it offers Sign In… and Settings…. Each section remembers whether it is collapsed; the Library header's context menu shows or hides Recently Added, Albums, Artists, Sets, Downloads and Private Shares. **Pinned** sits after the top items when anything is pinned (same rules as iPad Pins): a pinned playlist is a `.playlist(id:)` row that opens its page and stays highlighted, a pinned folder is a disclosure row (`MacPinnedFolderRow`) that opens its folder page (`.pinnedFolder(id:)`) with its playlists nested. Drag pins to reorder them, drag a playlist or folder onto Pinned to pin it, drag a pin out of Pinned onto the rest of the sidebar to unpin it; each pin's context menu has Unpin from Sidebar. Following and Followers are people grids from the library database (searchable, the same grid as an artist's Following tab), not the profile page with a tab picked; Activity is the inbox as a page with Mark All Read. The account row stays pinned at the foot of the sidebar and opens Settings. Tracks is the default page: your files and SoundCloud likes in one `Table` with the Source column shown as a mark plus label, and a Show menu for All, Files or SoundCloud, folder and Add Folder…. Likes is not a Library item: it is the second row of Playlists and the pinned first tile of All Playlists (Library › Likes ⌥⌘1 opens it). Albums is the shared grid and Artists the shared list-beside-artist layout described under iPhone (artists from your own files and liked SoundCloud albums, merged by Library identity; followed artists are under the account menu's Following). Each sidebar section keeps its own navigation stack: a pushed page keeps its section highlighted, switching sections restores each one's stack, and deep links select the section they belong to (an album opens under Albums, a playlist under All Playlists, your own profile under Profile; an artist opened from a browsing page pushes in place). Songs is the page a new window opens on. Signed out, every item stays; a SoundCloud page is a centred `ContentUnavailableView` ("Sign In to See Your Likes", **Sign In…** opening the SoundCloud sign-in sheet). No On This Mac item; folders live in Settings › Library › Folders.
- **Source lists are `List(selection:)`; content lists are not.** The main sidebar and the Settings category list are system `List(selection:)` sidebars (measured 2026-11-12: idle CPU settles to about 3%, so the old CPU-pinning rule no longer holds for them). Messages, the Artists source list and the DJ Prep and Library lists are a plain `List` whose rows are buttons that set a `@State`, `@Binding` or model selection, drawn with `macSelectableRow` (`MacPaneHeader.swift`): a rounded fill behind the row, selected state in the environment so secondary text follows. Sidebar-style rows (`.sidebar`) take the neutral pill above; only the DJ Library list inside the dark Decks panel (`.content`) fills with the orange accent and `Color.scAccentOn` ink. The Mac app's global accent is the brand orange (`ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_GLOBAL_ACCENT_COLOR_NAME = AccentColor`), so `Color.accentColor` and default controls are never system blue. Up and Down move the selection and Return activates through `macListKeyboardSelection`; double-click, context menus and drag and drop stay on the rows. Controls inside content stay default.
- **Decks** keep their layout and scale down as one surface below their natural width, so the console never scrolls sideways from 1040 pt up.
- **Decks is one dark stage with one inspector** (owner, 2026-11-09: "white on white, double sidebar??"; 2026-11-12: "decks should just be perform"). Decks is Perform and nothing else: there is no mode switch. Decks forces the dark appearance for the whole window while it's showing (`preferredColorScheme(.dark)`), so its toolbar, sheets, popovers and alerts are dark too. Opening Decks collapses the main sidebar and leaving restores it, like MainStage's Perform mode. Perform's Auto Mix/Library panel and the main inspector (Up Next or Prep) are the one inspector: opening one closes the other. Toolbar: Decks View, DJ Players, Search Library (⌘F) and the panel toggle. Waveform lanes stay quietly empty with only the deck badge; the deck below carries the empty state. Every Decks sheet, including the AppKit rekordbox folder sheet, takes the dark appearance of the window it opens from.
- **Prep lives in the Library, Plan lives in Perform** (owner, 2026-11-12: "prep goes into library somehow? and then plan idk it doesn't even damn load"). **Prep** is a Library sidebar item (`MacPrepPane`, Prep › Show Prep ⌥⌘P) in the window's own appearance, light or dark, unless Settings › Library › Prep › **Appearance** is **Dark** (owner, 2026-11-12: "make a setting"; `PrepAppearance`, device-local, default **Match Window**), which keeps the Prep page, the Prep inspector and the Prep window dark whatever the window is, applied live (`preferredColorScheme(.dark)`, so like Decks the window takes the dark appearance while Prep or its inspector is showing and its sheets, popovers and menus follow; the Mac inspector column is system material, so an environment-only scheme would leave light chrome behind dark text): the crate list is a source menu in the title while the main sidebar shows, and its own plain sidebar-material list when the main sidebar is hidden; the rekordbox section is two short rows, **Import Library…** and **Import XML…**. Any track row's context menu has **Prep**, which opens the track in the main window's trailing inspector (`MacPrepInspectorPane`: artwork, title, Open in Prep, then the grid, cue, start/end, analysis and memory-cue form) like Get Info; a deck without a grid offers Tap Tempo, Set Downbeat and Edit in Prep, which open the separate Prep window (Window › Prep Window) on that track so the stage stays put. Prep's track list is the Library database (own files, prepared tracks, then SoundCloud likes); with no files it opens on Likes rather than an empty All Tracks. **Plan** is a sheet over Perform (Decks › Plan Transitions… ⌘3, the Auto Mix panel's Plan button, or Plan Transition In from a track): a dark canvas with a Done button, the live strip, the Up Next crate and the transition editor. It reads only the next ten queued tracks and describes them concurrently, so it opens in a moment instead of walking the whole queue; an empty queue shows "Nothing to Plan".
- **Decks empty state:** each empty deck shows its badge, a dashed drop area and a `ContentUnavailableView` (No Track, Load Track…); dragging a track over a deck outlines it in orange.
- **rekordbox Import never runs a file panel by itself.** Import… always opens the import sheet: with no library file chosen it asks for one (Choose File…), otherwise it reads the chosen file, and the preview offers Choose Another File….
- **Hardware** lists controllers (a "Network" or "Lights" subtitle when it applies), **Network MIDI** (the network sessions turned on in Audio MIDI Setup, which owns network MIDI on the Mac, and **Open Audio MIDI Setup**), DJ Players (status, Connect, settings) and a small MIDI monitor; DJ Players is also a Decks toolbar popover. The Controllers and Network MIDI sections are shared with the iPad (`DJControllersSection`, `NetworkMIDISection`).
- **Card hover:** lift to about 1.02, a soft shadow, a 28% scrim, a glass Play button at the bottom-left and a glass "•••" at the bottom-right. The "•••" menu is the same as the context menu. Keyboard focus shows the same controls.
- Full keyboard access works everywhere.
- **Extensions match iOS** (2026-11-09). The Mac embeds a widget extension (`SoundingTV-macOS-Widget`) and a Share extension (`SoundingTV-macOS-Share`) built from the iOS sources. Widgets: Now Playing and Recently Played in small and medium for the desktop and Notification Center, Play or Pause and Next as App Intent buttons, taps open the Mac app; Play or Pause and Resume as Control Center and menu bar controls. No accessory families and no Live Activity. The Share extension resolves the SoundCloud link with the same views as iOS, titled "Play in Sounding", and hands it to the Mac app (Play Now, Play Next, Play Last); the app plays it on the current output. Without a session it still offers the three actions under the bare link. Widgets read the snapshot from the `group.red.forbes.sounding` app group, which the Mac app must have. Large and extra large widgets (Play, Now Playing, Recently Played) and the Next Track control are the same sources as iOS (2026-11-12).

### Apple TV (tvOS 27)
- **Like Music on tvOS 27, and the same product as the iPad** (owner, 2026-11-09: "go through the Apple TV app and make sure it aligns to the design language of the iPad and the tvOS 27 Apple Music app"). Same page structures, names, copy, accent, line art and glass controls as the iPad; the layout and motion of Music on tvOS 27.
- **Tabs:** the floating Liquid Glass tab bar at the top centre, as in Music (`.tabViewStyle(.tabBarOnly)`); it gets out of the way as focus moves into content. There is no app sidebar, sidebar header or toolbar status. Home, Search, Library, Social, Decks, Stations, Now Playing, Settings. Library shelves: Your Music, Recently Added, History, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Sets, Setlists, Downloads, Mixes, with a Likes tile pinned first in the Playlists shelf and the Playlists grid, opening the Likes grid (Albums and Artists from the shared `LibraryCollectionsModel` helpers; Artists are your own files' artists). Social is a row of pills, Following, Followers, Profile and Activity, over the chosen page. Decks is a tab; Menu there goes to the tab bar.
- **Tabs:** the floating Liquid Glass tab bar at the top centre, as in Music (`.tabViewStyle(.tabBarOnly)`); it gets out of the way as focus moves into content. There is no app sidebar, sidebar header or toolbar status. Search (the magnifying glass alone, first), Home, Library, Decks, Stations, Now Playing, Settings (titled with the gear, last: tvOS drops a tab labelled with only an image, and drops a gear glyph inside a `Text` too, measured on build 1204), so the bar fits without scrolling; no Social tab, as on iPhone. One `TabView` for signed in and device-music-only (tabs are added or removed inside it, never a second bar), and tab switches are not animated by the app. Every tab except Now Playing starts its content at the safe-area top (which includes the bar) plus `SCBrowseMetrics.contentTopInset`. Now Playing is full screen like Music (owner 2026-11-12, "now playing still looks too far" from the website mockup): the tab bar hides while focus is on the page, the head starts 96 pt from the top, Menu or Up from the top row brings the bar back over the page, and it hides again once focus has been to the bar and come back. The transport has no captions, the title is `.title3` bold, and comments under the tape are plain dots grouped every 96 pt, bigger and brighter with more comments, never count pills; backgrounds may bleed full screen. Behind every tab is `BrowseBackdrop`, an opaque near-black gradient, never the system's clear default. Now Playing's round buttons are flat translucent circles (white 14%, a 10% hairline, 30% when on), as on the website mockup, not Liquid Glass; a panel's scrim reaches the top of the screen and the page behind it dims in place without sliding, with Up Next hidden under the panel. The idle clock lines its cap height up with the top of the artwork. Every Now Playing full-screen cover (Setlist, Cue Points, Comments, Behind the Track) sits on `BrowseBackdrop` in the dark scheme through `npCover()`, never see-through over Now Playing. **Cue Points and Comments covers** (2026-11-12, owner unhappy with the cue points page: tiny type, empty pads, dead black, technical prose, a floating ✕) take Now Playing's own finish: Now Playing's backdrop (`NPCoverBackdrop`: the pre-blurred artwork, scrim and a 22% dim) over `BrowseBackdrop`, the page grid with the head 96 pt from the top, the track title as a quiet `.callout` line over the page name in `.title3` bold, and no close button, since Menu closes every cover. Cue Points: the full-width lane (150 pt) with coloured cue caps on the markers and the readout under it; then, scrolling, Mix In and Mix Out as rows (`.headline` title, `.body` caption, the time trailing, labelled flat pills for Keep Suggestion, Set to 2:30, Move to 2:30, Clear); eight hot cue pads A–H in one row, 170 pt tall, an empty pad a quiet `+` that shows the mark time when focused, a set pad tinted with its colour, its letter and dot, the name and time; then Cue Points and Music Analysis; Save Cue Point (with the mark time) and Add Loop as flat pills pinned at the foot. Delete and Clear ask for a second click ("Click Again") instead of an alert. Rows invert to near-white on focus, pads and pills scale and lift, nothing has a ring. Comments: the list starts on the grid under the header (no artwork thumbnail), rows and header share the 80 pt edge, a 1400 pt column, and each comment's Search is a flat round `magnifyingglass` at the column's trailing edge with the label promoted on focus. In the Now Playing Cue Points panel the first row is **All Cue Points** ("3 cue points", or "Hot cues, loops and mix points" when there are none) and captions wrap to two lines.
- **One page grid** (2026-11-12, after the owner called build 1203 "close, but not finished"): every TV page and full-screen cover sits 80 pt from the left and right screen edges (`Space.page`), starts `SCBrowseMetrics.contentTopInset` (24 pt) below the top safe area, and ends 48 pt (`Space.section`) above the bottom edge. Pages ignore only the side and bottom safe areas and pad those edges themselves; the top inset is left to the system so the tab bar is always respected. A page's backdrop is a `.background` that ignores every safe area, so nothing is ever a bare band of `BrowseBackdrop` at the foot of the screen; never wrap a page in a `GeometryReader` to read the safe area (read it with `onGeometryChange`). No fixed point sizes for text: tvOS text styles (`.title2` 57, `.title3` 48, `.headline` 38, `.callout` 31, `.body` 29, `.caption` 25), with `.system(size:)` only for glyphs. Library shelves: Your Music, Recently Added, Albums, Artists, Playlists, Sets, Setlists, Downloads, Mixes, with a Likes tile pinned first in the Playlists shelf and the Playlists grid, opening the Likes grid (Albums and Artists from the shared `LibraryCollectionsModel` helpers; Artists are your own files' artists). The Library sidebar opens with the **account menu**, a glass pill with your avatar and name (an orange dot for unread activity): Profile and Activity, then Following and Followers, each pushed inside Library; your own profile opened anywhere lands there. Track lists in Library have **Show Columns** in Sort & Filter (off: the artwork grid; on: one-line rows with the first two layout columns), not Roomy/Dense. Decks is a tab; Menu there goes to the tab bar.
- **Library at launch** paints whatever the library database already holds at once. Until the first library sync run of the session has finished and the database has been re-read, empty shelves show their shaped loading placeholders, never "Your Library Is Empty"; a failed first run with nothing cached is a centred Couldn't Load with Try Again.
- **Mixes** is the last Library shelf (saved sets, then saved mixes, then recent transitions, each tile both artworks with the name and move) and its header opens the shared Mixes page; a tile opens the transition or set page with Play This Mix. Mixes is read-only on the TV and no longer sits in Settings. **Tonight…** in the Decks Auto Mix menu opens the same Tonight section as Your Moves.
- **Home:** a full-bleed hero carousel at the top over the drifting artwork backdrop (`CollectionArtworkBackdrop`), with the artwork mesh (`CollectionPageMesh`) of the page on show behind the whole of Home. Pages: Continue From first when offered (Play, Not Now), then what's playing here (**Play** while it's paused, which starts it and opens Now Playing, or **Now Playing** while it plays; then Start Auto Mix or Auto Mix On), then up to five Top Picks (Play, plus Go to Album or Go to Playlist, or Behind the Track for a track); each page has the eyebrow, the title huge, the artist or curator, glass pills and the sharp 500 pt cover on the right; left and right move between pages and dots show where you are; focus enters on the first page's Play. Then shelves: bold 38 pt white headers ("Title ›" only when the header opens a page), 300 pt square tiles with the title and subtitle under them, wide 16:9 cards for featured collections (blurred cover, sharp cover, eyebrow, title, curator), round artist tiles. Shelves scroll edge to edge and rest on the 80 pt margin; 72 pt between shelves.
- **Glass pills:** hero and page buttons (Play, Shuffle, •••, Go to…) use `TVGlassPillStyle`: Liquid Glass at rest, the prominent one tinted with the page's accent wash; focused, near-white with a black label, 1.08 scale and a soft shadow.
- **Search:** the system search field and keyboard at the top; Recently Searched as glass capsules; **Browse** (the iPad's name) as large line-art genre tiles; results as shelves with the same headers.
- **Track actions:** long-press on any track row or tile (Library, playlists, artist pages, Home and Search) shows **Add to Playlist…** and **Edit Start & End…**; the Now Playing More pane has both too. Add to Playlist is a full-screen focusable list of your playlists under a New Playlist field (Create and Add), on the shared `AddToPlaylistModel`, with an "Added to …" glass toast. Start & End is a full-screen editor on the shared `TrackTrimStore`: the waveform with the cut parts dimmed, the Start and End pills (click one, then swipe or press left and right, click or Menu when done), Preview Start, Preview End, Suggest, Reset and Save.
- **Now Playing tape:** tracks and sets of 6 minutes or more scrub as a tape, like iPhone, iPad and Mac: the shared `SCWaveformRenderer` in centred mode (4-minute window, `SCWaveformStyle.tv`) moves under a fixed playhead. Swipe on the touch surface or press left and right (15 s) to move it, click to play from there, Menu to cancel; it lands by itself after 6 s. Shorter tracks keep the whole-track lane, where a click during a scrub also commits it. The lane spans the page grid (1760 pt). Above it, a **label row** (`NPTapeLabels`): tracklist entries as "Title · Artist" with a thin tick at their boundary, the current one pinned to the leading edge on the tape and pushed off by the next; each label gets the room up to the next tick less a 28 pt gap and truncates with an ellipsis inside it (never clipped mid-glyph, never under its neighbour); a label with under 60 pt of room is left out. Below it, a **dot row** (`NPTapeDots`): tracklist boundaries as 12 pt dots in the artwork accent, timed comments as 10 pt white dots bucketed every 28 pt with a small count capsule ("3") where they would overlap; no marker lines inside the lane. Then the time row: elapsed, the **mini-map skimmer** (owner 2026-11-12: the iPhone and iPad skimmer on the TV too; `NPTapeMiniMap`, the whole track as a 22 pt silhouette with the played part bright and a rounded thumb showing the tape's window, moving with the tape and with every scrub) and remaining; short tracks keep the scrub hint there instead. The passing comment capsule grows out of the dot row, hugs its text (one line, no stretched pill) and the mini-map fades while it shows. The round buttons share one 30 pt glyph size and one 14% fill (Liked shows as the filled heart only); focus is a 1.08 scale with a calm spring and the button's name fades in on its own 30 pt row under the buttons, which the layout reserves, so it never touches the entry line. The layout matches the website's TV mockup (`docs/tv/index.html`, `.k-np`): source line, title, tinted artist, round glass buttons, current entry line; Up Next (84 pt artwork rows) top right; the full-width tape; the transport centred under it.
- **Focus:** content scales and lifts (1.15 tile, 1.08 card, 1.05 hero) with a top-lit sheen and a soft shadow, and the label is promoted. There's **no focus ring of any colour.**
- Focused chrome inverts to a **near-white fill with a black label**.
- Glass is reserved for navigation and chrome.
- The app is dark by design.
- Settings rows are stock. Settings shows the app icon beside the category list and, on a category's page, that category's glyph large on the left, as tvOS Settings does.
- Panels slide in from the right.
- **Now Playing and the tab bar** (owner on build 1206: "why is title STILL clipping?"). Now Playing respects the top safe area like every tab and reads it with `onGeometryChange`; its content starts `max(96, safe top + 24)` from the screen top. While the bar is visible (focus on it, or Menu brought it back) that puts the source line, title and Up Next 24 pt under the bar; when focus enters the page the bar hides and the content settles to the mockup's 96 pt, in one 0.45 s smooth animation (none under Reduce Motion). Nothing on the page is ever drawn under the bar, in any state: short or two-line titles, sets with tracklists, panels open, lyrics, idle. The side panels and lyrics screen take the same inset.
- An idle ambient state appears after inactivity, only while focus is on the Now Playing page (never while it's on the tab bar); its artwork starts at the same top inset as the controls, so the crossfade never moves the page, because **idle hides the tab bar** (`toolbarVisibility(.hidden, for: .tabBar)`): the ambient screen is artwork, title and artist centred under it, and the clock top right on the page grid (80 pt in, level with the artwork's top), never a tab bar over it or a clock floating on its own. Focus moves to the ambient layer first and the controls are disabled a beat later, so the focus engine never falls back to the tab bar. Its background is Now Playing's own (`NPBackdrop` with `NPLegibilityScrim`: the pre-blurred artwork drifting in Core Animation, cross-fading on track change), so the two never differ and nothing is blurred live; still under Reduce Motion.
- **Brand moments carry the same welcome as iPhone and Mac** (owner, 2026-11-09: "tvos needs to use the same branding. this is not good."). The art, headline and tagline come from one shared view, `SoundingWelcome.swift` (`SoundingWelcomeHero`, `SoundingWelcomeTitle`, "Plug in. Turn it up." and "Your SoundCloud on Apple TV, iPhone and Mac."), used by the iPhone welcome, the Mac welcome sheet and both TV screens below.
- **Sign-in screen** (the signed-out start, and sign-in or Add a Device from anywhere; redesigned 2026-11-09 after the owner called it "very ugly"): it follows the system appearance (dark on most TVs, the light palette in light mode) with **one calm layer** behind: the brand line art at about a third of its strength, no colour masses, a wash in the middle, still under Reduce Motion. **The code plate is the one focal point.** From the top: the headline and tagline, the QR code on a white plate (white in both appearances), then **one quiet line** that changes with the state: "Scan with your phone's camera, or open Sounding on a device nearby.", "<name> is nearby: open Sounding on it to sign in.", "Connected. Finish on your device.", "Signing you in…", with "New code in 42s" after it for the last minute (the code renews itself). Then a single **Play Sound**, a secondary Liquid Glass capsule (plus **Sign in with Another Device** where the device picker exists, and **Try Again** only after a failure), and **Use Without SoundCloud** as quiet text centred under the controls, inside the layout so it's never clipped (it used to sit in the top-right corner). No two-row explainer, "or" divider, footer line, expiry bar or Get a New Code. Chrome here is glass at rest; focused, it fills with the primary ink (near-white with a near-black label in dark, umber with a cream label in light), scales to about 1.06 and lifts on a soft shadow, never a ring. Faults and the Mac numeric code replace the plate in place. **Every QR code is the shared liquid code** (`Shared/UI/Components/SoundingQRCode.swift`, `QRCodeView`): vector modules that merge into rounded shapes with concave fillets where they join, squircle finder eyes with an ember pupil, a round clear space for the Sounding mark with a thin ring, and a near-black to deep-ember ink (never below 4.5:1 on white; the pupil and gradient end are the dark ember, not the full brand orange, because orange on white reads as a light module to scanners). Error correction stays H, the quiet zone stays 4 modules, and it never inverts. It ripples out from the centre once when a code appears or renews, and not at all under Reduce Motion.
- **The chime plays only when asked** (owner, 2026-11-09: "lets not play the ultrasonic sound all the time"). Nothing sounds when the screen opens: no beep, pulse, pings, carrier loop or automatic replay. **Play Sound** plays the coded pulse with the ultrasonic carrier under it (+6 dB) for the pulse plus two carrier frames, about 13 s, then stops; the handshake renders off the main thread. `LinkServer.requestChime()` plays it when a nearby iPhone, iPad or Mac asks with `chime_play_request` over the Link offer (at most once every 10 s, inside the hello budget; §9 Nearby Apple TV card). While a device is offering the card, the line under the plate reads "<name> is nearby: open Sounding on it to sign in." (its own name, and its own symbol when the advert carries the device class, else the neutral one) instead of the scan instruction. While it plays, coloured sound rings (`SoundingSoundRings`, shared) swell left and right of the plate in time with the pulse and the plate breathes with it; the backdrop reacts as before. At rest the rings are hidden.
- **Signed out, Library** is a branded page: the shared hero art (dark palette) full-bleed to the top edge and fading into the page, the headline and tagline, **Sign In to SoundCloud** (prominent, brand orange) and **Link a Device**, then Your Music if any device offers it, then Playlists, Sets and Following shelves. Each shelf has its header (opening the shared sign-in prompt) and a line-art tile that starts sign-in, beside the prompt and one line from the shared gate. The first shelf fits on screen under the hero.
- **Sign-in screen** (the signed-out start, and sign-in or Add a Device from anywhere; redesigned 2026-11-09 after the owner called it "very ugly"): it follows the system appearance (dark on most TVs, the light palette in light mode) with a soft copper-and-amber sound field behind the cable: three radial gradients breathe over 11–15 s and drift over 16–22 s, behind three restrained jack contours with a small floor shadow and 3 pt of vertical float. No concentric rainbow arcs. Core Animation animates only opacity and transforms, capped at 30 fps; no per-frame SwiftUI timeline or path rebuilding. The background is still under Reduce Motion, inactive or while connecting. **The code plate is the one focal point.** From the top: the headline and tagline, the QR code on a white plate (white in both appearances), then **one quiet line** that changes with the state: "Scan with your phone's camera, or open Sounding on a device nearby.", "<name> is nearby: open Sounding on it to sign in.", "Connected. Finish on your device.", "Signing you in…", with **New code in 42s** on its own centred, 28 pt secondary caption below the status during the last minute (the code renews itself). That caption reserves its height when hidden, so the plate and buttons never jump; use monospaced digits. The title-to-plate gap is 32 pt; plate-to-status and caption-to-controls gaps are 24 pt, status-to-caption 8 pt, controls-to-secondary action 16 pt. Then a single **Play Sound**, a secondary Liquid Glass capsule (plus **Sign in with Another Device** where the device picker exists, and **Try Again** only after a failure), and **Use Without SoundCloud** as quiet text centred under the controls, inside the layout so it's never clipped (it used to sit in the top-right corner). No second instructional paragraph, "or" divider, expiry bar or Get a New Code. The countdown is a separate caption, not part of the status sentence. Chrome here is glass at rest; focused, it fills with the primary ink (near-white with a near-black label in dark, umber with a cream label in light), scales to about 1.06 and lifts on a soft shadow, never a ring. Faults and the Mac numeric code replace the plate in place. **Every QR code is the shared liquid code** (`Shared/UI/Components/SoundingQRCode.swift`, `QRCodeView`): vector modules that merge into rounded shapes with concave fillets where they join, squircle finder eyes with an ember pupil, a round clear space for the Sounding mark with a thin ring, and a near-black to deep-ember ink (never below 4.5:1 on white; the pupil and gradient end are the dark ember, not the full brand orange, because orange on white reads as a light module to scanners). Error correction stays H, the quiet zone stays 4 modules, and it never inverts. It ripples out from the centre once when a code appears or renews, and not at all under Reduce Motion.
- **Link progress replaces the sign-in choices** (owner, 2026-11-09). While a QR connection, numeric/chime exchange, approved credential handoff or linked-device credential adoption is active, show only a centred `ProgressView` with **Connecting to <device>…** (or **Connecting…** until the name is known), using `ContentUnavailableView` layout and orange tint. Hide the title, QR/code plate, countdown and sign-in actions. The server holds progress across the normal offer-socket close and subsequent authenticated connection; failure or a bounded timeout restores the choices. An outstanding **Allow** confirmation still shows its numeric code, because the person must compare it; same-owner proof never flashes that code. Numeric codes are centred on the content axis as one monospaced `000 - 000` text run, with equal thin spaces around a baseline hyphen and no tracking or separate digit boxes. Alert codes get their own centred line too.
- **TV accounts on the TV** (owner, 2026-11-12). The sign-in screen's done subtitle is "Signed in as Alex.". The Allow alert says "It's signed in to the same SoundCloud account. Only allow it if <device> shows this code." or, for another account, "Allow “<device>” to control this Apple TV?" / "It's signed in to a different SoundCloud account. Only allow it if <device> shows this code.". Linking never changes a signed-in TV's account, and Add a Device on a signed-in TV is control-only. A phone asking to switch gets a native alert, "Switch to Alex's SoundCloud?", **Switch** and **Keep Sam**, with focus on Keep Sam; then a centred "Switching to Alex…" (`ContentUnavailableView`, orange tint). The old account is signed out as on Sign Out, Links kept, and only after the new pair validates. A controller on another account gets a non-focusable bottom capsule, "<device> is connected · Alex", 4 s, once per connection; Remotes & Devices adds "Signed in as Alex" under it. Same-account devices show no account line.
- **The chime plays only when asked** (owner, 2026-11-09: the old sound was "horrifying"; supersedes every Omen, ominous, bass, veil and no-pitched-notes rule below). Nothing sounds when the screen opens, and there are no automatic replays or waiting pings. **Play Sound** plays an original, warm C4–E4–G4 chime: 1.45 s, notes 140 ms apart, a 35 ms raised-cosine attack, smooth exponential tails, only a faint second harmonic, mono-compatible stereo and a 0.16 peak (about −16 dBFS). Success uses E4–G4–C5 over 1.15 s at the same level. `ChimeSynth.renderWelcomeTone` synthesises both in Swift; the TV no longer emits the old bass bed or audible code texture. The existing ultrasonic carrier carries the full code at its existing boosted level for the chime plus two complete carrier frames, about 11 s, then fades out. This preserves the carrier protocol and old-device decoding; speakers that filter ultrasound need the existing QR/numeric fallback. `LinkServer.requestChime()` also plays it when a nearby device asks, at most once every 10 s. Audio render callbacks remain created in `nonisolated static` functions. **Visual feedback:** one soft orange halo expands gently from the plate and fades over the chime; the QR modules and plate never scale or pump. Reduce Motion gets only the soft glow fade. No beat analysis, cable surge, flashes, wobble or coloured rings.
- **Signed out, Library** is a branded page: the shared hero art (dark palette) full-bleed to the top edge and fading into the page, the headline and tagline, **Sign In to SoundCloud** (prominent, the sign-in blue) and **Link a Device**, then Your Music if any device offers it, then Playlists, Sets and Following shelves. Each shelf has its header (opening the shared sign-in prompt) and a line-art tile that starts sign-in, beside the prompt and one line from the shared gate. The first shelf fits on screen under the hero.
- **No focus traps.** Every screen, sheet, panel and every loading, empty and error state has something focusable, and Menu always goes back one level, ending at the tab bar. A state with no action is a focusable `StatusPanel` or uses `.remoteFocusTarget()`; controls are never `.disabled` when they are the only thing on the page (Play stays focusable while tracks load). `onExitCommand` is attached only while there is something to undo (a waveform scrub), never as a no-op. Library loads every shelf's first page up front, so no shelf sits below a row focus can't leave. `TVNavigationTests` checks this on the real TV.
- **Back goes up one level, and leaves the app only from the tab bar** (owner, 2026-11-12: "pressing back in places closes the app too, not going back to menu or whatever"). On the Siri Remote, Menu pops a pushed page, dismisses a sheet, cover, alert or dialog, closes a Now Playing panel (Cues and Sleep Timer go back to More first), closes Lyrics, wakes the idle and ambient screens, closes the Decks picker or show mode, clears a Browse-tile search back to Browse, and from a tab's root content moves focus up to the tab bar; only Menu on the tab bar itself goes to the Home Screen, as in Music. The system handles pushes, presentations and the tab bar; the app only adds `onExitCommand` where it has its own state to undo, with `nil` otherwise so the press falls through, never a no-op handler. **Focus is never left parked on the tab bar after the app itself navigated:** anything that selects a tab in code (playing a track, Browse Music, `LibraryRouter`) must put focus into the page, because Menu on a focused tab bar is "leave the app". `tvEntersFocus(_:_:when:)` (`Apps/tvOS/Design/TVBackRules.swift`) does this for Now Playing when it was opened by playback (`tabBeforeNowPlaying`), so Menu then returns to the tab you came from; Home, Search, Library and Settings take focus on appear. When a Now Playing full-screen cover closes, focus goes back to the control that opened it, never to the bar. `TVBackTests` presses Menu on every surface through `XCUIRemote` and checks the app is still in the foreground.
- **Album, playlist and set pages** (shared `CollectionHeroPage` built from `CollectionPageModel` with the shared `CollectionHero` and `SCTrackListRow`, TV wrapper `PlaylistDetailView`; own-file albums and artists, `TVLocalCollectionView`, use the same `CollectionColumnPage` with the release footer): the drifting artwork backdrop runs full width behind the top of the page and fades into the artwork mesh behind the rest (§3 Collection backdrop); in front, the sharp artwork (460 pt) with the title in large type, the artist, one line of metadata ("Album · 12 tracks · 54 min · 2024") and up to two lines of description; then **Play** (focus enters here) and **Shuffle** as chrome pills, Download and •••. Tracks follow in one list: number (a moving `waveform` when it's the playing track), title, artist when it differs from the album's, BPM · key, duration. Focused rows invert to near-white with a black label and lift slightly; tracks that can't play stay focusable, dimmed with a lock, so scrolling never skips. Under the tracks, as in Apple Music (every platform, §3 Collection footer): the quiet release footer, the artist, then **More by <artist>** and **You Might Also Like** shelves (each hidden when empty). The resting Play pill is filled with the artwork accent.
- **Artist and profile pages** use the shared `PersonHero`: the banner (or the avatar as the pre-blurred drifting artwork backdrop with the person's line art, or line art) full width across the top 45% of the screen, fading at its foot into the artwork mesh of the banner's colours (the avatar's when there is no banner) that fills the rest of the page, a capsule above the name only with real data (Verified, Following, or the follower count), the name huge and bold at the bottom left, then a round Play (focus enters here) and Shuffle. Top Tracks are the most played.
- **Decks** (`DJModeView` and `Features/Decks/`, a living-room DJ show that people watch and one person steers; logic shared with iPhone, iPad and Mac in `DecksLiveModel`, `DeckNextPicksModel` and `DecksBackdropMesh` so the platforms don't drift): the artwork mesh backdrop blends from the on-air deck's palette to the incoming one's as the mix runs. Top bar: the **Auto Mix** menu (on/off, Style, Tonight…, Session…, Decks Guide, Close Decks), a phone chip in the middle ("Alex's iPhone is connected", or "Mixing from Alex's iPhone" while it holds controls) and the **Energy** menu (Raise, Lower, Follow the Night; its label is the energy curve with the level). Two deck heads, readable from 3 m: 248 pt artwork, role (On Air, Paused, Up Next with a countdown, Mixing In, Loading, Couldn't Load), title, artist, BPM at the playing tempo ("~124" while estimated, never a "(est.)" caption), Key, and a bar · phrase counter with four beat pips on the on-air deck (Energy on the next one); a stat with no value is left out rather than shown as "…" or a default, and a deck with no analysis at all says "Analysing…" with a spinner or the Apple TV line "BPM and key come from your iPhone, iPad or Mac." (`AnalysisStatusCopy.gate(.unsupported, device: .tv)`); the stage sits on the page grid; the heads swap places when a mix lands. Under them two full-width beat-aligned Metal lanes (32 beats each, so the grids line up when synced; scrolling on the GPU, no per-frame SwiftUI work) with playheads, the mix window, the entry cue and any loop; between the lanes the shared Auto Mix transition glyphs and crossfader, or "Decks Can't Mix Right Now" with Try Again. At the bottom a glass control strip: Play/Pause, **Mix Now**, Extend, **Next…**, **Moves** (Next Mix: Let Auto Mix Choose or a named move; Now: Swap the Bass, Loop 4 Bars, Loop 1 Bar, Exit Loop, Skip) and Hand Back while the TV holds controls. Remote: focus enters on Mix Now (Play/Pause when it can't mix); a horizontal swipe on a focused lane sweeps the filter (left low-pass, right high-pass) and springs back on release; while paused it jogs the on-air deck by beats; click on the on-air lane plays or pauses, on the next lane mixes now (or opens Next… when nothing is queued); a long press holds the loops. **Next…** is a right-hand glass panel ranked by key and tempo against the playing track: Mixes Well, Up Next, Likes. **Show mode:** after 15 s of no input while playing, the top-bar menus and the control strip fade out; any button brings them back. The screensaver never covers Decks. Empty: "Nothing on the Decks" with Choose a Track… and Start Auto Mix…. An empty deck shows a quiet well with the Decks glyph, never generated art. Control strip pills carry their glass on the label itself (as `TVGlassPillStyle` does), and an unavailable control dims its label only. Menu closes the picker or leaves show mode first; from the tab it goes to the sidebar, from Now Playing it closes Decks. **Decks open only when asked** (owner, 2026-11-11: Play opened "this horrible screen"): from the Decks tab or Decks in the Now Playing More panel (`TVDecksEntry`). Play, Home and Now Playing always open the normal Now Playing, whatever Auto Mix or a past Decks visit left behind; Home's "Auto Mix On" means Auto Mix, not an armed decks session.
- **Top Shelf** (owner, 2026-11-09: "can we make the top shelf 10/10?"; layout in `Core/TopShelf/TopShelfLayout.swift`, shared by the extension, the app and the tests). Square shelves like Music on tvOS, not a carousel: SoundCloud artwork is square, and a full-bleed hero would crop and blur it.
  - **Shelves, in this order:** **Continue Listening** (sets and long tracks, 10 minutes or more, stopped between 2% and 95% in the last 14 days, at most 6, with a progress bar), **Recently Played** (the rest; a partly played track keeps its bar), **Your Likes** (newest first, read from the library database, no request), **Mixes For You** (Home's mixes and stations). At most 20 tiles a shelf, no tile twice.
  - **Titles:** "Title · Artist"; a set of 20 minutes or more adds its length ("· 1 hr, 12 min"); Continue Listening says what's left ("Title · 48 min left"). No "Played 2 h ago": the system keeps the shelf until the app says it changed, so relative times would go stale. Strings are `String(localized:)`.
  - **Actions:** select and Play both play the item at once, queued with the rest of its shelf (owner, 2026-11-09: keep "select plays"). A Continue Listening tile resumes where it stopped. Cold launches hold the action until the sign-in is ready and the saved session has restored, then run it; tracks resolve from Recently Played and the library database first, the API only for what's missing.
  - **Artwork:** the app saves each tile's art as an 800 px JPEG in the app group (the largest variant SoundCloud has, falling back to `t500x500`), so the shelf is sharp at 4K and still shows offline; until then the tile uses the remote `t500x500`. No art: seeded line art. The extension never makes a request; it only reads the snapshot.
  - **Freshness:** the app tells tvOS the shelf changed when Recently Played, a Like or Unlike, Home's mixes, or playback (pause, track change, going to the background) changes it, debounced to 1.5 s, and only when the content really changed.
  - **Signed out, nothing yet, or no snapshot:** one full-bleed carousel tile of the flat copper-jack photo (the Top Shelf Image cropped to 16:9, jack on the right so the system text sits on the felt), with "Sign In", "Open Sounding" or "Nothing Here Yet".

### Apple Watch (watchOS 27)
- `List`, toolbar, `.glass`, and text styles. No global tint.
- Now Playing: an artwork-tinted background and a progress ring around a glass play button, with glass Previous and Next in one `GlassEffectContainer`. A glass Like heart sits beside the title (under it at accessibility sizes). For a set with a tracklist, a caption line names the track playing in the mix and opens the tracklist; the Watch has no Lyrics, so mixes need nothing hidden. While Auto Mix has a plan, a glass plate under the title says "Mix into <Title>" with the move and countdown, then the phase. At large Dynamic Type sizes (xxLarge and up) the bottom bar goes away and Output, Up Next and More become buttons below the transport inside the scroll, so nothing ever overlaps.
- The Digital Crown on Now Playing sets Sounding's own volume whenever the Output reports one (a TV or other remote Output, or This Watch), with a brief glass volume indicator at the top; when the Output is the iPhone, which has no app volume, the crown seeks instead.
- **On This Watch** is music the iPhone sent with Send to Watch, played by the Watch's own engine over AirPods or other Bluetooth audio. It is the first Library section and a root-list row, and the app opens there when the iPhone isn't reachable. Output starts with a glass **iPhone / This Watch** switch (the selected side prominent); the empty Now Playing shows it too. Downloads lists what's on the watch with swipe to remove and "1.2 GB of 2 GB"; the limit is set on iPhone (Settings › Downloads › Send to Watch).
- **Continue From** on the Watch comes over the watch link: the iPhone adds the current `ContinueFrom` offer to the application context as `WatchContinueOffer` (`WatchLinkKey.continueOffer`, empty data when there is none) and handles `continueFrom` (accept and play) and `continueDismiss` (Not Now) commands that carry the offer id. The Watch shows it as a card above everything on Listen Now and the root list: artwork, "From <device>", title, artist and position, a progress bar, glass Play and a Not Now button. It never shows while the Watch's own now-playing is playing or when the offer is over 12 hours old, and it only appears while the iPhone app has published an offer.
- Lists use 40 pt artwork rows; Play and Shuffle are glass buttons; loading, empty and error states are centred `ContentUnavailableView`s (`WatchLoadingState`, `WatchErrorState`), with Try Again on errors.
- Up Next lists the phone's real queue (the next 20, tap to jump), sent over the watch link as `WatchQueueSnapshot`, headed "Playing from <source>".
- Output lists every Output by name (iPhone, Living Room, Bedroom; unreachable TVs say Not Reachable) with a Volume slider above them (the crown adjusts it). The volume is Sounding's own app volume on whichever device is the Output, never the system volume.
- Artist pages have Play and Shuffle above Popular, like playlists.
- Match My Workout says so when Health access is denied (footer plus an alert), and the toggle stays off.
- The **watch widget** (`SoundingTV-watchOS-Widget`, `red.forbes.sounding.watchkitapp.widget`) is a Now Playing complication and Smart Stack widget (circular, rectangular, corner, inline) with play/pause through `SetPlaybackIntent`, plus the play/pause control. It shows text and a progress ring only (no artwork, to stay inside complication budgets), a tertiary-fill Smart Stack background, a filled circle behind the rectangular play/pause, a centred "Nothing Playing" when idle, a redacted sample in the gallery, and flips to finished at the track's end. The watch app writes the same `SoundingSnapshot` the iOS widget reads into the watch's own `group.red.forbes.sounding` container whenever the phone's state changes.
- **Watch Smart Stack and controls** (owner, 2026-11-12): **Listening** is a `RelevanceConfiguration` widget (rectangular) that rises in the Smart Stack when headphones connect, when a workout is active and while a track plays, using the same entry and view as the Now Playing complication. Controls in Control Center and on the Action button: **Play or Pause**, **Next Track** and **Identify Song** (the same sources as the iOS controls; Identify Song opens the watch app on the listening screen). On Now Playing the glass Play and Pause button is the **double tap** target (`.handGestureShortcut(.primaryAction)`).


### CarPlay (iOS 26.5+, owner decision 2026-11-09)
- A CarPlay **audio** app built only from CarPlay templates, run by the iPhone app (`SoundingTV-iOS/Sources/CarPlay/`, scene delegate `SoundingCarPlaySceneDelegate`). Data adapters that other surfaces can use live in `Shared/Surfaces/ListeningCatalog.swift`. It stays dormant until Apple grants `com.apple.developer.carplay-audio`; never add that entitlement to an `.entitlements` file before the App ID has it, because an unprovisioned entitlement breaks signing for every build.
- Audio apps may only use the tab bar, list, grid, Now Playing, alert and action sheet templates. `CPSearchTemplate` and `CPInformationTemplate` are for other app categories and must not be used.
- Tabs: **Listen Now** (the Continue From offer, then Recently Played and the Home For You shelves as image rows; each row title opens the whole shelf), **Library** (Likes, Playlists, Albums, Artists, Downloads, Your Music), **Stations** (Start Station from the playing track, your personal stations, then the stations on Home) and **Search** (the Siri "play" cell plus your recent searches, run against the library only).
- Rows show the title with the artist as the detail text, small artwork from `SCArtworkStore` thumbnails sized to the car screen (at most four loading at once, at most 100 per list), and the playing indicator on the current track. Lists respect `CPListTemplate.maximumItemCount`, with a **Show More** row while more fits. Collections start with **Shuffle**.
- Now Playing is the shared template with **Up Next** (the real queue, tap to jump), the album/artist button (your library's tracks by that artist, plus **Play <artist>** for a SoundCloud artist), and image buttons: Like (heart, SoundCloud tracks only), **Auto Mix** (`infinity`, selected while on) and **Mix Now** (`arrow.triangle.merge`, only while Auto Mix is on). Like and Not for Me are also on `MPRemoteCommandCenter` while CarPlay is connected, never otherwise, so the Lock Screen keeps its skip buttons.
- Playback goes through `PhonePlaybackCoordinator`, so Output is respected: when a linked Apple TV is playing, CarPlay controls the TV like the mini player does.
- Signed out: with your own music, Library shows Albums, Artists and Your Music and one alert says "Sign In on Your iPhone"; with none, the root list says "Sign In on Your iPhone".
- Driving-safe: no comments, lyrics, Decks, Behind the Track or long text. No new SoundCloud traffic for browsing: Home, Library and search read the cached stores; only an explicit tap on an uncached playlist, a station or **Play <artist>** makes a request, as on the iPhone. Previews stay previews.
- **Mini player** (iOS 27): the Now Playing template sets `allowsMiniPlayer`, so other templates show the playing track in the system mini player; before iOS 27 nothing changes. The Now Playing Live Activity's compact layout also shows in CarPlay.

---

## 3. Colour, type and icon

### Copper accent
- `Color.scAccent` = `#FF6B1A` (1.00, 0.42, 0.10).
- **Use it for:** the brand moments (welcome, website), the line-art ink, and occasionally one primary affordance.
- **Never use it for:** eyebrow labels, timestamps or decorative text. It is the app tint everywhere (§2 All platforms), so standard controls follow it through `.tint`; never hand-colour a control blue or orange beyond that. Two exceptions: the active column header of a Show Columns list (owner asked for orange table headers, §2) and the selected search filter pill (below).
- **Search filter pills** (owner, 2026-11-09): the selected pill under the search field (Top Results, Artists, Tracks…) is a filled `Color.scAccent` capsule with its label in `Color.scAccentOn` on iPhone, iPad and Mac (`SearchFilterPills`; white on orange is only 2.9:1). Unselected pills are plain text. No other pill or chip we draw is orange; system controls follow the iPhone and iPad page tint (§2).

### Collection accent
Album, playlist, set, artist and profile pages take one accent colour from their artwork (`CollectionAccent` in `Shared/Design/CollectionAccent.swift`): the dominant vivid colour, found off the main thread and cached per URL, adjusted to at least 4.5:1 contrast for the current appearance. It tints the page's Play and Shuffle (`.glassProminent` and `.glass` on iPhone, iPad and Mac; never `.bordered`) and the playing-row indicator. Grey or missing artwork falls back to the system tint. This is the only place a derived colour, orange included, may tint standard controls; it never leaves the collection page. Artist and profile pages have no accent wash: they take the adaptive background below.

### Collection backdrop (Apple Music adaptive background)
Album, playlist and set pages on iPhone, iPad and Mac (SoundCloud albums and playlists, own-file albums and own-file artists) share one implementation in `CollectionBackdrop.swift`: `.collectionPage(artworkURL:)` on the page, `CollectionHero` with `CollectionHeroMetrics.phoneFullBleed`, `.padFullBleed` or `.macFullBleed`, and `.tracksCollectionOverscroll()` on the scroll view.
- **The top of the page is the artwork, full bleed** (`CollectionFullBleedArtwork`; owner, 2026-11-09: "it covers the entire screen, not just a corner"): the cover itself, edge to edge, as tall as the page is wide (at most 520 pt on iPhone, 560 pt on iPad, 460 pt on Mac), running under the status bar and the navigation bar or toolbar with the scroll-edge effect off while it shows. `.backgroundExtensionEffect()` is on the image only, so it reaches under the iPad sidebar. Where the track list is a `List` (iPhone and iPad), the artwork is never drawn inside a row: it is one layer behind the `List` that follows the scroll offset and the hero's height (`collectionFullBleedBackdrop`, built on `collectionScrollBackdrop`), and the hero row only reports its height. `.backgroundExtensionEffect()` only ever wraps that bare artwork layer, never rows or hero content: inside a list cell it mirrored a track row out under the sidebar (owner: album track list "looks shit and broken", 2026-11-09). The Mac's page is a scroll view, not a `List`, so its hero draws the artwork itself. Its lower half fades into the page colour; the title, artist, metadata and Play/Shuffle sit over the fade (centred on iPhone, leading on iPad and Mac). There is no second, smaller copy of the cover on these pages. Overscroll stretches the artwork from its bottom edge.
- **The whole page is the album's colour** (`CollectionPageTone`, `CollectionToneMesh`): the colour of the cover's bottom edge fills the entire screen behind the scroll view (pinned with `ignoresSafeArea`, never clipped by the scroll), with a slow 3 × 3 `MeshGradient` of the cover's colours over it. Tracks, footer and shelves all sit on it. **Colours are picked vivid, never muddy** (owner, 2026-11-09): grey or near-black cells in colourful art lean halfway to the art's most vivid cell, coloured cells keep at least 45% saturation before fitting so a darkened orange reads as rust rather than brown, the page colour is the bottom edge weighted towards its most vivid cell instead of a flat average of mixed hues, and two diagonal mesh corners sit a step deeper (lighter on a light page) so the mesh has depth; art that is genuinely grey stays grey. Each colour is fitted, not washed out: for a dark page it is darkened only as far as 60% white text needs to reach 4.5:1 (`CollectionBackdropRenderer.legibilityScrim`), for a light page lightened only as far as 60% black text needs; the page takes whichever needs the smaller change, dark winning close calls. Increase Contrast fits to 7:1. The mesh drifts on 23–37 s cycles at 15 fps, pauses when the page is gone or the app is inactive, and is still under Reduce Motion.
- **Artist pages get the same treatment on iPhone, iPad and Mac** (and profile pages, which are the artist page; owner, 2026-11-09: "have a look at just how it looks if you look at an artist… improve how the dynamic or adaptive backgrounds work"). One host layer behind the page (`collectionPersonBackdrop`, `PersonHeroBackdrop` in `CollectionHero.swift`), never drawn inside a list row, full bleed to the top of the window behind the bars, following the scroll, stretching on overscroll and extended under the iPad and Mac sidebars with `.backgroundExtensionEffect()` on that layer only. The image is the banner (sharp on iPad and Mac; on iPhone, where a wide banner would be a soft crop, its pre-blurred drifting backdrop with the round avatar beside the name), else the avatar's pre-blurred drifting backdrop, else line art; no live `.blur`. The name sits over a **measured scrim**: the bottom edge's brightest colour sets how dark the scrim gets for white text at 4.5:1 (7:1 with Increase Contrast), less whatever the drifting backdrop's own scrim already darkened. The image's last fifth fades into the page, and the page is the banner's (else the avatar's) fitted colour and slow mesh (`collectionPersonPage`), always dark, with the content and bars in dark appearance. The flat accent wash and the Mac's own artist backdrop are gone.
- **Content follows the colour**: the page content, the navigation bar or toolbar and the status bar take the tone's colour scheme (light content on dark colours, dark on light), so text, secondary text, separators and controls stay legible with no per-view tinting. The tone is the same in light and dark mode, as in Music. Sheets opened from the page keep the system appearance.
- **Apple TV** (owner, 2026-11-09: "adaptive artwork backgrounds on tvos too") keeps the drifting pre-blurred artwork backdrop at the top of the page, with the item's line art on it, and the page behind it is the same fitted colour and mesh, always dark (`.collectionPageMesh(url:)`). It covers SoundCloud and own-file albums, playlists and sets (`CollectionColumnPage`), Behind the Track, and artist and profile pages (banner colours, else the avatar's). Now Playing and the idle ambient screen have their own single backdrop (§5 Background) and never stack a second one. At 10 feet: the art is blurred once off the main thread and cached (`CollectionBackdropStore`), never a live `.blur` on a large layer; the drift is Core Animation at 15 fps and the mesh redraws at 10 fps, both paused off screen or when the app is inactive, and both still under Reduce Motion. Focus over it stays scale, lift and soft shadow with focused rows and chrome inverting to near-white, never a ring. Stations has no item page on Apple TV (a station plays at once), so its hero card keeps its own mesh.
- **Rows are plain, like Music's album rows**: track number (the playing mark replaces it), title, artist, duration and •••, on the page background with no row fill; rows without columns share the hero's horizontal inset so the numbers line up under the artwork's leading edge, and the hairline separators start at the title.
- No custom cards or materials behind content; glass only on controls and chrome.

### Scroll-driven changes animate
Owner, 2026-11-09: "animate the state changes during scroll of course, this is SwiftUI." On album, playlist and set pages every change driven by scrolling is continuous, never a jump: the hero text fades and settles (to 0.96 scale) as it reaches the bar and the artwork stretches on overscroll, both from `visualEffect` geometry with no state updates; the navigation title fades in from a 0–1 progress (`.collectionHeroProgress`, published only when its tenth changes) and the scroll-edge effect and toolbar title switch inside `withAnimation(.smooth)`. Show Columns, header sorts and the page colour arriving animate with `.smooth`. Reduce Motion drops the scale and keeps the fades.

### Collection footer (under the track list)
Album, playlist and set pages on iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV, once the whole list has loaded (`CollectionFooter.swift`, `CollectionRelatedModel`):
- **Release footer** in quiet secondary text, only the lines that exist: the date with the type word from `set_type` ("EP released 15 May 2026", "Album released …", "Playlist created …"; own-file albums only with a real release date); "4 tracks · 18 minutes" (the Mac leaves the count out because its table footer already says it); the ℗ line (the tracks' publisher ℗ line, else "℗ 2025 <label>" from the playlist's label or the tracks' publisher), plus the label on its own line when the ℗ line doesn't name it; the © line; a Creative Commons licence when it isn't All Rights Reserved. The genre lives in the hero, not here.
- **The uploader** (`SoundCloudUploaderRow`): round avatar (line art when there's none), name with a blue `checkmark.seal.fill` when verified, "47.5K followers · 37 tracks" ("Profile · 120 followers" for someone who doesn't post music), the first line of the bio and a bordered **Follow**/**Following** button (unfollow asks first; hidden for yourself and when signed out; state from the synced Following list). The row opens their page. Apple TV keeps the focusable row without the button.
- **More by <artist>** for artists, **More from <name>** for profiles (their other albums first, then EPs and playlists, never this one) and **You Might Also Like** (tracks related to the album's most-played track) as `CollectionShelf` rows of `ArtworkShelfCard`s.
- **Liked by** and **Reposted by** rows (`CollectionEngagementSection`): an avatar stack of up to five, people you follow first, and "Liked by Sam, Jo and 168 others" (or "Liked by 170 people" when none of them are people you follow). Tapping opens the list (a sheet on iPhone, iPad and Mac; a pushed list on Apple TV) with a "See All on SoundCloud" link when SoundCloud has more than one page. When the people can't be fetched the row shows only the count with the heart or repost symbol and isn't tappable.
- Traffic: each part loads only when scrolled near, one request each (artist, their playlists, related tracks, one page of likers and one of reposters), cached per album for 15 minutes (likers and reposters for 30); the first 429 stops each store until SoundCloud's reset. A shelf that comes back empty is hidden; no empty or error states here.

### Collection hero details
Under the title and uploader on album, playlist and set pages (`CollectionHero.swift`, parts in `SoundCloudDetailSections.swift`):
- **Metadata line**: type word · genre · year for albums, or type word · genre · "Updated 4 months ago" for playlists and sets (the year when it's never been edited), then `heart` and `arrow.2.squarepath` with the like and repost counts. No track count or length here; they're in the footer.
- **Description** clamped to the platform's line count with a **More**/**Less** button only when it's cut off (none on Apple TV). Links and @mentions in it open.
- **Tags** as small bordered capsule buttons ("#techno", `.tint(.primary)`), centred under a centred hero; tapping one searches for it. Apple TV shows them as plain capsules.
- **Counts** everywhere go through `CompactCount` in `User.swift` ("47.5K", "1.2M", "999", "1K").
- **Track rows** (iPhone and iPad, Show Columns off) show `play.fill` + the SoundCloud play count as trailing secondary text: on every list at regular width, and only on album and playlist pages at compact width; never at accessibility sizes, for own files, or when the list is sorted by Plays (the count is already in the subtitle).

### Artist and profile details
Under the hero on artist and profile pages: a Followers · Following · Tracks counts row (Tracks only for artists), the bio with **More**, then the city and country (`mappin.and.ellipse`) and links: the user's SoundCloud web profiles (`users/{urn}/web-profiles`, one request per person, loaded with the page, cached 30 minutes, stopped by a 429), their website and links in the bio, never SoundCloud itself. Links open in the browser; Apple TV shows them as plain labels.

### Now Playing tint
The **artist name** is the single element tinted from the artwork's palette, taken from the most vivid colour of the blurred artwork. Everything else is primary/secondary text over the scrim.

### Line-art palettes (`SoundingArtPalette` in `SoundingLineArt.swift`)
| | Ground | Masses | Inks |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dark** | near-black `0.063 0.051 0.047` | burnt copper `0.42 0.18 0.08`, plum `0.24 0.13 0.22`, deep verdigris `0.09 0.25 0.23` | copper (`scAccent`), brass `0.96 0.75 0.40`, verdigris `0.43 0.80 0.70`, rose copper `0.98 0.60 0.56`, cream `0.95 0.90 0.82` |
| **Light** | paper `0.985 0.962 0.930` | peach `0.99 0.79 0.64`, lilac `0.92 0.82 0.88`, mint `0.77 0.89 0.84` | deep copper `0.86 0.33 0.06`, ochre `0.70 0.50 0.12`, teal `0.08 0.46 0.41`, wine `0.66 0.21 0.24`, umber `0.27 0.18 0.14` |

Signal (pulse highlight): `1.00 0.86 0.62` in dark; copper in light.

### Type
- System fonts only (SF Pro / SF Compact / SF Mono where tabular). Every app string uses **Dynamic Type text styles**: no `.font(.system(size:))`, except decorative glyphs.
- One clear hierarchy per screen. Numbers use `.monospacedDigit()`.
- Text wraps or reflows at accessibility sizes rather than truncating important text. Caps belong only on the transport row and the mini player.

### App icon
- The **photograph** of the copper jack on black felt. It is final.
- The Mac version uses the standard macOS rounded-square shape, with no baked frame.
- Don't redraw it. A vector redesign was tried and rejected on 2026-11-06.
- The large surfaces (Apple TV tile, Top Shelf, App Store marketing) use the photo.
- **Apple TV icon:** the photo, split into real parallax layers so the jack lifts off the felt when the remote tilts. Front is the jack plug, Middle is the SOUNDING wordmark with its shadow, Back is the felt and socket with the jack removed. Layers come from `Branding/Sounding-tvOS-AppTile-Master.png` via `swift tools/make_tvos_icon_layers.swift` (Vision subject lifting, edge decontamination, hole fill). Never redraw it as line art or vector. Top Shelf stays the flat photo.

### Settings icons
- One source: `SettingsIcon` and `SettingsIconTile` in `Shared/Design/SettingsIcon.swift`. Every Settings category row on iPhone, the iPad sidebar and the Mac Settings sidebar draws its tile from there; Apple TV uses the same symbols, untiled (its Settings rows stay stock).
- Tiles copy Apple's Settings: a continuous rounded square (corner 22.5% of the side), the system colour as its `.gradient`, a white filled SF Symbol at medium weight, its point size half the side (it draws at about 70% of the tile, like Apple's). 29 pt on iOS and iPadOS, scaling with Dynamic Type up to 40 pt; 20 pt on the Mac, as in System Settings.
- Each row has its own system colour, as in Apple's Settings, so neighbouring rows never share one. Never orange. Groups are still list sections, in the table's order.

| Group | Rows: colour, symbol |
|---|---|
| SoundCloud account | Account `.blue` `person.fill`, Subscription `.indigo` `star.fill`, Activity `.red` `bell.fill`, Scout `.teal` `binoculars.fill`, Messages `.green` `message.fill` |
| Playback and audio | Playback `.pink` `play.fill`, Auto Mix `.purple` `slider.vertical.3`, Alarms `.indigo` `alarm.fill`, Music Analysis `.cyan` `waveform` |
| Devices and connectivity | Devices `.blue` `tv.and.mediabox.fill`, Home Lighting `.yellow` `lightbulb.fill`, DJ Players `.indigo` `opticaldiscdrive.fill` |
| Storage and system | Downloads `.gray` `arrow.down.circle.fill`, Music Folders `.blue` `folder.fill`, Library `.pink` `music.note.list`, About & Advanced / Advanced `.gray` `gearshape.fill` |

- Never use `appletv.fill` or `hifispeaker.and.appletv.fill` in a tile: their tv wordmark is unreadable at tile size.

---

## 4. The line-art system (the signature)
Source: `SoundingTV.swiftpm/Sources/Shared/Design/SoundingLineArt.swift`, with its web port at `Design/concepts/cable/art.js`. Procedural and seeded; no bitmaps.

### Vocabulary
- A **jack plug** (grip, collar, shaft, two insulator rings, tip) with **4–6 echoing outline contours** that continue into **parallel cable lines**.
- **Sound rings** from the tip.
- A **speaker cone** (concentric circles) or a **wave band**.
- **Three flat colour masses.**
- Original motifs only, built around the jack, cables, rings, cones and wave contours. Never SoundCloud's art.

### Rules
- **Stroke:** one stroke weight per size class, with consistent echo spacing, so it reads as one hand. Use fewer contours at small sizes; it must read at 24 pt.
- **Seeding:** seed from the item id (track, playlist, user, genre). The same item always gets the same art.
- **Light/dark:** use the palette's ground and inks. Now Playing is always dark.
- **Rendering:** draw once off-main to a `CGImage` at display size, then cache it in memory (bounded) and on disk. Never redraw paths per frame in lists.
- **Motion** is allowed only on the welcome screen, the Apple TV sign-in backdrop, the Now Playing background and the website, and always honours Reduce Motion with a static composition.

### Where it's used
- Welcome and sign-in, on iOS, tvOS (QR backdrop) and Mac (the first-launch welcome sheet and signed-out Home).
- Welcome and sign-in, on iOS (and its music step), tvOS (the appearance-aware QR backdrop and the signed-out Library hero) and Mac (the first-launch welcome sheet and signed-out Home).
- Generated covers for tracks, playlists and stations without artwork.
- Placeholders for avatars and artists with no image. SoundCloud's `default_avatar` counts as **no image**.
- Search vibe tiles (Apple TV) and Browse Categories tiles (iPhone, iPad, Mac): a seeded variant per genre. Category tiles screen-blend the line art over the category's own two-stop gradient, with a bold white title bottom-left.
- The Top Shelf placeholder.
- Big empty moments: first-run Library, empty Downloads, empty Setlists.
- The artist hero fallback: banner → blurred avatar → line art.
- The Now Playing background, reacting to the music (§5).
- The artwork backdrop at the top of album, playlist and set pages: ink strokes only, settled and still (§3 Collection backdrop).
- **The website's whole visual language** (§10).

### Where it's not used
Settings, forms, lists of real content, and most empty states (keep them stock).

---

## 5. Now Playing (decided; prototypes in `Design/prototypes/now-playing/`, decisions in `DECISIONS.md`)
- **One calm screen, always dark.** Nothing below the fold.
- **Layout:**
  - big artwork;
  - the title (fades at the trailing edge, no marquee), then the artist (the one tinted element);
  - Like and ••• as glass;
  - one clean waveform scrubber with elapsed and remaining time (the **Tape** for anything 6 minutes or longer, below);
  - bare-glyph transport;
  - volume;
  - the bottom row.
- **Bottom row:** Comments · Output · Up Next. While a mix with a tracklist plays, it becomes **Tracklist** · Output · Up Next, and Comments moves into •••.
- **Modes, not scrolling:** Up Next, Comments, Tracklist. The artwork morphs into a 64 pt header, and the waveform and transport dock at the bottom.
  - The pane switcher is Liquid Glass capsule buttons in a `GlassEffectContainer` at the standard control size, the selected one `.glassProminent` with a white tint and a black label; never a segmented `Picker`. It mirrors the bottom row: Comments (Tracklist during a mix) and Up Next, plus whichever pane ••• opened.
  - Rows in every pane line up with the 24 pt sheet inset.
- **Tape (tracks and sets of 6 minutes or more):** a fixed centre playhead with the waveform scrolling edge to edge under it, fading at the screen edges. About 4 minutes fill the width; pinch from 1 to 15 minutes, with momentum and rubber-banding at the ends.
  - **Label row** above the waveform: tracklist boundaries are thin lines with their titles ("Title · Artist", unknowns as "ID"). The current entry's title is pinned to the leading edge and pushed off by the next one. It replaces the separate mix line, which no longer shows under the title.
  - **Comments** are small markers just below the waveform at their times (owner 2026-11-09: a full-size avatar per comment "is so bloated"): a 10 pt avatar dot (11 pt on the Mac) with a hairline ring, and a small count capsule ("3") where comments would overlap. Markers group into fixed time buckets about two markers wide, so they never overlap and don't regroup as the Tape scrolls. At most one marker per ~4 marker widths shows, the ones nearest the playhead first, and they dim with distance from it. The text appears for one comment at a time: when playback passes it (3.6 s, at most every 7 s) or on pointer hover (iPad and Mac; a cluster shows the nearest comment and "+N"). It grows out of its marker as a plain, quiet capsule (20 pt avatar, black at 30%, no border; comments are content: no glass). Reduce Motion: the capsule fades, no scale.
  - **Time row:** elapsed, a full-length mini-map with a draggable viewport thumb, remaining.
  - **Dragging** grows the waveform about 30% and shows the time as a white glass capsule on the label row (solid white under Reduce Transparency or Increase Contrast). It snaps to tracklist boundaries with a selection tick; a dot under the mini-map shows where the audio really is. Release seeks; a flick carries on and seeks where it lands. Dragging the mini-map jumps the view.
  - **Short tracks** (under 6 minutes) fit the whole waveform to the width with a moving playhead, as before; pinching in switches to the Tape, and pinching back out returns.
  - **Accessibility:** VoiceOver adjusts by 10 s; "Next Track in Mix" and "Previous Track in Mix" are actions. Under Reduce Motion the Tape moves in one-minute steps.
  - **Per platform:** iPhone and iPad bleed the waveform to the screen (iPad: to the panel gutter). The Mac puts the Tape full width along the bottom of the window, with the transport centred under it and volume and Play On trailing; the artwork, title and panes sit above. Apple TV keeps its scrubber.
- **Scrubbing:** audio keeps playing untouched while you drag; the seek happens once, on release. After release the waveform stays at the released position until the player confirms it (3 s at most), and rapid seeks are coalesced. The enlarged waveform pushes the elapsed and remaining times down rather than covering them.
  - Tracklist: "N identified · M unknown", an **Identify This Track** button, the current entry highlighted with its progress, auto-scroll, and tap to seek.
  - Comments follow the playhead, with a compose field at the current time. In the Playing view, at most one comment bubble every 7 s.
  - **Comments from later in the track** (owner 2026-11-09: "let me see comments from future, just show they're from later in song"): the Comments pane lists every timed comment. Past and current ones come first, the current one highlighted for 25 s after it plays. Below them, under a quiet **Later in This Track** header, the rest follow in the same list, each row in the secondary foreground with "at 3:42" and a `forward` glyph in place of the accent-coloured time; nothing else differs. Tapping one seeks, exactly as for past ones. VoiceOver reads "name, later in the track, at 3:42, body". As playback passes a later comment it moves up into the past group with the pane's existing row animation (a plain fade under Reduce Motion). Untimed comments stay out of this pane. iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV share the split in `CommentTimeline` (`Shared/UI/Comments`); the waveform markers already showed every timed comment, past and later. Comments are never fetched more than once per track for this: the pane uses the list already loaded.
- **Lyrics** (LRCLIB, opt-in): a Now Playing pane on iPhone, iPad and Mac (pane tab plus Lyrics in •••), and a full-screen mode on Apple TV from a focusable Lyrics button in the button row, over the Now Playing backdrop.
  - Big bold lines; the current line full white, the rest dimmed and blurred by distance; spring scroll keeps the active line about a third from the top; word-by-word fill where LRCLIB has word timing; three interlude dots for gaps of 8 s or more; tap (or select) a line to seek; scrolling or swiping pauses auto-follow for 4 s (6 s on TV). Lyrics are content: no glass.
  - Unsynced lyrics show statically under "Lyrics not synced". A small "Lyrics from LRCLIB" credit ends the list.
  - States are centred `ContentUnavailableView`s: loading, "No Lyrics for This Track", Instrumental, "No Lyrics for Mixes" (20 minutes or longer), offline and unavailable with Try Again.
  - **Consent:** nothing goes to LRCLIB until the user agrees. First open shows "Show Lyrics from LRCLIB?" with Show Lyrics and Not Now; Not Now leaves "Lyrics Are Off" with Turn On Lyrics and never asks again. The synced setting is **Lyrics from LRCLIB** in Settings › Playback on every platform.
  - Reduce Motion: no blur, instant line changes, static dots.
  - **Translate** (owner, 2026-11-12): a round glass `translate` button at the top trailing corner of the lyrics pane on iPhone, iPad and Mac, never in a menu. It shows only when the lyrics are in a language other than yours and the system can translate it.
