On, it fills white with a black glyph (like the selected pane button) and every line shows its translation under it in a smaller semibold line, keeping the line's timing, tap to seek and word fill; repeated lines translate once. Translation is Apple's on-device Translation framework (the system offers the language download the first time); lyrics still come only from LRCLIB. The choice is remembered on the device. Apple TV and Watch have no Translation framework, so they have no button. Shared code: `LyricsTranslation` (`Core/Lyrics`), `LyricsTranslator` and `LyricsTranslationHost` (`Shared/UI/Lyrics/LyricsTranslationViews.swift`).
- **Behind the Track** (owner 2026-11-09; shared `BehindTheTrackModel`, `BehindTheTrackStore` and `BehindTheTrackContent`) replaces the About pane everywhere. It never sits behind a menu alone:
  - **Placement:** a Now Playing pane on iPhone (a visible tab: Comments or Tracklist, Behind the Track, Lyrics, Up Next), iPad and the Mac inspector; tapping the title or artist block on iPhone, iPad and the Mac full window opens it; the first item in the iPhone and iPad ••• menu and the mini player menu; and "Behind the Track" in every track context menu (Library, Feed, Home, Mac `MacTrackContextMenu`), which opens it as a sheet over whatever is on screen. On Apple TV it is an `info.circle` button in the Now Playing button row after Lyrics (and the More panel row), opening full screen with scale-and-lift focus and no rings.
  - **Content, in order:** artwork, title and the uploader (opens their page); **Liked or reposted by people you follow** as an avatar stack and summary ("Liked by Ana, Ben and 3 others you follow"), three rows, then Show All (tap opens the profile); plays, likes, reposts and comments; the description with links, @mentions and timestamps that seek (playing the track first if it isn't current); on-device generated insights where available; genre and tags as chips that open Search; Details as `LabeledContent` (BPM, Key, Genre, Tracklist with a link to the Tracklist pane, Release, Released, Posted, Label, Publisher, ISRC, Licence, Length, You Last Played It); then Buy (the uploader's link), Free Download on SoundCloud (opens the SoundCloud page, never a direct file, so Go and Go+ tiers still apply) and Open on SoundCloud.
  - **States:** the people section shows a small loading row, then hides itself when nobody you follow engaged, when signed out, for your own files, or when SoundCloud is limiting requests. Nothing else in the view waits on it.
  - Your own play count isn't stored; "You Last Played It" comes from Recently Played instead.
- **••• menu:** Tracklist, Cue Points, Auto Mix, Decks, Share, plus download items.
- **Auto Mix, live:** while Auto Mix has a plan, a compact Liquid Glass capsule sits under the transport on iPhone and Mac (`AutoMixNextMixCapsule`, owner 2026-11-09: the old dark panel with its wedge, tick, blue bar and "Echo out"/"Fade out" labels was "so ugly"). It holds the next track's small artwork; "Mix into **Title**"; the move as a sentence in secondary ("Echo out, then fade", "Loop, fade in, then swap the bass", from `AutoMixLiveState.moveSummary`), or the next cue, or "Your move · …"; a tiny two-lane glyph of the move (outgoing over incoming, echoes as decaying pulses, loops outlined, no colour until it plays); and the countdown ("in 3:59", then "in 8 bars" and beats, ticking on the beat). While mixing it names the phase ("Echoing out *Track*") with "Bar 3 of 16", and the glyph fills as it plays, the incoming lane in the album accent (the only accent). Tap for the plan in a native sheet (popover on the Mac) with Mix Now, Extend 8 Bars and Skip This Track. While a move runs, the waveform becomes a two-deck view: both waveforms beat-aligned and scrolling under a fixed playhead, the loop region outlined, a strip of the move's steps filling bar by bar, the phase named as it happens ("Loop 4 bars", "Bass swap", "Drop", "Riser"), a crossfader, and per-deck H/M/L and filter meters. It collapses back to the waveform when the move lands, and the line says "Mixed into *Track*" for 6 s. Reduce Motion freezes the view at the latest state. Apple TV shows the same line with focusable Mix Now and Extend pills and a 10-foot decks view in place of the waveform; the Mac puts the decks view in place of the scrubber with "Show in Decks" rather than repeating the console, and like the iPhone leaves the move's name and steps to the capsule; the Watch shows one caption line (move and countdown, then the phase).
- **••• menu:** Behind the Track, Tracklist, Cue Points, Auto Mix, Decks, Share, SharePlay, Add to Playlist, Wake to This (iPhone and iPad), Sleep Timer, Lyrics, plus download items.
- **Cue Points (the track map), one implementation** (owner 2026-11-12: "not unified"). `TrackMapModel` (`Shared/ViewModels/TrackMapModel.swift`) holds every rule, action and word (`TrackMapCopy`); `TrackMapSections`, `TrackMapLane`, `TrackMapCueEditor` and the pads (`Shared/UI/Components/TrackMapViews.swift`) draw it. iPhone and iPad (••• › Cue Points, a sheet), Apple TV (More › Cue Points, then Edit Cue Points full screen) and Mac (••• › Cue Points pane in Now Playing, and the Prep inspector and pads) all show the same thing: the waveform with cue marks, loop spans and the phrase strip; **Mix Points** (Mix In, Mix Out: set, suggested or not set); **Hot Cues** A–H (empty pad sets one at the mark, a set pad plays from it); **Cue Points** (your cues and suggestions with Accept, Accept as Hot Cue, Dismiss); **Music Analysis** (Key, Energy, Vocals, Phrases) when the track has it; Save Cue Point at the mark, Add Loop (4 bars), Move to the mark, Edit (Name, Colour, Type: Cue Point, Loop or Hot Cue A–H), Delete, and undo. Cues snap to the beat when the track has a grid. Platform files keep only chrome and input: swipe and context menus on iPhone and iPad, focus, swipe-to-scrub and click on the remote, keys on the Mac (←/→ a beat, Return plays from the mark, M save, L loop, 1–8 hot cues, I/O mix in and out, Delete). A memory cue is called a **Cue Point** everywhere. **Suggestions read as plain English** (owner 2026-11-12, about "Runs hot to the end — short 3s blend at 8:17, bass swap only, no accent"): `TrackMapCopy.suggestionCaption` gives every device the same quiet captions, "Suggested · Auto Mix would come in here", "Suggested · Auto Mix would leave here", "Suggested · Sounds like a drop", "Suggested · Sounds like a breakdown", "Suggested · You often come back here", "Suggested · A loop that fits here", or just "Suggested"; the engine's reasoning stays on the model and is never shown. Unset mix points say "Not set · Auto Mix will pick where to come in" (or "where to leave"). **Live everywhere** (owner 2026-11-12: "manageable on all devices, and integrated with the prep stage on macOS seamlessly"): an open cue screen never goes stale. `CueStore` announces every write by track (`CueStore.changes`, one announcement per sync batch), whoever made it: an edit on this screen or another, iCloud sync (cues, dismissals, prep, listening), a rekordbox import, Auto Mix's mix points, a controller or deck pad. Each `CueBoard` reloads its own track's sheet about 150 ms after the last change, and `TrackMapModel` reloads the track's prep with it, so Mac Prep and the Now Playing Cue Points pane on the same track show each other's edits as they happen, and Apple TV's page and panel stay in step. A reload keeps the selection, the mark and an open editor; a name being typed is kept until it's submitted, and an editor whose cue is deleted elsewhere closes. A screen's own write echoes back as one quiet reload that changes nothing. **Undo** works on every device: Edit › Undo and ⌘Z on the Mac, an Undo button in the iPhone and iPad sheet's toolbar (plus shake and ⌘Z), and an Undo pill beside Save Cue Point and Add Loop on Apple TV once there's something to undo; Apple TV hot cue pads also take Edit, Move and Delete from a long press, like the context menu elsewhere. Prep's menu adds Set Mix In and Set Mix Out.
- **SharePlay (Listen Together):** SharePlay in ••• starts a session for the current track and queue; during one, ••• offers Leave SharePlay and a quiet "Listening Together · 3" line sits under the grabber (iPhone), above the title (iPad), beside Like and ••• (Mac) or under the title (Apple TV). Auto Mix is off for the session ("Off during SharePlay").
- **Sleep Timer:** 15, 30 and 45 minutes, 1 hour, End of Track and, for a mix with a tracklist, End of Mix (End of Track then means the current tracklist entry). The last 30 s fade out, then playback pauses and the volume returns. While it runs, a moon and countdown sit beside Like and •••; on Apple TV under the title; on iPhone the Live Activity counts down too. It runs on the device that plays: with the Apple TV as the Output, the phone and Mac set the TV's timer.
- **Background:**
  - a pre-blurred, palette-driven artwork backdrop that drifts slowly and cross-fades on track change;
  - a legibility scrim (`SCAmbient.legibilityScrim`: 4.5:1, or 7:1 under Increase Contrast);
  - **Apple TV: the artwork itself is the background** (owner on build 1206: "album art is STILL not showing behind"; the website TV mockup is the target, `.k-bd`). `NPBackdrop` draws the artwork at 240 px with a 3.5 px blur and 1.3 saturation, filling 120% of the screen and drifting about 2% over 46 s, no palette blobs, swirl or second copy, so the picture stays recognisable like the mockup. Over it `NPLegibilityScrim` is the mockup's scrim: a flat black layer between 40% and 58% (50–70% under Increase Contrast) chosen by `SCAmbient.legibilityScrim` over the artwork's mean, then the vertical gradient (32% at the top, 8% at 45%, 50% at the foot). The reactive line art sits on top. The Cue Points, Comments and Setlist covers use the same `NPCoverBackdrop` with 10% more black; Behind the Track keeps the collection-page backdrop.
  - a faint line-art layer that **reacts to the music** via the waveform envelope: rings on peaks, contours breathing with loudness. It uses no audio tap and costs about 3% CPU or less.
- **State shown by motion:**
  - pausing shrinks the artwork;
  - scrubbing grows the waveform;
  - routing to a TV shows a "Living Room" badge;
  - swipe anywhere on the hero to skip, as an interactive carousel;
  - the mini player zooms into Now Playing, and dragging down collapses it.
- **Mini player** (`TransportBarView`, the tab bar's bottom accessory; the system draws its Liquid Glass capsule):
  - **iPad (regular width)**, like Music on iPad: shuffle (primary when on, secondary when off), previous, a big play/pause with the symbol replace, next, repeat (the same); then 40 pt artwork (8 pt corners) with the title (semibold) over the artist (secondary); then •••, Lyrics (`quote.bubble`) and Up Next (`list.bullet`) on the right. Lyrics and Up Next open Now Playing on that pane. ••• is the bar's track menu: Play Next, Like, Add to Playlist, Go to Artist, Share, then Auto Mix, then Play On with the current Output named. Output and Auto Mix show only as small glyphs before the artist, never as extra buttons. Shuffle and repeat hide below 640 pt or at Dynamic Type XL and up. Nothing playing: the bar stays, with a grey `music.note` tile, "Not Playing" and dimmed controls (this tile is the one placeholder that isn't line art).
  - **iPhone:** the system accessory. Expanded: 38 pt artwork, title and artist (swipe to skip), play/pause and next. Inline (tab bar minimised): artwork and play/pause only. Hidden when nothing plays.
  - **Legibility:** the bar is the system accessory glass and styles everything with `.primary` and `.secondary` only, with `.tint(.primary)`, on every variant (playing, Not Playing and the Continue From offer, whose round play button is `.borderedProminent` tinted `.primary` with a system-background glyph): no fixed white or black, no album or page accent. On iPhone (and iPad at compact width) the glass and the labels adapt together to what is beneath. On iPad at regular width the bar's labels follow the system appearance (`MiniBarAppearance`, read from the window scene), because there the floating glass and its labels were seen to disagree, white labels on white glass in light mode (owner, 2026-11-09, #121 reopened). Nothing above the tab bar may force a window-wide scheme: `.toolbarColorScheme` stays `for: .navigationBar` on its own page, and when Now Playing (which prefers dark) closes, any leftover window style override is released.
  - **Opening:** tap anywhere on the bar outside its buttons, or swipe it up. Now Playing is a `fullScreenCover` with `.navigationTransition(.zoom)`, its source the bar's artwork (`matchedTransitionSource`, clipped to the artwork's corners), so the artwork grows into the player; swiping down shrinks it back into the bar. Under Reduce Motion the system's zoom falls back to a cross-fade.
  - **VoiceOver:** the bar is one element, "Now Playing, Title by Artist" plus Output, paused and Auto Mix state, with actions Play/Pause, Next Track, Previous Track, Open Now Playing, Lyrics and Up Next.
- **Playing from a cover opens Now Playing** (owner, 2026-11-09: "if you click it opens up a full screen view … to mask the fact that it's loading", and "the album art expand to where it would be on the larger now playing screen"):
  - **iPhone and iPad:** Play or Shuffle on a collection hero opens Now Playing as soon as the first track is committed, with the same `.zoom` transition, its source the hero's artwork (`nowPlayingZoomSource`, id `nowPlaying.cover.<collection>`), so the cover grows into the player and shrinks back into it on dismiss. Opening from the mini player still zooms from the bar. Reduce Motion: the system cross-fade.
  - **Mac:** the play button on a cover card (`macCardHover`) opens the full window once playback is committed; the cover's cached image flies from the card to the Now Playing artwork with the existing spring, cross-fades into the real artwork, and flies back to the card on close. Reduce Motion: a 0.2 s cross-fade, no flight.
  - **Apple TV:** unchanged; any explicit play shows Now Playing.
  - Now Playing shows the first track while it buffers (play control in its loading state); Up Next fills in as the rest of the collection arrives. No blank or stuck state: a start that never commits clears its pending reveal after 4 s.
  - **Feedback within 100 ms:** a medium impact haptic on iPhone, and the hero's Play turns into a small spinner (label unchanged, so nothing shifts), then into **Pause** while that collection plays here. A spinner never outlives 8 s or an error.
- **Restoring the last session** (iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple TV; not Watch): launch puts back what was playing, never playing it.
  - Restored: the current track, paused at its saved position; the whole queue in order (and the unshuffled order when shuffle is on); shuffle and repeat; the "Playing from…" source; Auto Mix on or off with the same style, so Play carries on the mix (upcoming transitions re-plan when needed). An open Decks session is not restored: with Auto Mix off, Play after launch plays through the ordinary player.
  - Play pressed while the account is still loading at launch waits for it (at most 10 s), then plays or says why it can't; Apple TV shows that reason under the title in Now Playing. The Apple TV never launches silent: an app volume a remote left at zero comes back at full, since the TV has no volume control of its own.
  - The mini player shows it straight away from what is on the device (the saved track, downloads, the library database), before any network. Tracks not on the device fill in later from one batched lookup; until they do, they are kept in the saved queue, not dropped.
  - Saved per account, on every track change, on pause, every 10 s while playing, and when the app goes to the background or quits. Credential refreshes and synced-session adoption keep the same account, so they never discard or restart a restore.
  - Signing out closes the account's session; another account never sees it. Clearing the queue forgets it.
- **Per platform:**
  - **iPad (regular width):** two columns, with the player on the left and the panes as tabs on the right.
  - **Mac:** the full window opens from the toolbar capsule or ⌘⇧N.
  - **Apple TV:** a button row with white focus, side panels and an idle clock.
  - **Watch:** a progress ring.

---

## 6. Motion and performance budgets
- **Animate only:** transform, opacity, trim/stroke-dashoffset and clip. Springs are `.smooth`/`.snappy`, interruptible, and under about 500 ms.
- **Architecture:**
  - no per-frame body re-evaluation of whole screens;
  - narrow `@Observable` models;
  - an **anchored timeline**: the anchor changes only on play/pause/seek/track change, and views interpolate between anchors;
  - waveforms draw with the shared Metal `MetalWaveformView` (a GPU min/max pyramid; the main thread only sets uniforms); remaining Canvas waveforms move to it as they're redesigned;
  - animations pause offscreen, when the app is inactive, in Low Power Mode and under Reduce Motion.
- **Images:** downsampled to display size off the main thread, cached, and purged on critical memory pressure and in the background (`MemoryRelief`). An artwork already in memory draws on the first frame, with no placeholder, fade or pop; while the exact size loads, any cached size of the same artwork stands in. A loaded image never swaps back to a placeholder while its view lives.
- **Budgets:**
  - **Scrolling and transitions:** a hitch ratio under 5 ms/s.
  - **CPU:** idle about 0%; Now Playing playing under 3% (iOS) and under 2% (Mac and TV).
  - **Background:** no unbounded background work.
  - **Website:** CLS 0 and Lighthouse 100.
  - **Tap to audio** (owner, 2026-11-09: "there should be an instant play … we should be prioritising the quickest possible play"): first audio never waits on the full tracklist, waveform, analysis, lyrics, comments or the mix engine. A collection starts from what the device already has (the tracks on screen, then the library database, then downloads); only when it has nothing does it make one small request (the collection, or the first 10 tracks) before starting. The rest arrives after audio starts, 50 at a time, at most 10 batches, 400 ms apart, appended to the queue. Local bytes (downloads and the played-audio cache) start with `playImmediately` and no stall waiting; network streams keep AVFoundation's stall protection with a 2 s startup forward buffer.
- **Measuring (iPhone):** `tools/ios-frame-probe.sh` builds an optimised Debug app (fixtures stay available), launches it without XCUITest and lets the in-app driver (`SOUNDING_TV_FRAME_PROBE_DRIVE`) scroll, push and search while the probe logs hitch ms/s and the worst frame per phase. UI-test-driven numbers include XCUITest's accessibility snapshots on the main thread and overstate hitches several times. Off the main thread: SoundCloud response decoding and cache encoding.
- **Background analysis:** several jobs at once, chosen by `AnalysisLanePolicy`: Mac on power = performance cores minus 2, Mac on battery 2, iPhone and iPad 2 while charging or in the background and 1 otherwise, Apple TV 2; always 1 during playback. Jobs run at `.background`. On power and not playing there is no rest between jobs; otherwise each lane rests 2 s after a job. It pauses on Low Power Mode, low battery or heat, and waits up to 60 s at launch for the first iCloud analysis sync so it never redoes a track another device already analysed. The DJ engine sleeps when idle.

---

## 7. Accessibility
- **VoiceOver:**
  - every control is labelled, and track rows read as one element ("Title, Artist, 3:45, downloaded, 128 BPM") with actions;
  - decorative art is hidden;
  - the scrubber is adjustable;
  - announcements are made for queue changes.
- **Dynamic Type** works up to AX5. Caps are allowed only on the transport row and the mini player.
- **Contrast** is at least 4.5:1, and Increase Contrast and Reduce Transparency are honoured (glass falls back to solid).
- **Reduce Motion:** every animation has a static variant.
- **tvOS:** everything is reachable by focus, with no traps. **Mac:** full keyboard access.

---

## 8. Words
- **Voice:** British English ("optimise", "colour", "analyse"). Short, specific, calm. No hype, no jargon, no implementation prose (sandbox, cache internals, sync mechanics, engine names).
- **Vocabulary** (fixed):
  - **Output** is where audio plays: this device, a linked Apple TV, or a Sonos room or group. The user-facing labels are "Play On" and "Devices".
  - **Devices** is where you link an iPhone, Mac or Apple TV. "Remotes & Devices" on tvOS.
  - **Artist** is someone who posts music: `User.isArtist` (they have tracks) is the one rule, and the uploader of a track or album counts as one. Everyone else is a **Profile** ("Go to Profile", "Profile · 120 followers", "Playlist by Alex"). Never "User". The Artists lists hold artists only; people you follow who don't post music live under Following.
  - **Behind the Track** is the rich track information view (never "About", "Info" or "Details" as its title).
  - **Tracklist** is the pane for a mix. **Setlists** are saved tracklists, under Library.
  - **Music Analysis** is tempo, key and genre. **Never say "Intelligence"** in the UI.
  - **Name the other device only when it's known** (owner, 2026-11-12). The Apple TV's sign-in and link UI says "your iPad" or "Alex's iPad" when the peer's `DeviceClass` or own name arrived in the hello, offer, identity or nearby advert, and is neutral otherwise: "Sign in with Another Device" (neutral symbol), "your device", "Finish on your device.", "A device" as the default name, and "Scan with your phone's camera" only where the camera is the method. Copy and symbol come from one pure place (`PeerDevice` and `TVPairingCopy` in `TVAccountMatch.swift`); never hard-code "iPhone" for a peer.
  - **BPM and key** words (`AnalysisStatusCopy`, one place): the per-track editor is **Tempo & Key…** with **Tap Tempo**, **Analyse Now** and **Reset to Analysed**; Settings has **Analyse All Now** and **Stop**. Waiting copy says what it waits for, Apple Photos style: "BPM and key are found when your iPhone is connected to power with Sounding open.", "…when your Mac isn’t playing music.", "Paused: Low Power Mode is on.", "Paused: your iPhone is too warm.", "Paused until your iPhone is charging.", "BPM and key are found when this track is downloaded.", "BPM and key are found when a track is on the decks, or come from your iPhone, iPad or Mac." (Apple TV), "BPM and key come from your iPhone, iPad or Mac." (Watch); progress is "Analysing… 120 of 2,400 tracks"; a forced run that overheats says "Stopped: your Mac is too warm."
  - **Never say "Companion"** in the UI.
  - **Link** is the trust between two devices, created by signing one in from the other ("Link an Apple TV"). A linked TV's status is **Connected**, **Needs Sign-In** or **Not Reachable**; with several TVs, one that's on the network but not the one being controlled is **Available**. Forgetting on either side removes the Link on both. A Link can exist without a sign-in: linking a signed-in TV sends no token.
  - **Accounts on other devices:** "Signed in as <name>" (a linked device on another account, or what a linked TV last used), "Signed in to another account" (an unlinked TV, whose name we don't know), "Switch <TV> to <name>…" (the only way to change a TV's account from another device). Never "wrong account" or "account mismatch".
  - **Remote** is a device controlling an Apple TV's playback: **Remote** in Devices. When Output is the TV, the whole app is its remote; there is no separate "remote mode". Model and roles: `Design/specs/devices-and-auth.md`.
  - **Handoff** means NSUserActivity only. Listening together is **SharePlay**; there is no local leader-and-speakers mode (Polyphony was removed 2026-11-08).
  - **Home Lighting** is lights following the music (Settings › Home Lighting), with intensity **Subtle**, **Party** or **Off**. The scenes are **Party Mode** and **Wind Down**. The device playing the music drives the lights.
  - **DJ Players** are Pioneer/AlphaTheta CDJs and XDJs. "Pro DJ Link" appears only in small print, with "not affiliated with AlphaTheta". **Prep** is not a page or a window (owner, 2026-11-12): a track's Prep panel (select it, ⌘I or ⌥⌘P) or the deck's waveform is where beat grids, cue points and loops get set; Prep's lists are smart playlists ("Needs a Grid Check", "Not on the Stick") and Export to USB lives in the File menu; tracks without a local file are "SoundCloud only". Spec: `Design/specs/dj-gear.md`.
  - **DJ Players** are Pioneer/AlphaTheta CDJs, XDJs and DJM mixers on the Mac's network (Settings › DJ Players, and the DJ Players button in Decks). Inside it: **Virtual Deck** (the Mac joins as a player so CDJs can sync to Decks), **Sync to DJ Players** (Decks and Auto Mix on the Mac follow the tempo master; a track outside the tempo-match range keeps its own tempo), **Tempo Master**, **Player N**, **On Air**, **Record Setlists** ("Recorded at <date>" Setlists). "Pro DJ Link" appears only in the small print "Uses Pro DJ Link. Not affiliated with AlphaTheta." Never call it "Link" (that word is Apple-device trust).
- **Notifications outside the app** (Apple's "Apple Account is being used to sign in" is the model): a title that names the thing, one plain sentence, at most two actions, no app name (the system shows it), no exclamation marks.
  - An Apple TV asking to sign in: "Living Room wants to sign in", "Tap to sign it in to Sounding from here." ("Sign it in to Sounding from here." on the Mac), actions **Link** and **Not Now**. Time Sensitive. One per TV: a newer request replaces the last one (collapsed and threaded per TV), and the TV's start screen asks at most once every 10 minutes. It disappears when the TV signs in or leaves its sign-in screen, and once it expires.
  - A signed-in Apple TV on your account that this device hasn't linked: "Living Room is nearby", "Link it to control playback and share settings.", actions **Link** and **Not Now**, the drawn Apple TV on brand orange as its picture, interruption level Active (not Time Sensitive), threaded as `link-requests`. The TV asks when it's open and either sees an unlinked iPhone of your account nearby or has nothing linked at all, at most once per TV every 12 hours. **Link** opens the app straight into linking that TV (Add Apple TV if it isn't found within 15 s). **Not Now** is the same as the card's Not Now (12 hours, and the TV must have been off or closed for 30 minutes since) and stops the TV asking under the same rule. It disappears when the TV links, closes or signs out, and after 10 minutes. With Sounding open the banner never shows: the nearby card takes over.
  - The account on a new device: **New Sign-In**, "Your SoundCloud account is now signed in on Living Room.", action **Show Devices**. Time Sensitive. Never for this device's own sign-in, a token refresh, a relaunch, or a device that has been signed in before.
  - Permission is asked in context (after the first Link, or from Devices), never at launch; denied means in-app only, silently.
  - **Nothing stays once it no longer applies** (owner, 2026-11-12: "can we wipe these after they are no longer relevant?"). Delivered alerts go when their request is withdrawn or expires, when the TV signs in or links, when they're acted on from another device, or when the account changes, and at most one per TV is kept. The app sweeps at launch, on becoming active, after a Link and after an account change, besides the silent withdrawal push.
- **Errors** are plain and actionable, never raw error text. A rate limit says "SoundCloud is limiting requests", that the sign-in is saved, and when it resumes. On iPhone and iPad that notice is a dismissible full-width bar above the tabs that pushes the app down, so it never covers a title or toolbar button; on the Mac it's a bar under the toolbar.
- **Independence:** wherever branding appears, Sounding is described as "not affiliated with SoundCloud". SoundCloud's logos are never used.

---

## 9. Data, network and content
- **Library identity** (owner 2026-11-09: "we aren't doing a good job linking local and SoundCloud artists/albums/etc together"). One shared layer links your own files to SoundCloud: `LibraryNames` and `LibraryIdentityMatcher` (`Core/Library/LibraryIdentityMatching.swift`) and `LibraryIdentity` (`Shared/ViewModels/LibraryIdentity.swift`). Names fold case, diacritics, width and curly quotes, treat "&" and "+" as "and", drop a leading "The", split credits on feat./ft./featuring, &, and, x, ×, vs, b2b, commas and slashes, and strip album suffixes (" - Single", " - EP", "(Deluxe)", "(Deluxe Edition)", "(Remastered 2011)", "(10th Anniversary Edition)"). Own-file artists group by their primary credit, so "Bicep feat. Clara La San" sits under Bicep and Clara La San gets it in Appears On. **Artist ↔ SoundCloud user** is confidence-gated: a name match (0.5–0.6) plus evidence (followed +0.2, uploader of liked tracks up to +0.15, owner of a liked album +0.1, the same track titles in both up to +0.25, verified +0.05) must reach 0.75 and beat the runner-up by 0.15, or nothing links. **Album ↔ SoundCloud album** (`is_album`) needs the same suffix-stripped title, the artist (by name or by the artist link) and the track count (equal, or within two for a lower score), 0.85 to link. **Track ↔ SoundCloud track** needs the same title, a matching artist (name, credit or link) and a duration within 3 s, and exactly one candidate. Inputs are only what the library database already holds (follows, likes, liked and own playlists and synced album track lists) and the scanned files: **identity never makes a SoundCloud request.** Links persist in the library database (`ldb_identity_link`: automatic, manual or unlinked), survive an account switch, and a manual link or Unlink always wins over matching. The rebuild runs off the main thread, debounced after file scans and library syncs. Everything that shows the library reads it: Artists, Albums, the Tracks lists (a liked SoundCloud track with a local copy shows once, as the file, with both marks), the Mac Source column ("File · SoundCloud"), Library search (`LibraryIdentityIndex.search`, and liked tracks with a local copy come back as the file), artist pages and album pages (`soundCloudAlbum(forLocalAlbumID:)`, `localAlbum(forPlaylistID:)`, `localCopy(of:)`, `preferringLocalCopies`).

- **Manual linking** (owner 2026-11-09; `LibraryLinkSheets.swift`, `LibraryIdentity.link(album:to:)`, `link(track:to:)`, `unlink`, `allowAutomaticLinking`). Albums and tracks link by hand like artists, from both sides. Own-file albums and tracks get **Link to SoundCloud Album…** / **Link to SoundCloud Track…**; SoundCloud albums and tracks get **Link to File…**. Menus show a disabled **Linked to <title>** header, then **Unlink**, or **Don't Link Automatically** when nothing is linked, or **Link Automatically** after an Unlink. Album tiles carry the items in `LibraryCollectionGrid`, album pages in their ••• menu, tracks in `TrackActionMenuItems` and `MacTrackContextMenu`. The picker is a standard searchable sheet (a Mac sheet on the Mac) hosted once by `.libraryLinkHost()` at the app root. Rows show artwork, title, artist, and "N tracks · year" with "12 of 12 tracks match" for albums, or the duration with a ±seconds note in orange when it differs from the file by more than 3 s for tracks. Tracks rank liked first, then the linked album or artist. Search only filters what the library database holds; **the sheets make no SoundCloud request**. A save writes a manual link (it beats automatic), patches the in-memory index at once and persists; no full rebuild runs unless one was already in flight. A manual link keeps its SoundCloud item in the link's payload so it survives a rebuild even when that item is not in the library.
- **Fixtures** (`VisualFixtures`) are DEBUG-only and must never surface in Release or in marketing.
- **Default avatars:** SoundCloud's `default_avatar` is treated as no image, and the line-art placeholder is shown instead.
- **Artwork cache** (`SCArtworkStore`, owner 2026-11-09: "cache images too, resize them to be effective and not waste space"): every remote artwork and avatar goes through it (`ArtworkImage`, `SCArtworkResolver`), never `AsyncImage`. It requests the smallest SoundCloud size that covers the variant (`t200x200`/`t300x300`/`t500x500`, `original` only for heroes), downsamples off the main thread to the variant's display size (thumbnail 240 px iPhone/iPad, 200 Mac, 300 TV, 120 Watch; standard 500, Watch 240; hero 1200 iPhone/iPad, 1600 Mac, 1800 TV, 400 Watch) and stores that, never the original: HEIC where the device can encode it, else JPEG at 0.8, PNG only with alpha. A smaller variant is cut from a larger one already on disk before the network is asked. Disk caps, oldest-used evicted first: iPhone and iPad 256 MB, Mac 512 MB, Apple TV 128 MB, Watch 32 MB; it lives in Caches, so the system may purge it. Decoded images stay in memory, ready for display, with a budget of 1/32 of the device's RAM (iPhone and iPad 64–192 MB, Apple TV 64–128 MB, Mac 128–384 MB, Watch 16 MB), cleared on critical memory pressure and in the background; a plain memory warning leaves them to the system cache. A new image shows as soon as it is decoded; encoding and writing it to disk happen afterwards on a background queue. Prefetch near the viewport is a newest-first queue of at most 48, three at a time, so a fast scroll never leaves a backlog of off-screen downloads ahead of what is on screen; images that scroll away mid-download are cancelled. Playlist mosaics load their tiles in parallel from the same cache and stay in memory. Settings › Downloads shows **Artwork** with **Clear Artwork**. Accent colours and palettes sample the cached small image.
- **Be a good citizen:** cache, coalesce identical in-flight requests, sync incrementally, poll lazily, apply a request budget, and use jittered backoff. A 429 or DataDome challenge from something the user is doing triggers the global traffic stop. A 429 from background work pauses only background work, so the user is never interrupted. A lone 403 fails only that request.
- **Likes stick** (owner bug, 2026-11-09: "liking a song doesn't work, just stops being liked after a second"). Like and Unlike are optimistic and stay as tapped until SoundCloud confirms or refuses. A rate limit or traffic stop of up to 10 minutes keeps the change pending (the traffic bar says when it resumes) and retries it after the back-off, at most three tries. A real refusal, or a longer stop, rolls back once and says so: a plain alert ("Couldn't Add to Likes" or "Couldn't Remove from Likes" with one line of reason), or just the traffic bar when a stop is showing. Never a silent revert. In the library database a pending like, unlike, follow, repost or playlist change wins over any list response or peer chunk that started before it, and over any response for 2 minutes after SoundCloud confirms it, so a refresh, fill, walk or another device's older sync can't undo it. The Likes fill at launch is background work; only opening a list makes its fill the user's.
- **Request ceilings** (per device, SoundCloud API hosts only): at most **36 requests in any minute** (a burst of 6, then 30 a minute) and **900 in any hour**. Background work gets at most **12 a minute and 180 an hour**, and stops once the hour reaches 600 so the user always has headroom. Artwork, waveforms and audio come from the CDN and aren't counted. `SoundCloudTrafficScenarioTests` replays launch, likes indexing, Spotlight, Siri, Now Playing and Remote against a fake SoundCloud and fails if a ceiling is crossed.
- **LRCLIB** (lyrics, the only non-SoundCloud content host): `User-Agent: Sounding/<version> (https://sounding.forbes.red)`, one request at a time 200–500 ms apart, current and next track only, a 429/503 stops all lyrics requests for `Retry-After` plus jitter, results and misses cached on device (60 days, 3 days; the cache key carries a matcher version, so a better matcher retries old misses). Privacy notes: `Design/appstore/app-privacy.md`.
  - **Matching** (owner, 2026-11-12: "does the lyrics ever work?"): the title is cleaned first (split "Artist - Title", drop promo tags such as `[FREE DL]`, `**FREE DOWNLOAD**`, Premiere and Official Audio, prefer the publisher artist and title over the uploader), then at most four lookups, stopping at the first match: `/api/get` with the length; `/api/get` without the length on the base title (remix, edit and feat. removed); `/api/search` by track and artist; `/api/search?q=` on the cleaned title. No album is sent (a wrong album makes LRCLIB miss). Lyrics are synced only when the length is within 3 s and the version matches; an extended mix, edit or remix of a song LRCLIB has shows that song's lyrics unsynced. A length difference over both 90 s and 80% of the shorter length is a miss. A SoundCloud preview sends no length.
- **Behind the Track requests:** lazy (only while the view is open, after a 450 ms settle, never per row), deduplicated in flight and cached in memory for 30 minutes (40 tracks) on top of the gateway's 10-minute `tracks/*` cache. One `tracks/{id}` for the full metadata; for followed people, `tracks/{id}/likers` (falling back to `favoriters` on 404) and `tracks/{id}/reposters`, at most 2 pages of 100 each, skipped when the count is zero, signed out, for local files or with no followings, intersected locally with the library database's followings. A 429 or block stops all of them for at least 2 minutes; the page keeps what it already has. Forgotten with the account's data.
- **Never fan out per item.** Batch endpoints (`tracks?ids=`, 50 at a time) or the local library first; when one-by-one lookups are unavoidable (system entity hydration), cap them at 5 and stop at the first rate limit. Search-driven "load more" waits for typing to settle and stops after 10 pages per visit. A list footer that stays on screen loads at most 10 pages on its own, then shows **Load More**. A list paused by a stop resumes only when its footer is on screen again, never by itself the moment the stop ends.
- **Played-audio cache** (owner, 2026-11-09: "we should cache anything you play for at least a good couple of hours"; `PlayedAudioCache`, `PlayedAudioCacheLedger`). After 8 s of audible playback of a SoundCloud track this account may play in full (never previews, Opus, own files or downloads), the same signed stream is fetched once more in the background, progressive as one file or HLS assembled from its segments, encrypted with the account's download key and kept in Caches (`PlayedAudio/<account hash>`), never in Sounding Data and never exported. Filling skips cellular and Low Data Mode. Kept at least 3 hours, up to 72 hours since last played; 1.5 GB soft cap evicting the least recently played outside the 3-hour window, 2 GB hard cap, 400 MB per item. Replaying, or going back, plays from those bytes before any network. Playback still checks the plan first, a cached file that fails is dropped, signing out deletes the lot, and a rate limit or traffic stop pauses filling for 10 minutes. It counts as media, not API, traffic and shows in Network Activity as `play-cache`. Bytes are sealed as they arrive, so each track is written to flash once, never as a plain copy first.
- **Flash writes stay small during playback** (iPad disk-writes report, 2026-11-12: 4.3 GB in 85 minutes of Auto Mix). iOS budgets about 50 KB/s a day. Mix Engine sources (`SourceImporter`, Caches/MixEngine) keep the downloaded file and decode it in real time whenever `AVAudioFile` reads it at 44.1 kHz with the full length; decoded float PCM (21 MB a minute) is written only for files that need resampling or that `AVAudioFile` can't read. Caches touch their recency at most hourly (analysis rows), whole-file caches debounce and skip unchanged bytes, and nothing persists per playback tick.
- **Prewarming on intent** (`preparePlayback`, `CollectionPlayback.prewarm`): touch-down on Play (iPhone and iPad), pointer hover on a cover or Play (iPad and Mac) and focus (Apple TV) resolve the first track's stream before the tap lands. Hover and focus are debounced and spaced 2 s apart; touch-down is immediate. One prepared item at a time, 25 s fresh; the next queued track is resolved when playback starts and again 18 s before the end. Resolved URLs live in one LRU of 48 (`ResolvedStreamURLCache`): 30 s when unsigned, otherwise until 90 s before the signature expires, 20 minutes at most. Identical in-flight resolutions coalesce in the gateway.
- **Intent is honest.** Work the user didn't just ask for is background work: Home refreshing shelves it already shows, Siri and Spotlight entity lookups, and download stream resolution. "Not following" answers are cached like any other response.
- **Home never forgets** (owner, 2026-11-09: "the home page loses lots of things"). One long-lived store per surface (`HomeStore`: For You and Following) is shared by every view and window on iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple TV and Apple Watch, so switching tabs, surfaces or sidebar items never reloads from nothing. The last good Home is on disk and shows at launch. A refresh runs in the store, not the view, so leaving the page doesn't cancel it; it replaces a shelf only when that shelf's own request succeeds, keeps stale shelves on 429, offline or errors, and lands quietly (a soft animation, none under Reduce Motion). Visits inside 5 minutes don't refetch. Signing out drops the personal shelves and the Following feed; public shelves stay.
- **Stop lengths:** concurrent 429s from one burst are one strike, and background strikes never lengthen a stop the user sees. The first interactive stop lasts about 2 minutes and doubles per further episode up to an hour. A 429 from a waveform, audio or the token endpoint pauses only that kind of request.
- **Network Activity** (Settings › About & Advanced on iPhone and iPad, Settings › Advanced on the Mac and Apple TV; Debug and TestFlight only) is the one request log. Every `URLSession` path writes to `NetworkActivityLog`: the SoundCloud gateway (API, stream resolution, waveforms, token refresh), artwork, promoted tracking pings, downloads, Sonos, and media URLs handed to the player. It keeps the last 2,000 requests in memory: time, feature, method, host, path and query keys, status, duration, bytes, and whether a request came from cache, was shared, held, retried, rate-limited or failed. Token, client_id, OAuth, signature, policy and other secret-like query values, and long opaque values, are replaced by REDACTED; headers, cookies and bodies are never recorded. Issues are flagged automatically (rate limited, backoff, failed, duplicate within 3 s, repeated promoted ping, sent in the background, unexpected host) and counted at the top. A last `detail` column says why a token refresh ran and on which device ("refresh on phone, 401 handler"), carries the OAuth error code SoundCloud returned ("oauth error invalid_grant"), and marks requests held because the sign-in needs recovery; it never carries token values. The viewer is a native list with feature and Issues Only filters, search, a detail page, Export, Clear and Pause; an optional session file goes to Caches. Console.app shows the same lines under `red.forbes.sounding` / `network`, with paths private.
- **SoundCloud tiers** (`Design/specs/soundcloud-tiers.md`, logic in `SoundCloudEntitlements`):
  - **The gate is per track**. Any signed-in account, Free and Artist included, streams in full and downloads every track that isn't Go-restricted. Go-restricted means a Go+ catalogue track (`monetization_model` `SUB_HIGH_TIER`), a `SNIP` policy, or a preview-only track (`snipped` transcodings only). `MONETIZE` tracks count as not restricted while the one switch `SoundCloudEntitlements.monetizedTracksOpen` is on (it is). Signed-out accounts are unchanged: own files and SoundCloud's previews only (`requiresSignInForPlayback`). Sounding still plays no ads. `SoundCloudEntitlements.isGoRestricted`, `trackDenial` and `canDownload` hold the rule.
  - Go+ catalogue tracks need Go+ or DJ to play in full or download; on Free, Artist and Go they're previews. Downloads use the best transcoding the account is entitled to (no `hq` on Free, Artist or Go when a standard one exists), stay encrypted inside the app container and are never exported. Send to Watch carries only those downloads (and your own files); the Watch plays only the files it was sent, never streams them, and drops SoundCloud files when the iPhone does or after 30 days without hearing from it.
  - **Protected Go+ tracks** (2026-11-12): some Go+ tracks come only as FairPlay-protected HLS (`cbc-encrypted-hls`). Playback prefers any unprotected transcoding, uses FairPlay only when there's nothing else (`Track.needsFairPlay`, licence flow in `FairPlayStreaming.swift`), and never picks Widevine (`ctr-encrypted-hls`). Protected audio plays through the ordinary player on every device but can't be decoded, so it never loads on DJ decks or Prep, never blends in Auto Mix (plain advance instead), isn't analysed, cached or downloaded, and can't go to Sonos. Each place says so in one line: "Protected Go+ track. It plays, but can't be mixed or analysed." A refused licence shows "This track needs SoundCloud Go+."
  - **Previews:** when SoundCloud supplies its own 30-second preview (a `snipped` transcoding, mostly Go+ tracks) and the account can't play the full track (free, Artist, signed out, or Go on a Go+ track), the preview plays exactly as supplied: no extension, no looping, never a cut of our own. The scrubber shows the preview length; rows and Now Playing say "Preview · 0:30". Previews never blend in Auto Mix (hard stop, then the next track), never download, never load on DJ decks or Prep, never export, and never go to Sonos or SharePlay. When one ends, once per launch: "Full track needs SoundCloud Go+." with **Subscribe in SoundCloud**; Apple TV shows OK only. Signed-out tracks with no preview say "Sign in to SoundCloud to play tracks."
  - **Go+ is marked, quietly, everywhere** (owner: "do we support and clearly mark Go+?"). A Go+ catalogue track (`requiresGoPlus`) carries a small outlined **Go+** mark after its title in every track row and table on iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV (`SoundCloudTrackBadge`: caption2 semibold, the row's secondary colour, like Music's Explicit glyph; never orange). Rows already say "Preview · 0:30" in the time, so they show only Go+; tables, whose Time column shows the full length, show **Preview** instead when the track plays as a preview. Now Playing shows the Go+ mark under the artist while a Go+ track plays in full, and the preview line while it previews. Behind the Track has an **Availability** row ("SoundCloud Go+", "Preview · 0:30" or both). The account row and Plan row say the plan in full: "@name · SoundCloud Go+". Never imply a preview is the full track.
  - A lapsed plan falls back to Free at once: Go+ downloads stop playing, and they're deleted after 7 days on a confirmed unpaid plan. Every download is deleted after 30 days without a confirmed sign-in, on any plan. Settings warns only when Go+ downloads are due to go.
  - DJ gear, through one check (`SoundCloudEntitlements.djGearDenial`, wrapped by `MacDeckControlSurface.djDenial`):
    - Manual decks, controllers, and Prep grid and cue editing can stream SoundCloud tracks on Go+ and DJ, mirroring SoundCloud's own DJ-software deal. On every other plan they're own-files only: "DJ decks need SoundCloud Go+ for SoundCloud tracks." with **Subscribe in SoundCloud**.
    - Export to USB never takes SoundCloud audio, on any plan.
    - The Personal build's hidden unlock allows everything.
    - Auto Mix is ordinary playback.
  - **Promoted feed items** are shown as SoundCloud shows them, with a "Promoted" (or "Promoted by …") line, and SoundCloud's tracking URLs fire as SoundCloud's apps fire them:
    - an impression once the item is at least half on screen for 1 s, once per item;
    - play or click only when the user plays or opens it.
    - Never in the background, in fixtures, in tests or in snapshots, never while traffic is stopped, and at most 30 a minute.
  - Gated actions show a terse alert ("This track needs SoundCloud Go+.") with **Subscribe in SoundCloud**, which Apple TV omits. Only Go-restricted tracks and DJ gear ever ask for a plan; nothing asks a Free account to subscribe for a track it may play.
- **SoundCloud sign-in renewal** (SoundCloud rotates refresh tokens, and every device can hold the same pair):
  - one refresh at a time per app (`DeviceSessionRefresher`); the Share extension never refreshes, it reads what the app stored;
  - before refreshing, a device adopts a newer pair from iCloud or a linked peer, compared by issue time, then generation;
  - a refresh token SoundCloud refused (`invalid_grant`) is remembered and never sent again; the device adopts a synced pair, and only then asks to reconnect;
  - while the sign-in needs reconnecting, authenticated requests are held, not sent; `/me` never goes out without a token;
  - a 401 is retried once only when the pair changed underneath it; a 401 within 10 s of a 200 adopts a newer pair and does not refresh;
  - a handed-off pair is validated, not refreshed, unless it has expired.
  - iCloud keeps one session record per SoundCloud account (`session-<tag>`, encrypted account name and writer), beside the legacy record for older builds. A signed-in device never moves to another account from iCloud. A device that signed out isn't signed back in by sync; the welcome offers **Continue as <name>** ("Signed in on <device>") instead. A guest's sign-in on a TV (handed off or switched by a phone) is written to iCloud only when iCloud already holds that account (owner, 2026-11-12).
- **Your own music on Apple TV** (`LocalMusicSharing.swift`):
  - A linked Mac, iPhone or iPad offers its music folders to the TV over Companion: name, device class, track count and a 30-minute grant. The TV fetches the track list (title, artist, album, genre, length, BPM and key when analysed), artwork and audio from that device's Link content server, which only answers TLS on the Link secret and only serves `local/…` paths to a live grant it issued. Audio is fetched in 2 MB ranges into the TV's Caches (3 GB, oldest first) and then plays through the normal player, so Auto Mix uses it like any local file; cached analysis comes with it.
  - TV Library: a **Your Music** shelf first, and a detail page grouped by device ("On Studio Mac"). Signed out, the Library tab shows Your Music instead of the sign-in prompt.
  - The TV works without SoundCloud. Its sources are SoundCloud and the owner's linked devices; it has no music of its own. A signed-out first run is SoundCloud-first: the sign-in screen itself (QR code and chime) with **Use Without SoundCloud** centred below the sign-in controls; Close remains at the top right when presented from elsewhere; `SOUNDING_TV_START_SCREEN=1` forces it. After signing in or going on without SoundCloud, one step shows once per install (it replaces the old "Your sound. Fill the room." welcome): the same shared feature page in tvOS idiom, pushed in: "✓ Signed in as <name>" when signed in, `music.note.house`, **Your Own Music**, rows From Your Devices (folders chosen in Sounding on the Mac, iPhone or iPad), Over Your Home Network and From iCloud, and **OK** with default focus (`SOUNDING_TV_MUSIC_STEP=1` forces it). There is no folder picker on the TV. Settings › **Music Folders** lists the devices and iCloud offering music, with one footer saying where it comes from and that folders are chosen on those devices. If a linked device is already offering music, the TV skips that screen and lands in the Library. Without SoundCloud, the sidebar is Library, Now Playing and Settings only. Sign-in stays one press away: Settings › Plan › **Sign In to SoundCloud**, and the Your Music empty and offline states (**Link a Device**, **Sign In to SoundCloud**). Signing in from there never signs out or unlinks anything.
  - TV Library: a **Your Music** shelf first, and a detail page grouped by device ("On Studio Mac"). Signed out, the Library tab is the branded signed-out page in §2 Apple TV, with Your Music first when there is any.
  - The TV works without SoundCloud. Its sources are SoundCloud and the owner's linked devices; it has no music of its own. A signed-out first run is SoundCloud-first: the sign-in screen itself (QR code and Play Sound, §2 Apple TV) with **Use Without SoundCloud** centred below the sign-in controls; Close remains at the top right when presented from elsewhere; `SOUNDING_TV_START_SCREEN=1` forces it. After signing in or going on without SoundCloud, one step shows once per install (it replaces the old "Your sound. Fill the room." welcome): a centred `ContentUnavailableView` **Your Own Music**, "This Apple TV plays your own music from Sounding on your Mac, iPhone or iPad, over your home network or from iCloud.", and **OK** (`SOUNDING_TV_MUSIC_STEP=1` forces it). There is no folder picker on the TV. Settings › **Music Folders** lists the devices and iCloud offering music, with one footer saying where it comes from and that folders are chosen on those devices. If a linked device is already offering music, the TV skips that screen and lands in the Library. Without SoundCloud, the sidebar is Library, Now Playing and Settings only. Sign-in stays one press away: Settings › Plan › **Sign In to SoundCloud**, and the Your Music empty and offline states (**Link a Device**, **Sign In to SoundCloud**). Signing in from there never signs out or unlinks anything.
  - A device that isn't reachable keeps its tracks listed, dimmed and not playable, with "Make sure <device> is awake, nearby and running Sounding." When nothing is reachable it's a centred `ContentUnavailableView`. iPhone and iPad stop serving when Sounding goes to the background ("Open Sounding on iPhone"); the Mac keeps its Companion connection, and so keeps serving, while the app runs and has music.
  - Nothing is uploaded unless the owner turns on **Upload to iCloud** (Music Folders settings on iPhone, iPad and Mac; off by default). It uploads own files only, never SoundCloud audio, to the private database (zone `LocalMusic`, record `LocalTrackAsset`), on Wi-Fi only, showing progress, iCloud storage used and **Remove All from iCloud**. The TV lists those tracks "In iCloud" (tracks a reachable device also has stay under that device), prefers the home network when a device has the track, and otherwise downloads the asset to Caches and plays it.
- **Nearby Apple TV card** (owner, 2026-11-09, choices 1a and 1a: Sonos-style automatic linking with an AirPods-style card). One shared card, `NearbyDeviceCard` and `NearbyDeviceCardModel` (`Shared/UI/Components/NearbyDeviceCard.swift`): a large hierarchical device SF Symbol (below), the TV's name as the title, one line, a big prominent button and **Not Now**. iPhone and iPad show it rising from the bottom over whatever screen is up, on the system background with a dimmed scrim; swipe down or tap outside means Not Now. The Mac shows the same card as a sheet on the main window. It's remembered like AirPods (owner, 2026-11-09: "this is not remembered?"): never shown while another pairing sheet is up, never **Link** for a TV this device has already linked (matched by kind and name), never **Sign In** for a TV that's signed in (a signed-in TV's Add a Device screen now carries the account hint too). **Not Now** is saved on the device per TV (kind and name), and the card comes back only after 12 hours and once that TV has been out of sight for 30 minutes and reappeared, never just because the app relaunched. Hiding a TV for 30 days is no longer a button: it is **Don't Ask for This Apple TV** in the card's context menu (and the Mac sheet's ellipsis menu). **Layout, redesigned 2026-11-09** ("fuck ugly"): the SF Symbol on top, the TV's own name as the title (falling back to "Apple TV"), a small "Apple TV · Signed in as <account>" line, one terse sentence, then the buttons. iPhone and iPad: a full-width orange prominent **Link** (or **Sign In**) with a plain **Not Now** below, on a Liquid Glass card. Mac: a native sheet with **Not Now** and a default prominent **Link** side by side, trailing-aligned (Return links, Esc is Not Now), and the ellipsis menu leading. Tint is brand orange (`Color.scAccent`), never system blue. There is no inline Home row for a waiting Apple TV on any platform; the card is the only prompt (Devices keeps its Needs Sign-In row).
- **Reconnect SoundCloud card** (owner, 2026-11-12: "awful hidden hard to see banners"). When the account status becomes Needs Reconnect (the 2026-11-07 decision), the same bottom-rising card (iPhone and iPad) or sheet (Mac) shows `person.crop.circle.badge.exclamationmark` in brand orange, **Reconnect SoundCloud**, "Sign in again to get your picks, likes and library back.", a prominent **Reconnect** that opens the existing SoundCloud sign-in, and **Not Now**. `ReconnectCardModel` shows it once per launch and again whenever the status flips to Needs Reconnect; Not Now (or tapping Reconnect) hides it until then. Only one card at a time: the nearby Apple TV card wins and Reconnect waits behind it. The avatar badge, the Settings badge and the Account page stay as they are. The shared container (`bottomCard`) hosts either card: scrim, drag or tap to dismiss, Liquid Glass on iPhone and iPad, a native sheet on the Mac.
  - **The nearby card uses the original SF Symbol** (owner, 2026-11-09: "i prefered the old image of an apple tv"). A 92 pt regular, hierarchical `DeviceClass.symbolName` (`appletv.fill` for Apple TV), primary ink, centred in a 132 pt image area. No drawn or rotating product on the card. `DeviceIllustration` remains for `NearbySearchHeader`, which still uses it; its Reduce Motion and inactive-state handling remains.
  - **One row or card per TV.** A TV can show up more than once (several network interfaces, or an old session still advertised). Offers are merged by kind and name, keeping the one first seen most recently (`LinkOfferBrowser.distinctOffers`), for the card's candidates and for every Nearby list.
  - **Add Apple TV** (iPhone and iPad sheet, Mac Settings › Devices sheet) while listening is a native list, not a stack of buttons: a small header (the drawn Apple TV and "Looking for Apple TVs nearby…", with one line of guidance only once nothing has turned up after a few seconds or the TV can't be heard), then an inset grouped **Nearby** section with one row per TV: its symbol from `DeviceClass`, its name, a secondary status (**Ready to sign in**, **Signed in**, **Linked**) and a chevron. A row opens the existing numeric-code or one-tap link flow. **Use QR Code Instead** (Mac: **Use a Code Instead**, or **Listen for Apple TV** when not listening) is a plain button at the bottom.
  - **A signed-out Apple TV on its sign-in screen** (its Link offer has no account hint) and a signed-in phone or Mac: "Sign it in to Sounding as <account>." (owner, 2026-11-12; was "…with your SoundCloud account."), **Sign In**. While offering, the phone advertises `SoundingTV-Nearby-<salt>` with its name, so the line under the TV's code reads "<iPhone> is nearby: open Sounding on it to sign in." Discovery transfers nothing. **Sign In** turns the microphone on (asking permission then, if needed), asks the TV over the Link offer to play its sound (`chime_play_request`), hears the 128-bit code and runs the chime handshake exactly as Add Apple TV does; when the authenticated name matches, it confirms without a second tap, then the normal Link transfer signs the TV in under the "Signing in <TV>" Live Activity. Card states: Listening…, Connecting…, Signing in…; nothing heard in 20 s, or the TV can't be reached, shows the reason with **Try Again**, **Use QR Code Instead** (Mac: **Use a Code Instead**, the six-digit code) and **Not Now**.
  - **A signed-in Apple TV on the same account, not linked** (§4.4b in `devices-and-auth.md`): "Link it to control playback from this iPhone." under "Apple TV · Signed in as <account>", **Link**, which opens the six-digit code as before. This replaces the Home banner and the Mac's bar under the toolbar (owner: the banner "looks lost on the page, not a clear CTA"); Settings › Devices keeps its row.
- **The library database is king** (`Design/specs/library-db.md`). One local database per device, bound to one SoundCloud account, is the only source for every library screen on every platform: Likes, Sets, Playlists (with their tracks), Following, Followers, reposts and your own Tracks. Screens read and observe it and never wait on the network; while the first sync runs they show a centred loading state with the progress line, never an empty state. One engine fills it once (resumable, background lane, 200 items a page, about two requests in flight 1.5 to 2.5 s apart, 150 an hour, like lists 300 a day, followers capped at 2,000, parks at any 429), then patches it: newest-first heads that stop on 20 known items, playlist `last_modified|track_count` checks, `/me` count checks and a weekly full walk; your own likes, follows and reposts write the database first and revert if SoundCloud refuses. Devices copy it to each other in bucketed chunks over Link (iPhone, iPad and Mac to the Apple TV) and iCloud (zone `Library`, record `LibraryChunk`); a device that receives a fresh copy skips its own download. **One device backfills per account** (owner, 2026-11-09, after SoundCloud returned 500 and 503 when every device backfilled at once): devices elect a backfiller through a lease kept in the same `LibraryChunk` zone (record `<account>.lease.0`, written compare-and-swap, 10 minutes, renewed every 3 while that device is open and syncing, carrying any SoundCloud stop so no other device starts during it). The order is Mac on power, then iPad or iPhone on power, then anything else; Apple TV and Apple Watch never backfill while any other device of that account exists. A better device that is open claims a free lease first and the next waits 3 minutes per place; nobody takes a held lease until it expires. Every other device only refreshes the Likes head page (one page every 15 minutes, plus one page for a list you just changed) and reads everything else from the shared chunks, with "Your Mac is syncing your library…" as its status line until they arrive. Without iCloud a device decides alone after 90 seconds. Another account's data is never merged; switching accounts or signing out wipes it. Status is one quiet line ("Syncing your library… 3,200 of 5,400 likes", then "Library up to date") in Settings › Library and at the bottom of Likes and Tracks.
- **Library pacing** (owner, 2026-11-09: "fetch all the items in for example likes right away, and slowly read the full personal collection quite quickly, and store in icloud. but then for the actual data … it downloads a bit more chill, one at a time, staggered"). Three speeds. **Up front:** opening Likes, Tracks, Playlists, Albums or Following walks that list to the end at once on the user lane, 200 a page (launch also fills Likes on the backfilling device, as background work so its 429 never stops the app), so it is complete without scrolling; the first rows show at once and the rest fill in, and lists read from the database load whole, so sort and filter never need Load All. **Background:** one walker per account (the backfilling device) reads the rest of the collection (likes, own and liked playlists and albums with their tracks, reposts, followings, followers, uploads) with about two requests in flight 1.5 to 2.5 s apart under the gateway's background ceiling (12 a minute, 150 of its 180 an hour), resumes from its cursors after a relaunch, stops at the first 429 until `Retry-After`, and shares what it reads through `LibraryChunk` so other devices don't fetch it. If iCloud refuses the record type (schema not deployed), the library stays on the device and iCloud is tried again after 6 hours, without errors on screen. **Heavy data:** background downloads (collection downloads, quality upgrades) and background analysis run one item at a time with 3 to 7 s between items (`HeavyWorkPacing`), pause for Low Power Mode, heat, cellular (downloads) and streaming playback, and back off on 429 with `Retry-After`; a single-track Download, Analyse Now and playing a track skip the queue. Settings › Library shows two quiet lines: "Library: 4,210 of 4,500 items" and "Analysing 1 of 820 · paused on battery". Nothing else in the main UI.
  - **A signed-in Apple TV on the same account, not linked** (§4.4b in `devices-and-auth.md`): "Link it to control playback from this iPhone." under "Apple TV · Signed in as <account>", **Link**. Then the six-digit code and **Allow** on the TV. **Auto-link** (owner, 2026-11-12, Q1: "link auto if already linked and not unlinked"; replaces decision 1a): when this device was linked to the TV before and the user never ended it (Forget on either device is recorded as an explicit unlink), both hold this Apple ID's link key and both are on the same SoundCloud account, the link remakes itself with no card and no tap, sends no token, and shows "Linked to <TV>" in a glass capsule for 2 s (iPhone, iPad; the TV shows "<device> is linked"). Same Apple ID alone isn't enough. If it fails within 15 s the card takes over. A TV on another account never gets the card. This replaces the Home banner and the Mac's bar under the toolbar (owner: the banner "looks lost on the page, not a clear CTA"); Settings › Devices keeps its row.
- **The library database is king** (`Design/specs/library-db.md`). One local database per device, bound to one SoundCloud account, is the only source for every library screen on every platform: Likes, Sets, Playlists (with their tracks), Following, Followers, reposts and your own Tracks. Screens read and observe it and never wait on the network; while the first sync runs they show a centred loading state with the progress line, never an empty state. One engine fills it once (resumable, background lane, 200 items a page, about two requests in flight 1.5 to 2.5 s apart, 150 an hour, like lists 300 a day, followers capped at 2,000, parks at any 429), then patches it: newest-first heads that stop on 20 known items, playlist `last_modified|track_count` checks, `/me` count checks and a weekly full walk; your own likes, follows and reposts write the database first and revert if SoundCloud refuses. Devices copy it to each other in bucketed chunks over Link (iPhone, iPad and Mac to the Apple TV) and iCloud (zone `Library`, record `LibraryChunk`); a device that receives a fresh copy skips its own download. Another account's data is never merged; switching accounts or signing out wipes it. Status is one quiet line ("Syncing your library… 3,200 of 5,400 likes", then "Library up to date") in Settings › Library and at the bottom of Likes and Tracks.
- **Recently Played is one list on every device** (owner, 2026-11-09: "i don't think we send playing events to soundcloud so recently played doesn't work across device?"). Logic in `Core/Playback/PlayHistory.swift`, `Core/Playback/PlayHistoryRecorder.swift` and `Shared/ViewModels/RecentlyPlayed.swift`.
  - **A play** is 30 s of audible playback, or 90% of a track shorter than that. Skip-throughs, SoundCloud previews, Auto Mix's incoming track before it takes over, paused or loading time, and playing the same track again within half its length don't count. Visual fixtures never count.
  - **SoundCloud gets one event per SoundCloud play:** `POST api-v2 /me/play-history` with `{"track_urn": "soundcloud:tracks:<id>"}` on the user's own session, the call SoundCloud's own clients make, so plays here show in SoundCloud's history and on every device. Own files never go to SoundCloud. It is background work (`social` lane, allowed while the app plays in the background) through the gateway, so it obeys the request ceilings and stops at the first 429. Unsent plays wait on the device (at most 50, three days, three tries; account-bound, wiped on sign-out), are sent oldest first 2 s apart, and retry after a stop, offline or a 5xx, and every 10 minutes while open. A 403, 404 or other 4xx turns reporting off until the next launch. A play-history write clears only cached play history, not other cached responses. Play counts are SoundCloud's own pipeline and are not touched.
  - **One merged list:** the account-scoped synced setting `recentPlays` (the 40 newest plays from all your devices, own files included, synced as a `SettingsEntry`, no new CloudKit record type), this device's own plays (re-added if another device's write won), and SoundCloud's play history (25 newest, fetched with Home at most every 5 minutes). Newest play of each track wins. Tracks resolve from what the device already has (the play itself, SoundCloud's history, the library database, your own music by id, or by title, artist and length on another device), then one `tracks?ids=` request of up to 50 for the rest, once per launch; own files a device can't reach are left out there.
  - **Where it shows:** a **Recently Played** shelf, second on Home on iPhone, iPad, Mac, Apple TV and Apple Watch; the Apple TV Top Shelf and Now Playing recap (SoundCloud tracks only, since they open by id); the Recently Played widget and Siri's history. All of them update when a play counts or another device's play syncs in, not at the next launch.
  - **History** (owner, 2026-11-09: "expose … history from soundcloud.com/you/history"; logic in `Shared/ViewModels/ListeningHistory.swift`, shared states and collection cards in `Shared/UI/Components/ListeningHistoryViews.swift`) is the whole list behind the shelf, Apple Music style, on iPhone, iPad, Mac and Apple TV. Source: `api-v2 /me/play-history/tracks`, 50 a page, the next page only when you scroll near the end, plus `me/play-history/contexts` for a **Recently Played Collections** shelf (playlists, albums, stations and profiles you played from). If the tracks call answers 401, 403 or 404 it falls back to the official `api.soundcloud.com /me/recently-played/tracks` (25 tracks, no times, no paging, hydrated through `tracks?ids=`) under one **Recently Played** section. Local plays from Recently Played (your own files, and SoundCloud plays not yet reported) are merged in; a SoundCloud play SoundCloud already shows within a minute of ours is dropped, previews never appear. Tracks are grouped by day (Today, Yesterday, weekday names for the last week, then dates), each row the shared `SCTrackListRow` with the time played as its trailing text. Play All, Shuffle and a search field that filters what has loaded (on Apple TV a **Search** button beside them opens the field and keyboard full screen; no Library page shows an always-on keyboard, and Following and Followers work the same way). **No Clear History**: the official API has no delete, so we don't offer one. Loading is a centred `ProgressView`, empty is "No Listening History". The first page and the collections are cached per account so the page opens instantly; a rate limit stops paging until it lifts. Every request is tagged **history** in Network Activity, with which endpoint answered. Entry points: Home's Recently Played header (›), a History row in the iPhone Library list after Decks, a History shelf in the Apple TV Library after Recently Added, a History item in the iPad sidebar's Library section (its own stack; on iPad Home's Recently Played header selects it), and on the Mac View › History (⌘Y) and a sidebar item in Library. Never under Search or Social.
- **Continue from another device** (owner, 2026-11-09: "maybe even some sort of 'continue from' across devices"). Logic in `Core/Playback/ContinueSession.swift` and `Shared/ViewModels/ContinueFrom.swift`; views in `Shared/UI/Components/ContinueFromViews.swift` and the Apple TV Home.
  - **Each device says where it is** once it has actually played: track, position, up to 100 queued track ids around it, source, Auto Mix on or off, the device's name and `DeviceClass`, the time, and when it last started playing (for the other-devices order, §2). It publishes on play, pause and track change, and at most every 30 s while playing; a restored paused session publishes nothing. The record goes into the account-scoped synced setting `continueSessions` (newest per device, 8 devices, a week; a `SettingsEntry`, no new CloudKit record type) and, while linked, straight to the Apple TV or phone over Link (`continue_session`, signed like the local music offer).
  - **The offer** appears when another device on the same account played within 12 hours, more recently than this device, and this device isn't playing. It never plays or interrupts anything on its own. It surfaces in two places, never as a list row (owner, 2026-11-09: "far too non-descript, buried on the page"):
    - **The mini player is the primary surface.** With nothing playing, the iPhone and iPad bottom accessory (kept visible on iPhone while an offer exists) and the Mac toolbar capsule show the track's artwork, its title and "Playing on iPhone · 0:14" with the device's symbol, and a prominent round play button that loads and plays it here. Tapping the bar loads that queue at that spot, paused, and opens Now Playing; the context menu has **Not Now**.
    - **A Home hero** sits at the very top of Home on iPhone, iPad and Mac, like an iOS 27 Apple Music hero (owner, 2026-11-09: "still SO ugly" about the old boxed band and blue Play). Its backdrop is the shared drifting artwork (the album-page palette with its art-computed scrim) and runs **full bleed from the very top of the window** (owner, 2026-11-09: "that image should go off to the top of the page… it shouldn't just cap underneath the word home"): one layer behind the scroll view (`collectionScrollBackdrop(url:identity:fade:isPresent:)`, the same host the album pages use), pinned with `ignoresSafeArea` under the status bar, the navigation bar and the large "Home" title, which sit on it like the App Store and Music headers; it follows the scroll and the hero's height, stretches on overscroll and is extended under the iPad sidebar with `.backgroundExtensionEffect()` on that bare layer only. The Mac draws it under the toolbar and its own "Home" title row (`collectionScrollBackdropLead()`). The hero content stays in the safe area. Its foot fades softly into the page over 104 pt on iPhone and iPad (96 pt on the Mac) on an eased curve, and the first shelf starts where the fade ends: no hard top or bottom edge. With no Continue offer (signed out, nothing to continue, or Not Now) there is no backdrop and Home is a plain page. In front, in dark appearance: the artwork with a shadow (200 pt), then "Continue from iPhone" with the device symbol (secondary, semibold), the title (title2 bold on iPhone, title bold on iPad and Mac, at most two lines), the artist (secondary), a slim position bar with both times, and **Play** (`.glassProminent` tinted with the artwork's `CollectionAccent`, white glass with a black label when the art is grey) and **Not Now** (plain `.glass`) in one `GlassEffectContainer`. iPhone stacks it centred; iPad and Mac put the artwork beside the text. While the hero is under the bar, the bar is dark (`.toolbarColorScheme(.dark, for: .navigationBar)`) with its scroll-edge effect off, so the large title and toolbar stay legible. Home is always one scroll view (loading, empty and error states sit inside it, centred), so the large title never overlaps the first shelf; the page snaps back to the top when the hero arrives or leaves while you are at the top. Tapping the hero outside the buttons loads paused and opens Now Playing. Apple TV shows one big hero tile first on Home (artwork, title, caption, position bar) that takes default focus, with Play and Not Now pills beside it; selecting the tile loads paused and opens Now Playing, and its context menu has **Not Now**.
    - Both fade in and rise a few points (fade only with Reduce Motion). Not Now hides that listening session on this device. A playing device's position is estimated forward by at most 35 s.
  - **Handoff** (`NSUserActivity` `red.forbes.sounding.playing`, registered in the iPhone, iPad and Mac Info.plists) carries the track id, position, source and the same record, advertised while a track is loaded; continuing loads it paused. Apple TV has no Handoff.
- **Settings scopes** (owner, 2026-11-09: "store settings in icloud, so they persist. lock some settings behind user so they wipe if signed out"). Every setting has one of four scopes (`SettingScope` in `SettingsStore.swift`):
  - **Synced** follows the Apple ID and survives SoundCloud sign-out: streaming and download quality, Auto Mix and DJ preferences (style, flow, blend, energy, flair, start options, set length), lyrics, sharing links, Show Columns, track-table columns and sort orders, generated insights, mixer curves. Plain `UserDefaults` keys outside `SettingsStore` sync when they match `SyncedPreferenceRules.appleID` (exact keys plus the prefixes `trackList.`, `trackTable.`, `mac.table.`, `mac.library.filters.`, `red.forbes.sounding.synced.`); give a new preference a key under one of those prefixes and it syncs with no other change.
  - **Account** is bound to the SoundCloud user and synced only in a record tagged with a hash of that user's id: recent searches, collection policies, track trims, saved mixes, DJ plan edits, recently played, where each device left off, playlist folders (merged per folder, not per key). Signing out wipes them from the device but leaves the iCloud copy; signing back in to the same account restores them; another account never sees them.
  - **Account, this device only** is wiped on sign-out and never synced: downloaded collections and the download-model bookkeeping, the played-track log, this device's own recent plays and the queue of plays waiting to reach SoundCloud, this device's own Continue record and the offers it was told Not Now, the Mac's follow history, Scout, activity and messages state, playback resume.
  - **Device-local** never leaves the device: cellular rules, storage caps, Live Activity, screensaver timeout, on-device analysis, heart-rate matching, output and Sonos choice, Link pairing, window and sidebar layout (including which sidebar sections are collapsed; the Library rows choice and pins are synced, see §2), onboarding flags, alarms, Home Lighting, debug flags, and the sync toggle itself. Anything not listed as synced is device-local by default.
  - Sync runs over the existing `CKSyncEngine` path (`SettingsEntry` records in the `Companion` zone, one per device, plus one per device per account), merged per key, last writer wins by timestamp, then edit count, then writer id. A local write always gets a timestamp later than the last one it has seen. **Sync Settings with iCloud** (on by default; iPhone and iPad in Settings › About & Advanced, the Mac in Settings › Account › iCloud, Apple TV in About & Advanced › General) stops both pushing and applying. Settings sync never carries credentials.
- **BPM and key: when they're found, and yours win** (owner, 2026-11-09). Logic in `Core/Analysis/TempoKeyStatus.swift`, views in `Shared/UI/Components/TrackTempoKeyViews.swift`.
  - **The gate** (`AnalysisGateInputs.gate`, in the scheduler's order): Music Analysis off; Apple TV and Watch never analyse in the background (they get results from other devices; the TV still analyses the tracks on its decks, see below); Low Power Mode; thermal serious or critical; battery under 30% and not charging; iPhone and iPad not on power; music playing.
